\title{From Sparse Selection to Physical Savings:\\
 A Runtime Contract for Persistent Native Memory}
\author{PRA Research Program}
\date{September 2026}

\begin{document}
\maketitle

\begin{abstract}
Logical context sparsity is only an opportunity bound.  A runtime may select a
small history while rebuilding K/V, copying selected tensors, changing source
positions, transferring bytes, constructing K/V-sized temporaries, or inducing
additional model calls.  We define a vector-valued realization contract for
persistent native memory that qualifies semantic same-subset correctness,
original-position consumption, resident-state reuse, physical movement,
lifecycle, and end-to-end workload economics independently.

The corrected implementations realize disjoint resident K/V through native
views or scheduler-owned pages in bounded HF, MLX, SGLang, llama.cpp,
vLLM-Metal, and vLLM-CUDA scopes.  Matched-consumer probes report zero retained-
history re-encoding and zero selected-K/V packing while separately exposing
host/device transfer, offload, and bounded attention scratch.  The correction
ledger shows why these fields cannot be collapsed into ``zero-copy'': earlier
paths reconstructed caches, used compact rather than original positions,
performed hidden casts or copies, and created K/V-sized temporaries.

With frozen RAG selections on Qwen3-4B MLX, cold native execution is 3.2\%
slower than selected text, but warm repeated selection reduces mean TTFT from
2.640 to 0.166 seconds and total latency from 3.031 to 0.638 seconds.  Ordinary
exact prefix-cache reuse remains a required baseline; PRA's distinct advantage
is changing or detached addressable evidence.  On one fresh locked coding task,
MLX and SGLang-MLX PRA-100 reproduce all six plain-engine actions and the exact
patch without re-encoding selected history or copying selected K/V.  Their
task-aware 90\% treatments also preserve the solve and six-call count, but
realize only 2.19\% cumulative physical-input saving on the short trajectory;
SGLang additionally exposes a 17.5~MB suffix-graft copy and 1.61~GB peak
consumer temporary.  A stricter frozen matched-tail policy instead loses the
solve identically on MLX and SGLang-MLX after omitting one early discovery
bundle, despite zero history re-encoding and selected-K/V copy.  A separate
frozen plan omits about 40\% of resident
history, but its receipt-bearing transfers remain hybrid transformed-context
evidence rather than strict resident-subset reuse.
An audit then found that every engine adapter had silently consumed request one
as full history while importing that source.  After correction, a strict
selection-only llama.cpp transfer omits 36.53\% with zero selected-history
re-encoding or K/V copy, but fails the task in twenty calls; its matched
PRA-100 and plain controls both solve, although their successful trajectories
are not byte-identical.  Compact-text policy success therefore does not by
itself qualify original-position sparse-K/V utility.

A later strict BF16 MLX gate isolates a second model/task pair.  On locked
Task~7, plain and PRA-100 both resolve in seven byte-identical actions with the
same patch.  Tail-90 resolves with that patch in five actions at 93.81\% mean
realized retention and zero selected-history re-encoding.  Its own-trajectory
physical-input saving is 6.19\%; the 37.20\% paired physical-workload reduction
mostly reflects two fewer model calls.  This is a one-task correctness and
accounting result, not an accuracy or latency estimate.

The resulting measurement contract establishes two boundaries: logical
selection does not imply physical saving, and physical omission does not imply
end-to-end workload saving.
\end{abstract}

\section{Introduction}

PRA changes what a model must keep logically available.  A long-lived resource
may remain addressable through a compact routing representation while only a
small selected part of its native K/V participates in one attention operation.
That distinction is algorithmically useful, but it leaves a systems question
unanswered.  A selected set can be small while the runtime still stores every
detail tensor in HBM, launches many tiny gathers, copies the same bytes
repeatedly, constructs temporary concatenations, or serializes requests around
Python control flow.  In that case sparsity exists in a trace but not in the
resource bill.

This paper asks how to turn PRA into a portable inference primitive before a
serving engine becomes semantically aware of PRA memory.  The immediate task is
not to design a new scheduler.  It is to define an execution boundary that can
be measured, optimized, and later replaced without changing the meaning of a
selected reference.

The runtime composes previously defined model, resource, and session
interfaces, but this paper evaluates only the native realization boundary.
Policy, transport, storage, and tool surfaces remain separate contracts; their
product details are documented in the appendix and companion papers rather
than treated as additional systems claims here.

The paper is organized around four independent gates:

\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*]
\item \textbf{Semantic gate.} The engine must consume the same selected state
as an identical-subset reference from the same resident source.
\item \textbf{Position gate.} Sparse records retain original source positions;
compact selected width is never substituted for the source position base.
\item \textbf{Physical mechanism gate.} Re-encoding, selected-K/V copy,
host--device transfer, consumer temporaries, ownership, and lifecycle are
reported independently.
\item \textbf{Workload gate.} TTFT, total latency, calls, completion work, and
task outcome determine whether physical omission becomes an economic saving.
\end{itemize}

The companion Paper~8.5 owns engine-independent agent-memory discovery,
including record exclusion, causal policy, oracle add-back, and logical task
efficiency.  Paper~4.5 imports a frozen, hash-bound \texttt{AgentMemoryPlan}; it
does not tune or promote the selector.  Full history and 100\% retention remain
correctness controls before any reduced plan is interpreted.

More precisely, Paper~8.5 decides which logical records or observation spans
remain visible.  Paper~4.5 consumes a frozen, hashable
\texttt{AgentMemoryPlan} and tests whether an engine realizes that identical
plan from resident K/V with original positions, no selected-history
re-encoding or physical copy, safe lifecycle, and separately reported
temporary and runtime costs.  Engine outcomes do not retune the selector.

The unified runtime, gateway, authenticated resource lifecycle, typed tool and
result surfaces, and engine-specific adapters are the implementation needed to
test those claims.  They are not additional evidence categories.  In
particular, this paper treats correction of an overstated result as evidence
about the measurement contract: cache identity, residence, source position,
physical copy, and realized retention must be observed independently.

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\small
\fbox{\parbox[c][.9cm][c]{.18\linewidth}{\centering frozen logical\\selection plan}}
$\longrightarrow$
\fbox{\parbox[c][.9cm][c]{.18\linewidth}{\centering native\\materializer}}
$\longrightarrow$
\fbox{\parbox[c][.9cm][c]{.18\linewidth}{\centering engine\\consumer}}
$\longrightarrow$
\fbox{\parbox[c][.9cm][c]{.20\linewidth}{\centering mechanism +\\workload receipt}}
\caption{Paper boundary.  Policy chooses stable records and spans; the runtime
must realize that frozen plan and expose the physical work actually performed.}
\label{fig:runtime-boundary}
\end{figure}

\section{Accounting Model}

For $L$ decoder layers, $T$ source tokens, $H_{kv}$ physical K/V heads, head
width $D$, and $b$ bytes per scalar, complete detail storage is
\[
B_{\mathrm{detail}}=2|L_{KV}|H_{kv}TDb.
\]
If request-time selection admits $S$ tokens to $|L_C|$ consuming layers, the
active payload opportunity is
\[
B_{\mathrm{active}}=2|L_C|H_{kv}SDb.
\]
The ratio $B_{\mathrm{active}}/B_{\mathrm{detail}}$ is not peak HBM or runtime.
Weights, local K/V, allocator state, routing indices, transfer staging, score
buffers, and temporary packing remain outside it.

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\small
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{p{.28\linewidth}Y}
\toprule
Quantity & Interpretation \\
\midrule
Full/selected logical tokens & Policy opportunity before engine realization. \\
Visible prompt tokens & Text actually submitted through the ordinary prompt path. \\
Resident/selected source K/V & Canonical stored extent and the intervals read by this request. \\
Original-history re-encoding & Old selected tokens evaluated through the model again. \\
Synthetic-history encoding & Post-cache summaries, tombstones, or receipts evaluated as new tokens. \\
Partial-old-history encoding & Previously seen but uncached tails evaluated again, for example an incomplete page. \\
Selected-K/V copy and H2D & Explicit packing/copy and residency transfer, reported separately. \\
Consumer temporary bytes & Peak request-local score, reduction, concatenation, and allocator state. \\
Workload & Requests, TTFT, total latency, completion tokens, peak memory, and task outcome. \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\caption{Independent accounting quantities.  No single ``reuse'' or
``zero-copy'' label substitutes for these fields.}
\label{tab:editorial-accounting}
\end{table}

Evidence is reported in four classes.  A \emph{semantic gate} verifies that the
selected state produces the intended same-subset result.  A \emph{mechanism
gate} observes positions, historical evaluation, copying, and ownership.  A
\emph{physical-efficiency gate} measures resident bytes, transfer, temporaries,
and peak memory.  A \emph{workload gate} measures requests, TTFT, total latency,
completion work, and task outcome.  An engine can pass the first two and still
fail the latter two; the vector must not be collapsed into ``PRA supported.''

The accounting receipt is request-scoped and stage-labelled.  Bytes copied to
bootstrap a canonical source are different from bytes copied for every
selection.  Similarly, one-time ingest encoding is different from re-encoding
already resident history.  Peak allocation is reported separately from
cumulative traffic, because a small reusable scratch buffer and a newly packed
K/V tensor can have similar peaks but radically different repeated-request
costs.

\section{Native Realization Contract}

The core contract is \EngineContract{}.  A request identifies the exact source
cache, runtime/model/tokenizer revision, stable selected records and spans,
original logical positions, session and tenant scope, and a plan digest.  A
same-subset comparator consumes identical K/V values at identical positions.
Ownership remains with the canonical source while requests borrow intervals;
finish, cancellation, error, eviction, offload/restore, termination, and stale
generation rejection are independently tested.

Qualification is vector-valued:
\[
G=(S,P,C,L,E),
\]
where $S$ is same-state semantic correctness, $P$ is original-position sparse
correctness, $C$ is copy/re-encoding behavior, $L$ is lifecycle correctness,
and $E$ is end-to-end workload qualification.  Passing one coordinate does not
promote another.

After one source bootstrap, strict resident-subset execution permits only
genuinely new history delta, the current suffix, and generated tokens.  It
requires
\[
R_{\mathrm{orig}}=R_{\mathrm{synthetic}}=R_{\mathrm{partial}}=0,
\qquad C_{\mathrm{selectedKV}}=0.
\]
Ingest-time compaction may create reusable K/V before source admission.
Replacing already resident records with new receipt text is instead a hybrid
transformed-context treatment and cannot qualify this invariant.

\subsection{Position and composition semantics}

The plan carries source/original positions, while an implementation may also
have chunk-local and request-packed coordinates.  These are not interchangeable.
For rotary models, a cached key associated with logical position $p$ contains
the corresponding rotation (schematically $K_{\mathrm{post}}(p)=R_p
K_{\mathrm{pre}}$).  Relabelling an already rotated key or compacting disjoint
records into positions $0,\ldots,S-1$ changes the attention state.  The
same-subset comparator therefore uses the identical selected K/V and original
positions; it does not compare sparse original-position execution with a newly
packed text prompt and call any difference an implementation bug.

Composition is separately declared.  Independently encoding chunks at local
positions can be a valid alternative representation, but it is a different
treatment from globally encoding the concatenated document.  The engine must
name which representation it consumes and qualify quality under that
representation rather than infer equivalence from token identity.

\subsection{Ownership and lifecycle}

The canonical source owns resident K/V; requests borrow immutable intervals or
pages.  A lifecycle gate covers concurrent session isolation, cancellation,
normal termination, stale-fork rejection, eviction, offload/restore, and final
cleanup.  Releasing one borrower must not free state still referenced by
another, while eviction must either wait for borrowers or move through a
generation-checked handoff.  These tests are mechanism evidence, not throughput
benchmarks, but they are required before an aliasing implementation can be
called reusable.

Paper~8.5 supplies the following frozen handoff:
\begin{verbatim}
AgentMemoryPlan
  selected stable record IDs and spans
  original logical positions
  materialization/compaction stage
  source and plan digests
\end{verbatim}
The engine realizes this plan; it does not infer task semantics or retune the
policy.

\section{Runtime Architecture}

The contract is implemented by a narrow set of components.  The application or
Paper~8.5 policy emits a logical plan; a resource registry resolves stable
identities to a canonical source generation; a materializer validates spans and
positions; an engine adapter creates non-owning views or scheduler aliases; a
lifecycle manager owns eviction and restoration; and telemetry records the
work performed.  Tool schemas, generic gateway UX, and policy tuning are not
part of this core path.

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\small
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{p{.20\linewidth}p{.33\linewidth}Y}
\toprule
Component & Authority & Required receipt \\
\midrule
Selection plan & stable record IDs, spans, positions, digest & selected logical extent and materialization stage \\
Resource registry & tenant/session/model/source generation & exact source-cache identity and residency state \\
Materializer & interval validation and consumer descriptor & rejected spans, old/synthetic/partial encoding counts \\
Engine adapter & attention consumer and native alias geometry & K/V copy, transfer, temporary, and position mode \\
Lifecycle manager & borrow, release, eviction, restore, cleanup & ownership generation and terminal-state outcome \\
Workload harness & prefix/full controls and task execution & TTFT, latency, calls, completion work, peak, and quality \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\caption{Minimal architecture supporting the native realization contract.}
\label{tab:runtime-architecture}
\end{table}

A plan digest binds policy output to runtime evidence.  If an adapter changes
the selected intervals, compacts positions, inserts receipt text, or falls back
to prompt replay, it must emit a distinct realization digest and accounting
class.  This makes silent degradation observable and permits the same frozen
logical treatment to be compared across engines.

Logical saving is therefore an engine-invariant input to this paper, not an
engine outcome.  A valid cross-engine row has the same agent, model and
tokenizer revisions, independently observed source-checkpoint revision, task
snapshot, history digest, plan digest, selected record identities, and logical
token counts.  Declaring the same model alias is insufficient.  Converted or
quantized runtime-artifact digests may differ and are recorded as physical
provenance; they do not replace the common source-checkpoint identity.  Engines
may differ only in physical page rounding and in measured residence, copies,
temporaries, and time.  If logical saving differs, the adapter did not consume
the same experiment and the row fails closed.  The historical autonomous rows below use
Qwen3-Coder-30B, Qwen2.5-Coder-14B, and Qwen2.5-Coder-7B; they qualify bounded
mechanisms or model/task admissions but are not a pooled engine-savings
comparison.  The new strict cohort pins one checkpoint and precision wherever
the engine can load them: Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct at revision
\texttt{c03e6d35}, BF16.  Quantized-only replications form a separate stratum.
The executable declaration is
\path{experiments/paper4_5_agent/configs/transverse_engine_qwen25_7b_bf16_v1.json}.
After engine qualification, the joint portability bridge reuses this exact
checkpoint, precision, tokenizer, frozen policy, and task snapshots with both
mini-swe-agent, OpenHands, and OpenCode.  Qwen3-Coder-30B cross-agent rows remain a
separate model-family replication and cannot establish the joint claim.

The first strict admission attempt is negative and therefore blocks selective
arms rather than weakening their gate.  Direct MLX independently observed the
pinned BF16 checkpoint and enforced the declared 8,192-token window before K/V
allocation or mutation.  With stock mini-swe-agent, Tasks~3 and 4 reached that
window after five and seven actions without a valid patch; Task~5 executed 29
actions but alternated two unsuccessful searches and never mutated the
workspace.  A task-independent scaffold diagnostic moved the existing
noninteractive-shell rule into the system prompt and reduced oversized visible
observations from 10,000 to 2,000 characters.  It enabled edits, but Task~4
still left syntactically invalid Python before reaching the window, while the
trivial OrderedSet Task~7 exhausted 30 actions and submitted an empty patch.
These are model/task admission failures, not PRA-100 or PRA-90 results.  The
corresponding selective rows remain prohibited until a repeatable plain success
is found.  A stock \texttt{mlx\_lm.generate} control excludes a hidden direct-
adapter divergence: for the 1,480-token Task~5 first prompt, stock MLX and the
endpoint emit byte-identical first actions (matching SHA-256
\texttt{66fa54fe83b3}).  The manifests and reduction are archived in
\path{docs/papers/shared/results/paper4_5_runtime_productization/coding_agents/engine_agent_gates/mlx_qwen25coder7b_bf16_admission_v2_20260921/}.

A subsequent common-agent/common-engine bridge froze three different Django
identities and the same Qwen2.5-Coder-7B BF16 checkpoint before execution.
Its direct-MLX plain diagnostics likewise produced zero workspace mutations in
18, 15, and 12 calls.  An economical 35-call Task~2 ledger identifies a
repeated malformed-edit/no-op-edit/grep/interactive-editor cycle, rather than
a cache or selector error.  Those executions are excluded from outcome tables
and block PRA-100 and reduced-policy arms under the declared admission rule.
They also exposed a lifecycle hazard: after a client was stopped, an orphaned
agent could reconnect to a restarted endpoint and contaminate the next task.
All five direct endpoints now accept a per-run lease and reject stale callers;
privacy-safe request-shape receipts and an economical action/observation ledger
make the failure auditable.  True in-flight generation cancellation remains an
open lifecycle gate.  The diagnostic artifact is
\path{docs/papers/shared/results/paper4_5_runtime_productization/coding_agents/engine_agent_gates/common_qwen25_7b_bf16_bridge/admission_diagnostic_20260922.json}.

The ordinary prefix cache remains available beside addressable resources.
Local request deltas should continue through the engine's normal incremental
path; persistent source records remain separately addressable.  PRA is useful
when the next request needs a disjoint or changing subset that cannot be
represented as one unchanged prefix.  It should not replace an exact-prefix hit
with a more expensive sparse path.

\section{Results}

\subsection{Warm repeated selection produces a real native-memory benefit}

The cleanest realization experiment freezes 50 Qwen3-4B MLX RAG selections at
2K context.  Selected text and native memory match every output and quality
metric.  Cold native execution costs 3.121 versus 3.024 seconds, a 3.2\%
regression from native encoding.  Once the identical selection is resident,
mean TTFT falls from 2.640 to 0.166 seconds and total latency from 3.031 to
0.638 seconds; throughput rises from 10.56 to 50.21 tokens/s.  At 4K, cold
native is 3.3\% slower while warm total latency is 0.799 versus 6.121 seconds.
Qwen3-8B repeats the pattern: 1.8\% cold regression, then an 82.1\% warm total-
latency reduction.  These are repeated-selection results on one M4 host, not
general serving speedups.

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\small
\begin{tabular}{lrrrr}
\toprule
Model / context & Cold selected & Cold native & Warm selected & Warm native \\
\midrule
Qwen3-4B / 2K total (s) & 3.024 & 3.121 & 3.031 & 0.638 \\
Qwen3-4B / 2K TTFT (s) & --- & --- & 2.640 & 0.166 \\
Qwen3-4B / 4K total (s) & --- & $+3.3\%$ & 6.121 & 0.799 \\
Qwen3-8B / 2K total & --- & $+1.8\%$ & baseline & $-82.1\%$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\caption{Repeated-selection realization summary.  Dashes indicate that the
main claim uses the reported relative effect rather than reconstructing an
unreported absolute cell.}
\label{tab:warm-native-summary}
\end{table}

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=.83\linewidth]{../shared/results/paper4_5_runtime_productization/rag/powered_qwen3_4b_mlx_4bit_n20_2k_50/plots/realization_ttft.pdf}
\caption{Qwen3-4B, 2K, 50-query mechanism cohort.  Cold native realization has
no material TTFT advantage; warm residence removes repeated selected-text
prefill.  Values are host- and cohort-specific.}
\label{fig:warm-native-ttft}
\end{figure}

\subsection{Exact prefix caching is a necessary baseline}

Warm native memory must not be compared only with fresh text prefill.  The
required ladder is fresh selected text, exact text-prefix hit, persistent native
selection, and changing-selection partial native reuse.  An autonomous
llama.cpp control illustrates the strength of the baseline: at 100\% retention,
ordinary live-prefix continuation matches cold-prefill behavior while reducing
single-task wall time by 27.85$\times$.  This is a prefix-cache result, not a PRA
speedup.  PRA's distinct systems opportunity begins when selected evidence is
detached, disjoint, or changes across requests.

\subsection{Complete retention must remain on the native dense path}

The corrected HF dispatcher no longer sends PRA-100 through the disjoint
online-softmax consumer.  It shallow-forks only request-local cache descriptors
while every key/value tensor continues to alias the pinned canonical source;
request growth replaces private descriptors without mutating that source.
Transformers therefore executes its ordinary dense attention and last-logit
projection geometry.  A real Qwen2.5-0.5B FP16 control with 2,713 resident
source tokens is token exact and has maximum logit delta 0.0 against two
independent ordinary-prefix executions and after lossless restore.  It reports
zero selected-history re-encoding, selected-K/V copy, interval packing, or
consumer K/V temporary.  This is a bounded correctness result, not an
autonomous workload or latency claim.  Earlier PRA-100 probes that forced a
complete contiguous prefix through a sparse consumer remain useful numerical
diagnostics but are not admissible full-retention implementations.  The
machine-readable receipt is
\path{shared/results/paper4_5_runtime_productization/coding_agents/engine_gates/hf_qwen25_05b_full_native_dense_fix_20260919.json}.

\subsection{Changing selection changes composition semantics}

Persistent-corpus MLX reuses independently encoded 256-token chunks across 50
changing questions.  It reuses 22.0\% of chunk lookups and reduces cumulative
request time by 12.8\%, but official score changes from 0.440 to 0.380.  The
cause is representational: chunk-local post-RoPE positions differ from the
single global position sequence of packed text.  This is not cache corruption.
It establishes
\[
\boxed{\text{same selected evidence}\;\not\Rightarrow\;
\text{same hidden-state semantics}.}
\]
Original, chunk-local, and request-packed positions are therefore explicit
contract fields rather than implementation details.

\subsection{Fresh autonomous controls separate mechanism from policy}

An earlier SGLang diagnostic sent identical tokens through different numerical
consumer paths and diverged at request three.  That row motivated the matched-
consumer requirement but is superseded as engine qualification: complete
retention must remain on the ordinary dense path.  The resulting rule is
\[
\boxed{\text{same token IDs}\;\not\Rightarrow\;
\text{same numerical consumer path}.}
\]
Comparisons must therefore identify both selected state and its consumer.

Fresh autonomous MLX and SGLang-MLX controls then hold the model revision,
temperature-zero decoding, task, container, and grader fixed.  On both engines,
plain and PRA-100 resolve in the same six actions and submit the byte-identical
patch; all six assistant actions match exactly, with zero selected-history
re-encoding and zero selected-K/V copy.  The frozen Paper~8.5 matched whole-turn
token tail fails identically on both engines after nine calls: the assistant
action trajectory and erroneous 94,017-byte submission are byte-identical.
At request three it omits only the initial \texttt{grep} discovery bundle while
retaining the task plus the failed \texttt{nano} action and error observation;
the next edit diverges.  This cross-engine replication localizes the failure to
the policy/model pair rather than the K/V consumer.  By contrast, the
structural progress/mutation/verification-
floor policy resolves on both engines in six calls with the reference patch.
It first diverges only in final-response wording and realizes 8,725 physical
tokens from 8,920 logical tokens, a 2.19\% saving.

The same matched-tail rule is not globally invalid.  A later engine-independent
Paper~8.5 transfer with OpenHands and Qwen3-Coder-30B preserves 3/3 official
resolutions, reduces actions from 114 to 79, and saves 46.86\% against paired
\textsc{Full} input (commit \texttt{ad1152b9}).  The contrast establishes
that the policy can transfer, but it is not a controlled agent or engine
comparison: the historical OpenHands trace uses diagnostic whitespace token
accounting and does not bind the endpoint model digest.  Paper~4.5 therefore
imports neither its saving percentage nor an agent-specific plan.  It imports
only the transverse whole-record policy after strict same-tokenizer
qualification.  Paper~8.5's extra slicing of oversized text observations is not
a native-K/V saving unless record-internal original-position spans are consumed
without re-encoding; the paired 46.86\% also includes trajectory contraction
and is not an engine-local K/V or latency number.

The engines nevertheless have different physical realization costs.  On the
matched-tail negative, direct MLX reports 15.43~GB on its cumulative total-K/V-
copy counter and 197.2~MB of suffix grafts, despite zero selected-K/V copy;
SGLang-MLX reports 161.8~MB, all suffix graft.  Both expose large consumer
temporaries, peaking at 2.29 and 1.61~GB respectively.  On the less selective
successful task-aware treatment, SGLang's suffix graft is only 17.5~MB.  These
are one-task correctness and policy-
sensitivity results, not useful workload-economics claims.  The fresh SGLang
evidence is archived under
\path{shared/results/paper4_5_runtime_productization/coding_agents/engine_agent_gates/sglang_task01_qwen25coder14b_postfix_6e97f0c6/}.
The frozen matched-tail transfer and first-divergence receipt are archived under
\path{shared/results/paper4_5_runtime_productization/coding_agents/agent_policy_transfer/sglang_task01_matched_tail_ad1152b9/}.

The next strict gate uses Qwen3-4B BF16 revision
\texttt{1cfa9a7208912126459214e8b04321603b3df60c}, one fused direct-MLX
endpoint, the append-stable no-thinking template, and locked baseline-success
Task~7 (\texttt{django\_\_django-14089}).  Plain and PRA-100 each resolve in
seven actions with byte-identical trajectories and the same 405-byte patch.
Tail-90 first diverges at action three, skips two verification actions, and
still submits the identical passing patch in five calls.  Mean realized
retention is 93.81\% (86.95--100\% by request); selected history is never
re-encoded, selected intervals are not packed by the production path, and no
host--device transfer occurs.  Physical input is 7,595 of 8,096 logical tokens,
or 6.19\% own-trajectory saving.  Relative to PRA-100's 12,093 tokens, the
37.20\% reduction includes a 33.05\% logical workload contraction from two
fewer calls, so it cannot be attributed to K/V omission alone.

This task also caught a consumer bug before the quality claim was admitted.
The eager segmented path differed from its identical-subset dense reference by
0.5 maximum logit value on the first sparse request; the fused disjoint path is
exact on all four eligible sparse live-cache turns.  Qualification is not free:
the per-request dense same-subset reference and canonical suffix growth produce
3.546~GB PRA-100 and 3.423~GB tail-90 cumulative total-K/V-copy counts, while
whole-request peak temporary allocation is 371.9 and 626.5~MB.  These copies
belong to the validation/reference and canonical-growth scopes, not selected-
interval packing, and are excluded from any production speed claim.  The full
receipts are archived under
\path{shared/results/paper4_5_runtime_productization/coding_agents/engine_agent_gates/mlx_qwen3_4b_bf16_task07_fused_20260921/}.

\subsection{Frozen agent-plan transfer separates omission from economics}

Paper~8.5 supplies one Prompt Pinned plan on Scikit-learn-14496.  The current
transfers are hybrid because they create model-visible closure receipts after
the source cache exists; they qualify original-K/V selection but not the strict
resident-subset invariant.

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\small
\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{%
\begin{tabular}{lccrrcccl}
\toprule
Engine & PRA-100 & Reduced & Calls F/R & Logical/KV omission & Hist. re-enc. & K/V copy & Paired workload & Status \\
\midrule
llama.cpp & pass & solve & 8/8 & 40.23/40.23\% & 0 orig.; receipts n/a & 0 selected & calls preserved & hybrid; timing open \\
MLX & pass & solve & 9/12 & 39.45/40.04\% & 1,056 synth. & 0 selected & $+15.23\%$ saving & hybrid \\
SGLang-MLX & pass & solve & 7/15 & 39.45/37.21\% & 1,344 synth. & 0 selected & $-33.10\%$ saving & hybrid \\
HF CUDA & mech. & open & --- & --- & --- & --- & open & autonomous gate open \\
vLLM CUDA & mech. & open & --- & --- & --- & --- & open & autonomous no-copy gate open \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}}
\caption{Frozen-plan workload economics.  F/R denotes matched Full/reduced
calls.  Positive paired workload is saving; negative is regression.  Receipt
rows are hybrid transformed context and are not pooled with strict resident-
subset execution.}
\label{tab:editorial-agent-transfer}
\end{table}

These rows establish the second systems boundary:
\[
\boxed{\text{physical omission}\;\not\Rightarrow\;
\text{end-to-end workload saving}.}
\]
Selection-only reruns with zero synthetic history are required before the plan
can enter the strict native class.

A first such rerun also corrected a shared bootstrap error.  The adapters had
captured an imported transcript by generating request one from \textsc{Full}
history, deferring selection to request two.  HF, MLX, SGLang, and llama.cpp
now populate the canonical source without a model-visible completion and apply
the declared subset immediately.  Pinned vLLM lacks a public prefill-only API;
it samples and discards one hidden capture token, reports that overhead, and
does not commit it to agent history.

On Qwen3-Coder-30B Q4 and locked
\texttt{django\_\_django-15741}, plain full-history execution resolves in ten
calls and native PRA-100 resolves in eight with zero selected-history
re-encoding and selected-K/V copy.  The first three commands match, but the
later successful trajectories differ, so exact PRA-100 trajectory parity is
still open for this identity.  The strict selection-only arm uses 372,180 of
586,360 cumulative logical tokens (36.53\% saving), re-encodes and copies zero
selected K/V, but fails official grading after twenty calls.  This valid
negative transfer blocks production-default promotion and shows that compact
ordinary-text policy qualification is not equivalent to original-position K/V
consumption.  The auditable bundle is
\path{shared/results/paper4_5_runtime_productization/agent_memory_plans/paper8_5_selection_only_e2_django15741_v1}.

The same corrected request-9 gate does not yet qualify the direct MLX 14B
path.  On MLX 0.32.2 / MLX-LM 0.31.3 with Qwen3-14B 4-bit, both fused and
eager disjoint consumers differ from the native dense identical-subset
reference by 0.5 logits, above the predeclared 0.01 limit; the eager first
token nevertheless matches (ID 3617).  The engine rejects the request before
generation, so this is a mechanism negative rather than an autonomous task
result.  A 154-token control further localizes the discrepancy: fused and eager
disjoint consumers each agree exactly with a packed copy under the same
consumer, yet differ from native dense MLX by 1.375 and 1.1611 logits.  Both
preserve four generated tokens, and the fused diagnostic preserves 16/16, but
the predeclared numerical gate remains failed; consumer equivalence cannot be
inferred from short token agreement.

\section{Prefix Cache and PRA Are Complementary}

Exact prefix caching is the correct default when the next request extends one
unchanged token prefix.  It preserves the engine's native consumer path and can
reuse all prefix K/V with minimal planning.  PRA addresses a different shape:
the request needs a changing, detached, or disjoint subset of already resident
records.  A runtime should choose between the two using observable request
geometry, not route every request through sparse selection.

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\small
\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{p{.22\linewidth}p{.22\linewidth}p{.22\linewidth}Y}
\toprule
Request shape & Preferred path & Required baseline & Main risk \\
\midrule
unchanged prefix plus suffix & exact prefix cache & live-prefix continuation & replacing a near-perfect hit with planning/copy overhead \\
same frozen detached evidence & persistent native selection & warm selected-text and prefix hit where representable & residence cost and consumer temporaries \\
changing disjoint records & partial native PRA & fresh selected text plus matched consumer & position/composition mismatch and churn \\
post-cache summary or receipt & hybrid transformed context & selected K/V plus charged synthetic encoding & describing new text as cache reuse \\
uncached or partial source tail & mixed/fallback path & explicit partial-history accounting & hiding old-token evaluation in a nominal hit \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\caption{Dispatch rule and comparator ladder by request geometry.}
\label{tab:prefix-pra-dispatch}
\end{table}

This division also clarifies the value proposition.  PRA does not need to beat
prefix caching on unchanged prefixes; it must outperform rebuilding or copying
the selected state when ordinary prefix identity no longer applies.  The
telemetry receipt therefore records exact-prefix reused tokens and addressable
resident intervals separately.  A combined runtime can keep the local agent
delta on the prefix path while borrowing older resource intervals through PRA,
without re-encoding either class.

\section{Qualification and Promotion Procedure}

Qualification proceeds from semantics to workload; later measurements cannot
repair an earlier failure.  First, a deterministic bounded geometry compares
native sparse execution against a same-subset reference with identical K/V and
positions.  Second, instrumented counters require zero original-history
evaluation and separately expose synthetic or partial-history work, selected
K/V copies, transfers, and consumer temporaries.  Third, lifecycle tests cover
concurrent borrowers and every terminal path.  Fourth, cold, warm, and
changing-selection cohorts measure physical and latency effects against both
fresh prefill and exact prefix caching.  Finally, an autonomous workload
consumes a frozen policy plan without engine-specific retuning.

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\small
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{p{.16\linewidth}p{.30\linewidth}Y}
\toprule
Gate & Required comparator & Promotion evidence \\
\midrule
Semantic & identical selected K/V and original positions & bounded output/logit agreement under declared dtype and consumer path \\
Mechanism & instrumented resident source & zero old-history evaluation; copy, transfer, temporary, and lifecycle fields reported \\
Physical & cold/warm/changing selection & resident and request-local bytes plus peak and cumulative traffic \\
Workload & prefix cache and fresh prefill & task outcome, requests, TTFT, latency, completion work, and paired cumulative cost \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\caption{Promotion ladder.  A row is not inferred from success in a later row.}
\label{tab:promotion-ladder}
\end{table}

This ordering prevents three common category errors.  Matching output alone
does not prove that historical K/V was reused.  Zero selected-K/V copy does not
prove low temporary memory or low transfer.  A fast request does not prove an
end-to-end saving if the model takes more requests to solve the task.

\section{Scope and Remaining Gates}

The corrected adapters establish bounded mechanisms, not universal engine
support.  HF remains qualified only for declared Qwen/CUDA batch-one geometry;
MLX and SGLang retain batch, topology, and head-dimension restrictions;
llama.cpp still lacks complete endpoint temporary-allocation visibility; and
vLLM CUDA remains limited to in-process V1, complete pages, and a homogeneous
K/V group.  Partial pages, hybrid groups, multiprocess scheduling, broader
models, and production concurrency require separate gates.

The warm RAG result is a repeated-selection mechanism cohort on one Apple host.
It demonstrates that correct residence can remove repeated prefill, but it does
not establish portable serving speedups.  The changing-selection experiment
also shows why globally packed text cannot be treated as the universal semantic
reference for independently encoded chunks.

The receipt-bearing frozen-plan transfer remains hybrid because closure
receipts are encoded after caching.  Its value is diagnostic: similar original-
K/V omission leads to preserved, reduced, or negative workload benefit across
consumers.  The strict MLX Task-7 gate now removes synthetic history and admits
one paired autonomous plain/PRA-100/tail-90 result.  It establishes exact
PRA-100 behavior and one preserved tail-90 solve, but not task-clustered
accuracy or production economics.  HF, vLLM CUDA, and fresh SGLang have bounded
mechanism evidence but no admissible post-contract autonomous reduced-plan row;
their workload coordinate $E$ therefore remains explicitly open.

\section{Conclusion}

Sparse logical selection is an opportunity bound, not a systems result.  Native
reuse requires source identity, original positions, same-subset correctness,
zero hidden historical evaluation, scoped K/V movement, bounded consumer state,
and safe ownership.  Correct persistent native memory can deliver large warm
repeated-selection gains, while exact prefix caching remains a formidable
baseline.  Changing selections introduce composition semantics that ordinary
prefix reuse does not solve.  Finally, an agent can react to physically correct
omission by taking more calls, erasing or reversing the saving.  The runtime
contract therefore ends at vector-valued evidence, not a single reuse flag.

\clearpage
\appendix
\ifdefined\TECHREPORT
\section{Superseded Measurements and Detailed Runtime Report}

The remainder preserves the full architecture, transport, product-surface,
historical measurement, qualification, and artifact narrative for auditability.
It is supporting detail rather than an expansion of the main claims.

\section{PRA Runtime Abstraction}

\subsection{Logical and physical memory are different quantities}

Consider $L$ decoder layers.  Each reference has $T$ tokens, $H_{kv}$ physical
key/value heads, head width $D$, and $b$ bytes per scalar.  Storing complete K/V
for the reference requires
\begin{equation}
B_{\mathrm{detail}} = 2 L H_{kv} T D b,
\end{equation}
where the factor two accounts for keys and values.  If routing selects $S$
unique tokens, the active payload is
\begin{equation}
B_{\mathrm{active}} = 2 L_{c} H_{kv} S D b,
\end{equation}
where $L_c$ is the number of memory-consuming layers.  The ratio
$B_{\mathrm{active}}/B_{\mathrm{detail}}$ is a logical opportunity.  It is not
peak HBM.  Peak HBM also includes model weights, local K/V, routing indices,
allocator slack, transfer staging, and temporary packed tensors.

Grouped-query and multi-query attention make the distinction especially
important.  Warm storage should preserve $H_{kv}$ physical heads.  Expanding
them to the larger number of query heads before materialization duplicates
memory without adding information.  The runtime therefore represents every
native tensor as
\begin{equation}
K,V \in \mathbb{R}^{B \times H_{kv} \times T \times D}
\end{equation}
and leaves head expansion to the model-family attention adapter.

These equations require four layer sets, not one overloaded set of ``PRA
layers.'' Let $L_A$ store compact addresses, $L_{KV}$ store detailed native
K/V, $L_R$ perform request-time routing, and $L_C$ consume selected memory.
The runtime enforces
\begin{equation}
L_C \subseteq L_{KV}, \qquad
L_R \subseteq L_A \quad \text{for layer-native routing}.
\end{equation}
The persistent detail term is therefore


\begin{equation}
B_{\mathrm{detail}} = 2 |L_{KV}| H_{kv} T D b,
\end{equation}
whereas one request pays active attention over $|L_C|S$ layer-token states.
Address layers may be broader than detail-K/V layers when compact search is
useful at several depths. Production defaults do the opposite kind of
conservatism: detail K/V is encoded only for the resolved consumer profile,
unless profile switching or calibration explicitly requests a union.

\subsection{Latency is a sum of visible stages}

For one request, PRA overhead is not a single routing timer.  We decompose it as
\begin{equation}
t_{\mathrm{PRA}} = t_{\mathrm{lookup}} + t_{\mathrm{graph}} +
t_{\mathrm{plan}} + t_{\mathrm{gather}} + t_{\mathrm{transfer}} +
t_{\mathrm{pack}} + t_{\mathrm{position}} + t_{\mathrm{metadata}}.
\end{equation}
Generation and attention follow this preparation.  Cold requests also pay
source fetch and native encoding; compiled paths pay graph capture and code
generation.  Reporting only warm attention can therefore reverse the practical
conclusion.

\section{Deployment and Integration Model}

\subsection{One facade, separate authorities}

Figure~\ref{fig:architecture} shows the runtime.  The facade does not make every
component interchangeable in meaning.  It gives them one lifecycle and one
inspection surface while retaining their ownership boundaries.

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\fbox{\begin{minipage}{0.91\linewidth}
\centering
Agent / existing harness / CLI\\[3pt]
$\downarrow$\\[3pt]
\textbf{PRA gateway}: logical IDs, capability negotiation, protocol trace\\[5pt]
G00 pass-through \quad G10 text fallback \quad G01 upgrade \quad G11 mediation\\[5pt]
$\downarrow$ JSON logical request; no raw K/V $\downarrow$\\[5pt]
\textbf{Inference runtime}: config, sessions, traces, cache accounting\\[5pt]
\begin{tabular}{c@{\qquad}c@{\qquad}c}
\textbf{Model path} & \textbf{Resource path} & \textbf{Tool path}\\
PRAForCausalLM & ExternalMemoryManager & ResourceDiscoveryEngine\\
route $\rightarrow$ select & cold $\rightarrow$ warm $\rightarrow$ hot & discover $\rightarrow$ disclose\\
native K/V consume & resolver + scoped caches & SafeToolExecutor
\end{tabular}\\[6pt]
lazy tool/skill palette $\rightarrow$ exact capability view\\[3pt]
compact result $\rightarrow$ scoped backing $\rightarrow$ selected replay / cursor\\[6pt]
$\downarrow$\\[3pt]
deduplicated plan $\rightarrow$ gather/transfer/pack $\rightarrow$ HF or thin engine handoff
\end{minipage}}
\caption{The distributed product boundary. Stable URIs join the paths, but the
agent owns task meaning, the gateway owns translation, and the engine owns
native K/V. Routing does not authorize source access or tool side effects.}
\label{fig:architecture}
\end{figure}

The public configuration has two layers.  \texttt{PRAConfig} specifies
semantic behavior and all four model-layer roles: \texttt{address\_layers},
\texttt{detail\_kv\_layers}, \texttt{routing\_layers}, and
\texttt{consumption\_layers}, plus chunking, closure mode, and materialization
budget. \texttt{PRARuntimeConfig} specifies physical behavior:
backend, compilation mode, K/V layout, page size, cache bounds, prefetch policy,
and timing synchronization.  Both serialize to a versioned JSON artifact.  A
runtime optimization is valid only when changing the second layer preserves
selected identities and output parity under fixed model semantics.

Layer selections use topology-normalized forms: all layers, last $n$, last
fraction, evenly spaced $n$, or an explicit list. A versioned registry resolves
request override, workload/model profile, family balanced fallback, then the
all-eligible correctness reference. The named objectives are
\texttt{reference\_correctness}, \texttt{quality\_max}, \texttt{balanced}, and
\texttt{economy}; the resolved trace records model ID and revision, workload,
materialization class, physical layer lists, profile source, and registry
version. These are internal optimization objectives. The product registry
labels smoke-only realizations of \texttt{quality\_max} as
\QualityMaxCandidate. A profile name is not portable evidence by
itself.

Address and detail construction have independent lifecycle states
(\texttt{NOT\_BUILT}, \texttt{PARTIAL}, \texttt{BUILT}, \texttt{SKIPPED}, or
\texttt{DEFERRED}). The detail policy is minimal, profile union, all layers, or
explicit. The address policy is routing-only, all candidate routing layers,
external-only, or explicit. If a new consumer requires absent detail K/V, the
runtime must re-encode missing layers, fail, or follow an explicitly configured
profile downgrade. It never silently substitutes a shallower profile.

\subsection{Engine instances and runtime model identity}

PRA distinguishes engine-instance identity from loaded-model identity. One
engine process may expose one or more loaded models. Model-specific PRA
state---including bundle, profile, qualification, session state, storage, and
native memory---is scoped by a stable runtime-model identity and model
fingerprint. Engines supporting only one base model use the same contract with
a single \texttt{default} loaded model. This keeps multi-model behavior in the
management container rather than turning the semantic runtime for one model
into a fleet manager.

Internally, the management provider maps each \texttt{runtime\_model\_id} to a
small runtime-state record containing the observed model descriptor, storage
manager, session source, capabilities, and effective configuration. The old
single-model constructor is normalized to one such record. Canonical source
records may be shared across models, but derived native identity includes the
model fingerprint together with resource identity, version, selected span or
representation, and layout. Model-native K/V can therefore neither cross a
model switch nor survive an unload as an apparently compatible prefix.

The Registry retains one engine instance with a \texttt{models[]} observation
and accepts either the legacy singular desired fields or a
\texttt{desired\_models[]} list. Drift is matched by runtime identity and
reported both per model and in aggregate. The gateway similarly routes to the
pair \((\textit{instance},\textit{runtime model})\), which distinguishes one
model replicated on several engines from several models resident behind one
upstream. Dynamic load and unload are optional capabilities; a single-model
runtime returns \texttt{NOT\_APPLICABLE}, \texttt{ACTION\_NOT\_SUPPORTED}, or
\texttt{RESTART\_REQUIRED} rather than pretending that process replacement is
an in-process model transition.

\subsection{A multi-axis execution policy}

PRA does not imply one routing frequency. We represent one request with four
orthogonal choices. The \emph{selection stage} is request, prefill/completion
phase, or token. The \emph{layer scope} is shared logical identities or an
independent selection per active layer. The \emph{materialization scope} is
request, phase, layer, or token. The \emph{residency policy} retains a payload
for that lifetime or releases it after one layer window. Shared identities never
mean shared tensors: chunk $C_7$ resolves to the native K/V independently
encoded at each consuming layer.

The global default is request/shared/request/keep. A model default can override
individual fields, and a request override takes final precedence. The resolved
policy records field-level provenance. Unsupported combinations raise before
generation; there is no implicit downgrade. The engine-neutral
\texttt{PRASelectionPlan} contains stable URI/chunk identities and row-local
spans, never tensors. A layer resolver maps that plan to native payloads, and a
separate materialization manager applies physical lifetime and residency.

The HF reference path preserves the old high-level request/shared behavior and
the old low-level token/per-layer behavior. It adds request/per-layer by
capturing all active layers in one memory-disabled prompt pass, routing each
once, and freezing each result. It adds token/shared through a request-owned
bridge: layers before the designated routing layer receive no current-token PRA
memory; that layer selects once, and later layers resolve the same identities
to their own K/V. Phase/shared execution uses a cache-free routing probe at the
first active PRA layer, seeds the shared logical plan, and then performs one
cache-producing prefill. Decode reselects once at the same boundary and reuses
the resulting plan. Requiring the routing layer to be the first active consumer
prevents an earlier layer from entering the cache with a different memory view.

\subsection{Stable resources and authenticated sessions}

A stable URI is the common identity for text memory, native K/V, tools, and
typed observations.  Paper 4's \texttt{PRASession} retains tenant, user,
session, cache scope, admitted resources, and warm/hot handles.  Credentials
remain opaque callbacks passed only to resolvers; they are absent from prompts,
snapshots, and artifacts.  Cache presence never grants authorization.

\subsection{Durable logical sessions and task scope}

An interactive agent needs continuity longer than one model process. The
runtime therefore separates logical \texttt{AgentSessionState} from physical
\texttt{PRASession}. Logical state is resolved by user and session identity and
contains an ordered typed-record stream, a task descriptor, and an idempotent
event log. Physical state owns admitted resources, native K/V, and result-store
handles. Closing it releases accelerator and resolver state without deleting
the conversation or task graph.

\texttt{SessionService} is the persistence boundary. Its in-memory
implementation provides thread-safe ephemeral operation. Its local
implementation hashes path identities, writes versioned JSON through atomic
replacement, and rejects stale optimistic versions. A database can implement
the same create, resolve, save, list, and delete contract without changing the
model path.

Task events create, link, activate, block, resume, complete, or cancel nodes in
a validated DAG. Typed records carry producing-task, consuming-task, relation,
and event-sequence provenance. Before ordinary PRA ranking,
\texttt{TaskScopeSelector} admits the active task, its structural closure, or a
bounded adaptive neighborhood. Scope controls which records may compete;
record and chunk routing still control which admitted detail is materialized.

\subsection{Agent and toolset product surface}

\texttt{Toolset} couples a callable to its generated schema, namespace, tenant,
tags, and declared side-effect class. Bundles compose through the existing
\texttt{CapabilitySDK}. The built-in local bundle provides file listing,
bounded reads and search, exact writes and replacements, Git status, and
workspace-rooted command execution. Path operations reject traversal outside
the configured workspace. Shell commands remain write-class actions:
workspace selection is not a security sandbox.

\texttt{PRAAgent} assembles model, capability discovery, lazy skill folders,
safe execution, compact results, durable sessions, and task scope. A turn
persists the user record, selects task-relevant records, discloses a bounded
tool palette, generates, validates at most a configured number of tool rounds,
stores compact output with exact-backing provenance, and persists the final
assistant record. The terminal adds resumable sessions and slash commands for
tasks, context, and tools. Write and destructive calls are denied by default
and can receive one host confirmation; disclosure alone never grants authority.

Paper 6.5's tool resources enter through the same runtime but a separate
decision path.  Discovery can identify a tool.  Disclosure can expose its
schema.  A model can propose a call.  Only \texttt{SafeToolExecutor}, given a
request-scoped \texttt{ExecutionAuthorization}, can validate identity,
arguments, write authority, and destructive authority.  Its result becomes a
typed observation resource with producer and call provenance.  This separation
prevents a high retrieval score from turning into ambient execution privilege.

\subsection{Lazy tools and skills}

Paper 6.5's latest capability contract is now part of the facade rather than an
adjacent experiment.  \texttt{AgentConfig} accepts ordinary Python callables,
explicit declarative \texttt{Skill} objects, and a parent directory containing
OpenAI- or Anthropic-style skill folders.  Callables are inspected into stable,
provider-neutral schemas.  Folder loaders parse declarative metadata and
instructions but never execute bundled scripts.

The default is a two-stage model-side lifecycle.  Discovery and Phase A encode
only compact selection views under count and token budgets.  Once the model or
host chooses a stable record ID, \texttt{activate\_capability} activates the
complete tool schema or skill instructions from immutable local backing.  It
does not rerun semantic discovery.  Encoded views may be cached, while active
bytes and resident cached bytes remain separate counters.  Eager selection or
full-view encoding is an explicit policy override.

\subsection{Compact typed result backing}

A successful tool output need not return as one flat prompt block.  Every open
\texttt{PRASession} now owns an \texttt{AdaptiveContextRuntime} with an exact,
hash-verified backing store scoped by tenant and session.  The runtime infers
tabular, log, graph, terminal, or generic structured shape for tool and API
results.  Type-specific compactors retain schema, counts, statistics, errors,
and bounded representative units while lexical, entity, rare-term, schema, and
optional summary addresses remain retrieval-only.

The public operations preserve stable record identity: compact inspection,
address search, full or selected materialization, and bounded cursor actions.
\texttt{execute\_tool\_and\_record} joins authorized execution to this record
lifecycle while retaining producer, call, and observation provenance.  Closing
an ephemeral session removes its backing bytes.

Paper 7's final result changes the product default. Native PRA is appropriate
for typed backing only while its ingestion and resident-state cost stays inside
an explicit budget. Above that budget, the record must remain recoverable
through cheaper addresses, search, cursors, and bounded lazy native encoding;
it must not be truncated and labeled as a complete native index.

Accordingly, \texttt{ContextPolicy} exposes
\texttt{max\_native\_index\_tokens} and
\texttt{max\_native\_index\_bytes}, plus per-\texttt{RecordType}
overrides. The byte gate can reject an oversized descriptor before tokenization;
the token gate more directly bounds model work when token accounting is
available. The reference defaults are 4,096 tokens and 65,536 bytes, but these
are deployment policy values rather than claimed universal optima.

Every record has one auditable native-index lifecycle state:
\begin{center}
\small\ttfamily
NOT\_REQUESTED\quad BUILT\quad SKIPPED\_SIZE\_LIMIT\quad DEFERRED
\end{center}
The ordinary Hugging Face loader automatically prepares in-budget records.
\texttt{BUILT} means complete backing passed through the production PRA encoder.
\texttt{SKIPPED\_SIZE\_LIMIT} preserves compact and cheap-index recovery.
\texttt{DEFERRED} is an explicit synchronous lifecycle decision, not a claim of
background work. After a cheap selector identifies a bounded row, field, line,
item, or chunk, \texttt{encode\_result\_region\_native} authorizes that exact
region and creates native K/V only for it. Teardown removes compact, full, and
lazy-region references. A shared multi-tenant model cache is not claimed safe
without engine-level partitioning.

\subsection{User workflow}

The supported workflow is deliberately small:

\begin{verbatim}
agent = AgentConfig(tools=(lookup,), skills_path="./skills")
context = ContextPolicy(
    max_native_index_tokens=4096,
    max_native_index_bytes=65536,
    record_policies={DB_RESULT: TypeContextPolicy(...)})
runtime = PRARuntime.from_pretrained(
    model_id, runtime_config=config,
    agent_config=agent, context_policy=context)
session = runtime.open_session(...)
palette = runtime.activate_capability_candidates(record_ids)
runtime.activate_capability(selected_id)
result = runtime.execute_tool_and_record(..., session=session)
audit = runtime.result_native_index_audit(
    session, result.record.record_id)
if audit.native_index_state == SKIPPED_SIZE_LIMIT:
    detail = runtime.encode_result_region_native(
        session, result.record.record_id, {"rows": [20, 30]})
trace = runtime.inspect()
\end{verbatim}

The same facade still accepts direct references, an
\texttt{ExternalMemoryManager}, a custom discovery engine, and a safe executor.
The executed notebook demonstrates model memory, physical materialization,
lazy capabilities, compact results, cursors, size-gated native result routing,
and selected-region native promotion
with an offline Hugging Face model and pure in-memory tools.

\section{Physical Materialization}

\subsection{Planning before copying}

Routing produces logical half-open intervals
$I_i=(u_i,\ell_i,a_i,b_i)$, naming URI $u_i$, layer $\ell_i$, and token range
$[a_i,b_i)$.  The planner groups intervals by URI and layer, sorts them, merges
overlaps, and then applies a global token budget.  If two requested intervals
overlap, their common tokens are copied once.  The trace reports requested,
unique, and dropped token counts, making deduplication and budget truncation
auditable.

The portable materializer slices source tensors, transfers selected slices when
needed, and packs them by consumption layer.  It reports output bytes, transfer
bytes, and temporary bytes.  It does not infer peak HBM from these values; CUDA
profiling records peak allocator state separately.

\subsection{Native-reference semantic gate}

\paragraph{Historical pre-gate diagnostic.}
The quantitative native-execution results in this subsection predate
\EngineContract{}. They are retained to document
the development path, but do not support current Native Memory exactness,
quality, copy-avoidance, or product-qualification claims. The admissible HF
replacement is the bounded same-consumer agent gate reported later in this
paper; workload economics remain unmeasured.

Reference K/V is captured at source-relative positions. The direct query must
therefore begin strictly after every source key that it may attend to. For
independent records $r$ with source lengths $L_r$, the runtime uses
\begin{equation}
p_{q,j}=\max_r L_r+j,
\end{equation}
not $j$ and not $\sum_r L_r+j$. The maximum avoids query/source position
collisions while preserving each record's independent coordinate system.
Earlier code started $p_{q,0}=0$, which invalidated full-reference RoPE parity.
This was a runtime bug, not a routing or training deficiency.

The corrected path enters selected native K/V and local K/V into the same
family-native softmax. It retains grouped-query K/V heads physically, preserves
the model's scaling and causal mask, and keeps the immutable materialization
plan active through prefill and every decode call. Compact telemetry records
query width, local-cache width, and active native width without retaining
attention tensors.

The storage fingerprint now distinguishes fixed \texttt{post\_rope} keys from
optional \texttt{pre\_rope} keys. A pre-RoPE entry carries a contract over the
model revision, every layer's exact frequency tensor and scaling policy, RoPE
dimensions, and rotation layout. At request time an adapter may apply the
selected packed positions before exposing the entry to attention. Contract
mismatch invalidates the derived cache. Post-RoPE remains the default because
it avoids request-time rotation when positions are fixed; pre-RoPE is an
experimental composition facility, not a claim that rotation alone restores
the causal history missing from independently encoded records.

Papers~3.2 and~3.3 own the detailed document-selection, positional-rebinding,
and answer-quality studies.  Paper~4.5 imports only their runtime decision:
independently encoded pre-RoPE records remain
\texttt{CALIBRATION\_PENDING}.  An engine adapter must qualify its RoPE contract
and numerical path, and that qualification does not recreate cross-record
causal contextualization.

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\caption{HF native-reference correctness ladder. E0 is an offline FP32 mechanism
test; E2--E3 reuse the 15-case frozen Qwen3-0.6B Paper 8 ablation.}
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{l Y r r l}
\toprule
Gate & Condition & Accuracy & Native tokens & Result\\
\midrule
E0 & One complete reference; visible/native logits & --- & 8 & $5.97\times10^{-8}$ max error\\
E1 & Multiple complete records; boundary/dedup audit & --- & 144 & pass\\
E2 & Selected complete task records; all 28 layers & 60.0\% & 80.2 & functional\\
E3 & Paper-3 radius-zero evidence; all 28 layers & 0.0\% & 35.7 & insufficient\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{table}

The bug fix closes the semantic blocker but does not make every sparse geometry
adequate. Visible full task scope reaches 73.3\%, corrected selected full-record
native consumption reaches 60.0\%, and sparse late-layer or radius-zero paths
remain at 0\% on this frozen checkpoint. The first expected token is often
ranked correctly even when later generation fails. The remaining boundary is
autoregressive composition across materialization depth and layer coverage;
training is not proposed as a remedy for a broken native path.

\subsection{Cross-model semantic reference gate}

We next apply one architecture-neutral protocol to three frozen pretrained
models. The exact checkpoints are Qwen/Qwen3-0.6B revision
\texttt{c1899de2}, \texttt{unsloth/Llama-3.2-1B} revision \texttt{9535bd9b},
and \texttt{google/gemma-3-1b-it} revision \texttt{dcc83ea8}. The official
Meta Llama revision \texttt{4e20de36} remained license-gated to the available
account; the experiment therefore uses a public weight mirror and records that
substitution rather than implying that the official repository was tested.
All runs use eager FP16 CUDA on the GTX 950M, one model per process.

Before the semantic cases, each model must preserve logits, hidden states,
greedy generation, and the complete dynamic cache exactly with PRA disabled.
Reference registration must retain native K/V-head count and head width without
query-head expansion, preserve source-relative positions, and remain active for
two explicit decode calls. The four measured conditions are a visible prefix,
the same complete reference as native K/V, an explicitly selected complete
record, and a family-declared sparse layer profile. Three one-token
continuations test a capital, color, and numeric code. This small diagnostic
localizes integration failures; it is not an answer-quality benchmark.

one-token exact accuracy over three cases. Full-native errors are against the
same visible prefix. KV-head fidelity and request-lifetime checks pass for all
rows.}
\label{tab:cross-model}
\small
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{p{.17\linewidth} l Y r Y Y}
\toprule
Model & Disabled & Full native & Selected full & Sparse profile & KV heads\\
\midrule
Qwen3-0.6B & exact & \QwenFullMaxError{} max & \QwenSelectedAccuracy{}\% &
8/28 layers; \QwenSparseAccuracy{}\% & 8/16 preserved\\
Llama-3.2-1B mirror & exact & \LlamaFullMaxError{} max & \LlamaSelectedAccuracy{}\% &
8/16 layers; \LlamaSparseAccuracy{}\% & 8/32 preserved\\
Gemma-3-1B & exact & partial; \GemmaFullMaxError{} max & \GemmaSelectedAccuracy{}\% &
2/4 global; \GemmaSparseAccuracy{}\% & 1/4 preserved\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\normalsize
\end{table}

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=.96\linewidth]{hf_cross_model_semantic_gate.pdf}
\caption{The full and selected-full conditions preserve expected-token rank for
Qwen and Llama. Reducing consumer-layer coverage changes semantics despite
selecting the same complete record. Gemma's global-only path retains the top
token in these cases but has large logit deviation because unchanged local
sliding layers do not consume external K/V.}
\label{fig:cross-model}
\end{figure}

For Qwen and Llama, full-native replay preserves the visible-prefix top token
in all three cases. Maximum FP16 logit error is \QwenFullMaxError{} and
\LlamaFullMaxError{}, respectively. The sparse condition exposes the three
independent materialization axes: record selection remains fixed, token width
remains the complete selected record, but consumer layers fall from 28 to 8
for Qwen and from 16 to 8 for Llama. Mean expected-token rank consequently
moves from 1.33 to \QwenSparseRank{} and \LlamaSparseRank{}, and Llama accuracy
falls from \LlamaSelectedAccuracy{}\% to \LlamaSparseAccuracy{}\%. Sparse
routing therefore does not establish a semantically adequate consumption
profile.

Gemma is a useful failed abstraction boundary. Its 26-layer decoder contains
four global-attention layers; the adapter intentionally leaves 22 local
sliding-window layers native. Disabled parity, single-head MQA storage,
positions, and decode lifetime pass, but global-only native replay differs from
the visible prefix by \GemmaFullMaxError{} maximum and \GemmaFullMeanError{}
mean absolute logit error. The one-token top answers remain unchanged, but that
coarse agreement is not full-prefix equivalence. We therefore report
\emph{partial topology}, not cross-family semantic parity.

The runtime now also carries an attention-only MoE contract inherited from the
HF mechanism study.  Injection resolves ordinary and nested text decoders,
snapshots every sparse MLP, router, and expert-container identity, replaces
only eligible attention modules, and rejects the result if any host-owned MoE
object changed.  Tiny structurally faithful tests cover Qwen3-MoE, nested
Mistral~3, and GPT-OSS; GPT-OSS sliding layers remain untouched and its learned
attention sinks are forwarded to the native kernel.  Qwen's hybrid GDN/QSA
family is described by a fail-closed structural plan: QSA layers are eligible,
whereas recurrent GDN state and the sparse indexer remain host-owned.  These
are implementation contracts, not model-scale qualification results.

The pre-publication queue therefore keeps five gates separate: contract test,
exact model load, optional adaptor training, matched No-PRA/PRA-without-adaptor/
PRA-with-adaptor measurement, and immutable bundle publication.  Its order is
Qwen3-30B-A3B, Gemma~3 12B and 27B, Mistral Small~3.2 24B, GPT-OSS 20B,
QwQ 32B, and Llama~3.3 70B.  Qwen3-30B-A3B additionally has an exact loaded
MLX 4-bit identity and five-seed native-no-adaptor measurements on QASPER,
HotpotQA, and 2Wiki.  That receipt does not close the three-way gate: its
No-PRA and trained-adaptor arms, qualification, and publication remain
pending.  The other targets currently establish only their structural
contracts.  Reduced consumer-layer profiles remain
\texttt{CALIBRATION\_PENDING}; \texttt{BALANCED} retains all eligible consumer
layers.

The executable qualification path fixes the candidate chunks once, compares
generic cosine with a five-seed evidence-supervised router, and replays each
selection twice: as visible selected text and as all-consumer native K/V.  Its
five named conditions are full No-PRA, generic selected text, generic native
K/V, learned-router selected text, and learned-router native K/V.  Thus a
router gain cannot be mistaken for a transport gain, and native parity is
measured against the exact selected-text twin rather than an independently
retrieved context.

\subsection{Corrected layer-profile calibration}

\paragraph{Historical pre-gate diagnostic.}
The model-consumer measurements below were described as corrected relative to
an earlier position-base bug, but they still precede the current live-history,
copy-accounting, same-consumer, and lifecycle contract. Their numerical values
remain useful for diagnosis only; structural profile declarations and
selection/router-only results remain valid.

The earlier sparse column was a diagnostic, not a calibrated deployment
profile. We therefore inject all eligible layers once and replay one fixed,
complete reference identity through a common profile sweep. Qwen uses all 28
decoder layers; Llama uses all 16; Gemma normalizes over its four eligible
global-attention layers. Candidate profiles are all layers; contiguous late
suffixes of 1, 4, 8, 12, 14, 16, 20, and 24 where defined; early and middle
blocks; and evenly spaced blocks. Reference width, query offset, native
post-position K/V, GQA/MQA head count, and shared-softmax attention remain
fixed. The 102 rows comprise three frozen one-token cases per model. They
calibrate SDK behavior but do not replace Paper 3's workload-scale placement
study.

log probability relative to the visible-prefix control. Rank is averaged over
three cases. ``All'' means all eligible layers, not necessarily every physical
decoder layer for Gemma.}
\label{tab:layer-calibration}
\small
\begin{tabular}{llrrrr}
\toprule
Model & Profile & Consumers & Active layer-tokens & $\Delta$LP & Rank\\
\midrule
Qwen3-0.6B & all & 28 & 196.0 & $+.001$ & 1.33\\

 & last 8 & 8 & 56.0 & $-.650$ & 2.33\\
 & last 1 & 1 & 7.0 & $-1.705$ & 20.33\\
\midrule
Llama-3.2-1B & all & 16 & 106.7 & $-.002$ & 1.33\\
 & last 8 & 8 & 53.3 & $-1.986$ & 5.33\\

\midrule
Gemma-3-1B & all four global & 4 & 28.0 & $-.038$ & 1.00\\
