\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=.86\linewidth]{layer_profiles/layer_profile_quality_cost.pdf}
\caption{Target-token quality against active layer-token cost with reference
identity and token width fixed. The sharp family-dependent trajectories rule
out a universal ``last eight'' product default.}
\label{fig:layer-profile-calibration}
\end{figure}

All-layer Qwen and Llama replay are the only tested profiles with maximum
full-logit error below .1. Qwen last-24 has the best mean target log probability
on these three cases, but its maximum full-logit deviation is 7.14; it is
therefore registered under the internal \texttt{quality\_max} objective but
published as \QualityMaxCandidate, not as a production-grade
\QualityMax{} profile or the balanced product default. Llama requires
all 16 layers for the same fidelity
criterion. Gemma requires all four eligible global layers to preserve the
tested target tokens, yet still cannot reproduce the hidden computation of its
22 unchanged local layers. Thus \texttt{balanced} and
\texttt{reference\_correctness} resolve to all eligible layers for all three
families in this registry version. The economy profile is an explicit last-one
stress point and carries no quality guarantee.

The storage consequence is intentionally separate. Routing-only address state
is retained at one late layer, while detail K/V is retained at the resolved
consumer layers. On these short references, Qwen detail storage falls from
802,816 bytes for all 28 layers to 688,128 for last 24 and 229,376 for last 8;
the routing index remains 2,048 bytes. A service that needs rapid profile
switching may ingest the union of candidate detail layers. The production
default instead stores only the chosen profile and re-encodes explicitly if a
later request asks for missing detail.

\subsection{Larger-model profile scaling on Apple Silicon}

\paragraph{Historical pre-gate diagnostic.}
All Native-Memory parity, latency, memory, and scaling values in this subsection
are quarantined pending clean exact-identity reruns under
\EngineContract{}. They must not be read as
current MLX qualification or economics.

The small semantic cases above establish a profile contract, not a scaling
law. We therefore apply the same normalized suffix space to pinned 4-bit
Qwen3-8B, 14B, and 32B checkpoints and Qwen3-30B-A3B on an M4 Pro with 48~GiB
unified memory. Each model sees the same five unique annotated-evidence
examples per dataset from QASPER, HotpotQA, and 2Wiki. Selection identity and
the 384-token source cap are fixed across conditions. This is workload-backed
calibration evidence; it is not a routed end-task confirmation because the
dataset annotations provide the selected documents.

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\scriptsize
\caption{Large-model MLX profile calibration. Concat and segmented agreement
are greedy-sequence agreement with selected-text E0. PRA MiB counts active
all-layer selected K/V. Every reduced-layer profile remains
\textsc{Calibration Pending}.}
\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{\input{../shared/results/mac_scaling/generated_mlx_scaling_table.tex}}
\label{tab:profile-large-scaling}
\end{table}

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=.94\linewidth]{../shared/results/mac_scaling/mlx_scaling_profiles.pdf}
\caption{Profile sensitivity and execution time across the dense Qwen ladder
and the 30B-A3B control. Larger dense models reduce likelihood sensitivity to
missing early consumers, but do not establish sequence-stable sparse
profiles.}
\label{fig:profile-large-scaling}
\end{figure}

Full concatenated native K/V preserves all 60 selected-text sequences and is
therefore the \ReferenceCorrectness{} execution path for these rows. The live
segmented one-softmax implementation preserves 57/60, with mean absolute
gold-log-probability differences of .189, .027, .039, and .146 nats at 8B,
14B, 32B, and 30B-A3B. It remains a kernel candidate rather than a replacement
for the exact reference path. The dense last-three-quarter profile becomes
less likelihood-sensitive with scale: its mean absolute delta falls from
2.545 to 1.665 to .356 nats. Sequence agreement does not improve monotonically
(.267, .467, and .133), so this trend cannot justify a family-level sparse
default. The MoE profile uses less active K/V but is more layer-sensitive than
dense 32B. Parameter sparsity and semantic-context sparsity are separate
profile axes.

These results leave product naming unchanged but strengthen its basis.
\ReferenceCorrectness{} remains all eligible layers. A reduced
\QualityMaxCandidate{} may be emitted only with model, revision, workload,
materialization, and evidence tier attached. \QualityMax{} remains unassigned;
neither a favorable likelihood mean nor a large-model label can promote it
without held-out sequence/task validation.

\paragraph{Long-context pressure receipt.}
The same frozen selections were then held at at most 384 source tokens while
same-dataset distractors expanded the full condition to 8K. Selected segmented
E2 preserves every selected-text sequence at the measured 8B and 14B points,
but the sign of full-minus-selected answer likelihood changes from $+1.192$
nats at 8B to $-1.485$ at 14B. This confirms that a profile cannot be promoted
from a monotonic larger-model context-tolerance assumption. The physical
profile is stable: selected all-layer K/V is 39.6 MiB at 8B and 43.9 MiB at
14B, versus 1,152 and 1,280 MiB for the 8K dense source. At 32B it is 70.3 MiB
versus 2,048 MiB; selected segmented E2 preserves 13/15 sequences with a
$+0.030$-nat mean likelihood shift. At the three-case 8B
32K boundary, selected K/V remains 39.6 MiB while dense native K/V reaches
4,608 MiB. These are \textsc{Calibration} rows. The 64K request exceeds the
pinned 40,960-position limit and is recorded as unsupported, not silently
shortened.

\paragraph{Corrected 48-GiB scaling and profile-policy receipt.}
The matched M4 Pro run adds one graph warm-up per path and compares warm with
warm and cold with cold. Over 30 natural-QA examples and five seeds per
checkpoint, concatenated E2 preserves every E0 output at 4B, 8B, 14B, and 32B.
Warm E2/E0 ratios contract from 1.054 to 1.031, 1.018, and 1.011; cold usable-
context ratios are 0.932, 0.932, 0.950, and 0.976. The 32B warm 95\% paired-
bootstrap interval is [1.009, 1.013], so this exact path remains slightly above
E0 rather than crossing below it. Unfused all-layer segmented ratios contract
from 1.165 to 1.102, 1.055, and 1.047, but sequence agreement is only 0.833 at
32B. An earlier 17.18-GB host correctly recorded 32B as
\texttt{NOT\_RUN\_CAPACITY\_GATE}; the 48-GiB result supersedes that capacity
limitation without rewriting the earlier receipt.

A held-out noncontiguous Qwen3-4B calibration also fails to remove any layer
group under a 0.05-nat likelihood and 0.90 first-token constraint. The
calibrated set remains 36/36 layers and all 12 held-out outputs match E0.
This is a negative sparsity result, not evidence for a learned reduced
profile. Accordingly, \texttt{BALANCED} means all eligible consumer layers;
every reduced or segmented realization remains
\texttt{CALIBRATION\_PENDING}.

Finally, corrected Qwen3-8B occupied-context rows keep selected detail at
39.6~MiB and selected-E2 completion at 387.4~ms across 2K, 8K, and 32K source
occupancy, with 18/18 selected-text sequence agreement. The full visible path
grows from 6.37 to 31.85~GiB peak allocation. These receipts are imported into
the product matrix with model revision, host, seed count, and measurement
status; they do not upgrade unfused segmented execution to a production
kernel.

\subsection{Cross-family learned-router qualification}

Structural compatibility does not answer whether an extra routing adapter is
useful. We therefore compare two PRA configurations while holding the model,
tokenizer, examples, chunking, and extracted hidden states fixed. The generic
condition scores each mean chunk gist by normalized cosine similarity to the
last query state. The learned condition uses a rank-128 asymmetric linear
router trained with a multi-positive softmax objective. Both conditions retain
the rest of PRA's typed-resource, chunk-index, selection, and consumer-layer
machinery. Thus the comparison isolates the routing adapter rather than
comparing PRA with and without memory.

For each exact checkpoint identity, independent 128-token encoding blocks are
divided into 32-token routing chunks. We extract the normalized attention input
from the final decoder layer while retaining each architecture's native
normalization, masks, RoPE, grouped-query attention, and, for Gemma, mixed
sliding/global topology. Identity-disjoint splits contain 48 training, 16
validation, and 32 held-out test examples, evenly divided between QASPER and
HotpotQA. The HF runs use bfloat16 after a finite-feature gate; the gate rejects
non-finite extraction before optimization or publication. Five seeds (11, 23,
37, 53, and 71) are trained for 512 steps; the
seed with the highest combined validation AUC$_{0--30}$ is selected before the
test split is evaluated.

\input{../shared/results/paper4_5_runtime/hf_catalog_adapters/generated_hf_catalog_adapters.tex}

\begin{figure*}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=.88\textwidth]{../shared/results/paper4_5_runtime/hf_catalog_adapters/adapter_recall_comparison.pdf}
\caption{Parameter-free generic routing and the validation-selected learned
router on held-out identities. Learned routing consistently improves QASPER,
but HotpotQA recall is flat or lower. The adapter is consequently a
dataset-qualified option, not the catalog default.}
\label{fig:hf-catalog-adapters}
\end{figure*}

Table~\ref{tab:hf-catalog-adapters} separates four conclusions. First, the
adapter is useful on QASPER across model scale and family: $R_{20}$ rises from
0.413 to 0.640 for Qwen3-4B, 0.318 to 0.679 for Qwen3-14B, 0.318 to 0.468 for
Llama-3.1-8B, 0.226 to 0.454 for Gemma-3-1B, and 0.375 to 0.610 for
Qwen3.5-27B. The matched Qwen2.5-1.5B
checkpoints extend the result to post-trained HF identities: general instruction
tuning rises from 0.296 to 0.381, while code-and-instruction tuning rises from
0.173 to 0.394. Second, this gain is not a
universal property of additional parameters. HotpotQA $R_{20}$ declines by
0.044, 0.180, 0.195, 0.018, and 0.210 for the five MLX checkpoints, and by 0.051 and
0.183 for the two HF checkpoints. Gemma's HotpotQA MRR nevertheless
rises from 0.385 to 0.538, showing that even the direction can depend on the
metric. Third, model size alone does not remove domain dependence: Qwen3.5-27B
retains a substantial QASPER gain while producing the largest Qwen HotpotQA
loss, whereas Qwen3-14B has the largest QASPER gain.
Fourth, post-training changes routing geometry even when architecture and size
are matched. The code-tuned checkpoint starts from lower generic QASPER recall
but receives the larger learned gain; its qualification still concerns document
evidence routing, not source-code retrieval or coding-task accuracy.

The SDK policy follows the measurement. Every public bundle exposes canonical
\texttt{quality}, \texttt{balanced}, and \texttt{economy} profiles, but only
\texttt{balanced} is currently qualified and recommended. Bundles with a
learned asymmetric router additionally expose \texttt{qasper-learned} as an
opt-in research profile. These are exact checkpoint identities; no result is
transferred between MLX and HF, between base and post-trained checkpoints, or
across tokenizer revisions. Routing diagnostics remain separate from generation
quality, equivalence, and serving economics.

\subsection{Qwen3.5-27B hybrid-attention qualification}

The 27B Qwen3.5 checkpoint tests a materially different deployment boundary.
We run the exact \path{mlx-community/Qwen3.5-27B-4bit} revision
\texttt{45797d2...9553} on an M4 Pro with 48~GiB unified memory. The model has
64 decoder layers in a repeating three-DeltaNet/one-full-attention pattern,
so only 16 layers expose ordinary attention K/V. A short warm-up decodes at
31.1 tokens/s with 15.4~GiB peak unified allocation. PRA can select and
serialize evidence for the whole model, and the router can use the final
attention-input representation. It cannot honestly detach only K/V: the
remaining 48 layers also require recurrent and convolution state. The bundle
therefore advertises Selected Context and fails closed for Native Memory.

The routing protocol matches the catalog experiment above: 48 training, 16
validation, and 32 identity-disjoint test examples; five seeds; 32-token
chunks; and a rank-128 asymmetric router with 1,310,720 parameters. The
validation-selected seed improves QASPER $R_{20}$ from 0.375 to 0.610 and MRR
from 0.346 to 0.683. It simultaneously lowers HotpotQA $R_{20}$ from 0.518 to
0.308 and MRR from 0.656 to 0.282. This is not a general 27B routing upgrade.
The public \texttt{qasper-learned} profile is opt-in; generic routing remains
the default.

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\small
\caption{Qwen3.5-27B held-out routing at a 20\% chunk budget.}
\label{tab:qwen35-routing}
\input{../shared/results/paper4_5_runtime/hf_catalog_adapters/qwen3.5-27b/generated_qwen35_routing.tex}
\end{table}

We then execute the same 32 test questions under full context, generic PRA,
learned PRA, and an evidence-aware oracle control. The selected conditions use
about 375 visible tokens instead of 1,669 on HotpotQA and 773 instead of 3,648
on QASPER. Mean TTFT falls from 15.31 to 3.64~s and from 32.94 to 7.08--7.09~s,
respectively. Quality depends on routing. Generic HotpotQA F1 is 0.602 versus
0.887 full, while the oracle reaches 0.900 with the same selected-token budget.
On QASPER, learned routing improves F1 from 0.385 generic to 0.459, narrowing
but not closing the gap to 0.532 full. The oracle's QASPER evidence recall is
0.958 but F1 is only 0.470, which also exposes answer-generation error and the
small cohort's sensitivity to yes/no response formatting.

The immutable bundle is published as
\path{EInnovator/pra-qwen3-5-27b-mlx-4bit} at commit
\texttt{48d50f2...fde0}. It contains the structural contract, the router, exact
base/tokenizer revisions, aggregate and row-level qualification receipts, and
the native-memory exclusion. An adapter can therefore improve a qualified
domain without being enabled globally, while unsupported physical state never
silently degrades to a misleading K/V-only implementation.

\paragraph{Evidence-to-card publication.}
The following exported Native-Memory headline is historical pre-gate evidence.
It is retained for auditability, but no longer qualifies a bundle or current
engine behavior; the current public catalog recommends Selected Context until
the corrected exact-identity reruns complete.
A schema-checked importer now reads the paired MLX artifacts rather than
copying numbers into model cards. It rejects a mismatch in model ID, immutable
revision, quantization, engine/version, profile, or execution mode. For the
8B, 14B, and 32B Qwen identities, the exported \texttt{BALANCED} headline uses
the concatenated all-consumer native path: all 15 paired natural-QA outputs
match selected text, token F1 is unchanged, and visible prompt tokens fall by
89.1\%. Mean completion-latency deltas are respectively $+3.0\%$, $+1.1\%$,
and $+0.5\%$; the 32B median-TTFT point estimate is $-6.6\%$. These small
cohorts support \textsc{Engine Qualified}, not production-wide qualification.
Router recall is printed only under research diagnostics.

\subsection{Product profile and benchmark matrix}

\paragraph{Historical pre-gate native rows.}
The registry snapshot and generated tables below preserve the classifications
made when the measurements were captured. Every engine-dependent E2 value in
that snapshot is now \texttt{HISTORICAL\_PRE\_GATE}; labels such as
\textsc{Measured} record the decision at that time, not current qualification.
Structural profiles and explicit \textsc{Not Measured} cells remain usable.

The registry also emits a strict machine-readable product row rather than
mixing numbers and labels such as ``not measured.'' Schema 2.0 keeps numeric
values, measurement status, and provenance in separate fields for every
quality, latency, memory, transfer, scheduler, and reuse metric. Missing values
are null with explicit \texttt{NOT\_MEASURED} or \texttt{NOT\_APPLICABLE}
status; they are never encoded as zero. Unknown columns, duplicate row
identities, unsupported integration levels, throughput without a paired
quality measure, and invalid status values fail validation.
Table~\ref{tab:product-matrix-v2} is generated from the
same JSON artifact used by the SDK.

The calibrated layer candidates become useful to an SDK only after their

The accepted \QualityMax{} name is deliberately reserved and has no

keyed by model ID, immutable revision, workload, and profile. Engine, engine
version, hardware, and dtype identify a physical realization of that row; they
do not redefine the profile. This separation prevents one GPU measurement
from becoming an implied portable latency property.

\subsection{Inherited typed-record product policy}

The runtime also forward-ports Paper 7's finalized typed-record policy without
reinterpreting its metrics as layer-profile evidence. Per-record compression
accepts target-token, maximum-token, and ratio bounds. Typed postings, indexed
BM25, and a small embedding index are built independently of the full-body
native Q/K size gate. Above that gate, cheap hybrid discovery returns an exact
selector; only the selected region is eligible for lazy native encoding and
detail-K/V materialization.

In Paper 7's controlled protocol, type-aware compression reduced mean visible
tokens from 180.8 to 54.1, a compact ratio of 25\%. Automatic recovery was
71.9\%, and cheap-hybrid recall at four was 87.5\% over 24 cases. These are
inherited measurements with their original cohort and provenance, not new
Paper 4.5 quality results. Their role here is to define canonical runtime
behavior: full native indexing is budgeted, while cheaper address modes and
exact backing identities remain available above the gate.

The task smoke invokes the inherited Paper 8 task graph, provenance attachment,
and task-structural selector rather than copying expected IDs into the
report. It selects the expected record in all 18 model/condition rows (three
tasks, three models, visible and selected-full conditions). Selected-full
one-token accuracy equals visible-prefix accuracy for every model. Tool and
skill disclosure, safe execution, compact results, and session persistence are
shared control-plane contracts and pass the executed SDK tests and notebook;
we do not label them model-generated tool-use results. The base Llama mirror has
no chat template, so no Llama tool-call claim is made.

\subsection{Caching and reload amplification}

The runtime includes a thread-safe, byte-bounded LRU for opaque warm or hot
payloads.  Besides hits, misses, and evictions, it records loaded and reused
bytes.  We define reload amplification as
\begin{equation}
A_{\mathrm{reload}} = \frac{B_{\mathrm{loaded}}}{\max(B_{\mathrm{resident}},1)}.
\end{equation}
An apparently high hit rate can still be costly if the misses repeatedly load
large references.  Conversely, byte reuse can be high even when object-level
hit rate is modest.  Both views are needed.
Native reusable keys include tenant, user, session, resource, layer, payload
variant, source revision, positional frame, materialization layout, and scope
signature. Raw backing can be reused across compatible encodings, but per-layer,
rebound, and packed payloads require exact geometry identity. The audit accepts
only the identical key and rejects changed source, position, packing, or request
scope. Per-tenant byte caps evict within that tenant before the global
cap is considered, and scope-local cleanup cannot evict another tenant's
entries. A concurrent same-URI fixture confirms tenant isolation under pressure
and cancellation cleanup. This is logical/runtime isolation, not hard GPU
partitioning.

\section{Engine-Portability Model}

\subsection{Agent, gateway, and engine are independent}

The distributed contract has three authorities. The agent owns user-visible
task and session semantics, typed records, provenance, and authorization
references. The gateway owns transport sessions, logical resource mapping,
protocol translation, and explicit capability negotiation. The inference
engine owns tensor layout, physical K/V blocks, device placement, attention,
and scheduling. The normal wire carries JSON logical identities rather than
raw K/V tensors.

The agent boundary is now explicit in code. \texttt{AgentTurnContext} contains
the conventional chat trajectory and separate context, tool, skill, and task
records. \texttt{PRAAgent} performs semantic selection but does not choose a
wire representation. \texttt{NegotiatedRemoteBackend} probes
\path{/v1/pra/capabilities} once at launch or reconnect and resolves TEXT,
PRA-FULL, or PRA-DELTA from advertised features. A reachable endpoint without
the capability route is ordinary OpenAI; an unreachable endpoint is an error,
not a reason to silently downgrade.

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\fbox{\begin{minipage}{0.88\linewidth}\centering\small
\texttt{AgentTurnContext}\par
$\swarrow$\hspace{7em}$\searrow$\par
OpenAI message spine\hspace{3em}typed \texttt{ContextRecord}s\par
$\searrow$\hspace{7em}$\swarrow$\par
\texttt{AUTO} capability negotiation\par
$\swarrow$\hspace{7em}$\searrow$\par
TEXT compatibility\hspace{3em}PRA logical wire\par
\hspace{12em}$\swarrow$\hspace{5em}$\searrow$\par
\hspace{9em}Gateway G10/G11\hspace{2em}direct PRA engine\par
\hspace{12em}$\downarrow$\hspace{7em}$\downarrow$\par
\hspace{9em}E0 or PRA engine\hspace{2em}logical/native resolution
\end{minipage}}
\caption{The agent preserves the pretrained conversational spine separately
from detached typed context. Compatibility loss occurs only at an explicit
TEXT or G10 downgrade; native K/V remains engine-side.}
\label{fig:agent-transport}
\end{figure}

The canonical record projection preserves ID, type, URI, version, source
fingerprint, provenance, authorization scope, task ownership/status, available
views, selected view, and sharing scope. It never exports Python policy objects,
native tensors, or physical page IDs. G10 advertises that its immediate gateway
accepts typed records even when its downstream engine is E0; consequently AUTO
sends logical records to the gateway and the gateway owns deterministic text
injection. G11 forwards record, task, and session semantics. An ordinary server
receives no \texttt{pra} field.

The implemented \texttt{PRAWireRequest} preserves messages, tenant/session/task
IDs, resources, facets, budgets, execution-policy hints, provenance, and
required capabilities in a version-1 extension of the OpenAI request.
\texttt{PRAEngineAdapter} reports static capabilities,
prepares and closes sessions, and offers generation plus streaming. Four deterministic gateway modes cover both sides of the migration:
G00 passes ordinary traffic to an ordinary engine; G10 converts explicitly
selected resources to labeled text for a PRA-unaware engine; G01 infers typed
resources from system and tool-result boundaries before calling a PRA-aware
engine; and G11 preserves an explicit PRA request for a PRA-aware engine. G10
is control-plane PRA with text materialization, not native PRA execution.

Capability negotiation is fail-closed. A request that requires native K/V is
rejected by an E0 engine unless it explicitly allows text fallback and selects
G10. The trace then names the unsupported capabilities, downgrade, selected
logical IDs, gateway mode, correlation ID, and whether native K/V was used.
Cross-tenant resources and credential-like request metadata are rejected before
translation. Relevance still does not grant source or tool authorization.
G01 promotes only explicit record boundaries---system records, tool results,
attachments, and already typed messages. It does not infer semantic records
from arbitrary unstructured user traffic.

We freeze four integration levels. E0 receives ordinary selected text. E1
understands logical PRA identity, versions, intervals, authorization, session
deltas, and fallback semantics, but still realizes context through an ordinary
model path. E2 consumes selected resources directly as detached native K/V or
an equivalent non-prefix representation with matched geometry, one attention
normalization, request-local lifetime, and isolation. E3 adds scheduler-owned
placement, prefetch, promotion, eviction, affinity, batching, and distributed
movement on top of E2. The local HF adapter is the E2 semantic reference. The OpenAI-compatible HTTP adapter and standalone
\texttt{pra gateway serve} process implement E0. FreeToken remains reachable
through that E0 surface~\cite{freetokenDocs2026}. Companion Papers 6.1 and 6.2
exercise native SGLang-MLX and MLX-LM E2 mechanisms; their provider declarations
are available here, but their engine measurements do not promote Paper 4.5 to
E3. Paper 6 now exercises native vLLM-Metal V1 generation and
scheduler-disjoint pages under a pinned provider, while its generic provider
remains E0 unless that bridge is explicitly installed. The portable
HF adapter implements E2 native execution and streaming but remains stateless
between gateway calls. The configurable remote E1 envelope carries incremental
messages and resource operations when the selected engine advertises those
capabilities. The HTTP gateway emits OpenAI-compatible server-sent events.
Cancellation is cooperative at token
boundaries; request-owned references are released only after the decode worker
stops. No backing content is included in trace metadata.
The capability object names native K/V, logical references, streaming,
selected-interval materialization, request lifetime, host/device residency,
tenant isolation, phase selection, and scheduler hints independently. The HF
reference advertises phase selection through the constrained cache-correct
handoff above, but still rejects scheduler hints. This prevents an E2 adapter
from being mistaken for an E3 scheduler merely because both accept the same
logical wire request.

\subsection{Session-aware realization and cumulative context reuse}

PRA does not replace ordinary prefix caching. A session may simultaneously
retain a conventional sequential-prefix K/V cache and separately address PRA
records or native detail. The gateway preserves append-only prefixes for
ordinary engines and can send logical deltas to PRA-aware remote engines.
Between selection and request construction, an engine-independent realization
planner reconciles a visible-materialization ledger against the actual
serialized model context. Identity is strict: resource ID, version, selected
interval, rendering profile, and rendered-content digest must all agree.
Compatible occurrences remain in place; dropped, superseded, removed, or
task-closed occurrences materialize again. This rule survives process restart
through a durable logical snapshot, while opaque engine handles and inferred
physical residency are intentionally discarded.

The planner classifies each selected item as already visible, natively
available, requiring materialization, optionally refreshable, or invalid. Its
accounting keeps three quantities disjoint. Logical visibility prevents a
duplicate input occurrence. An engine prefix observation reports only physical
reuse of a sequential prefix and may be confirmed, likely, unknown, or absent.
Native Memory reports selected model-native state only on a qualified path.
Correctness never depends on a likely or unknown prefix hit, and worker,
model-fingerprint, or engine-restart changes invalidate unproven continuity.
The first PRA-DELTA turn sends a full inventory. Later unchanged resources emit
body-free UNCHANGED operations; changed identity, content fingerprint,
authorization, task ownership, or shareability emits ADD or UPDATE, while
retirement emits REMOVE. The gateway retains bodies outside its public trace so
G10 can render unchanged resources without requiring retransmission. Endpoint
restart, reconnect, protocol mismatch, or explicit refresh clears the agent's
acknowledged inventory and forces a full resynchronization before delta mode
resumes.
Figure~\ref{fig:gateway-two-cache} makes these state classes explicit. The
inference engine physically owns the ordinary prefix cache. PRA logically owns
resource identities, versions, addresses, and selection; the physical owner of
their indexes or selected native K/V depends on E2--E3 integration. A hit or
invalidation on one axis therefore says nothing by itself about the other.

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.96\linewidth]{gateway_two_cache_architecture.pdf}
\caption{The session-aware gateway maintains two independent state axes.
Sequential prompt K/V follows the engine's ordinary prefix-cache policy;
detached PRA resources follow the PRA lifecycle and may be text-materialized
at E0 or retained natively at E2+.}
\label{fig:gateway-two-cache}
\end{figure}

The wire contract promotes \texttt{history\_mode}, engine-session identity,
prefix handle, cache-affinity key, maximum decode length, and resource
operations to typed fields. \texttt{FULL} means that the request supplies
canonical complete history; \texttt{DELTA} means only appended messages;
\texttt{AUTO} resolves from capabilities rather than message count. Stable
resources similarly produce \texttt{ADD}, \texttt{UPDATE}, \texttt{REMOVE},
or \texttt{UNCHANGED} operations while preserving ID, URI, version digest,
record type, and authorization scope.

A versioned engine registry resolves OpenAI-generic, vLLM, SGLang, FreeToken,
Ollama, Hugging Face, llama.cpp, MLX, and custom profiles. Unknown compatible
servers safely remain E0, full-history, and cache-telemetry-unknown. vLLM and
SGLang profiles represent automatic prefix-cache capability but do not infer
native PRA from an engine label. Companion E2 mechanism results are registered
through explicit providers and remain separate from Paper 4.5 evidence. The
local HF reference remains E2-native but
stateless across calls; the session/resource-delta row is exercised by a
deterministic remote-engine contract fixture. Explicit configuration may
override registry defaults; resolved capabilities, rather than labels, control
transport.

For G10, the former message-zero synthetic system block remains only as a
legacy experimental control. The default inserts selected evidence into the
current user turn, after the byte-identical prior serialized conversation.
Role-sequence tests cover Qwen, Llama, Gemma, and generic OpenAI-style
transports. This placement preserves template validity and the previous
prefix. It does not imply that changing evidence is cacheable: only the prior
sequential prefix is reusable.

The sidecar is keyed by tenant, logical session, and model. It retains only
ephemeral engine handles, prefix fingerprints, resource versions, policy
digests, and affinity hints; durable canonical history continues to use
\texttt{SessionService}. Preparation is idempotent, explicit close releases
engine state, and cancellation of one stream does not close its reusable
session. Model revision, chat-template, visible-prefix, history-rewrite,
engine-restart, and invalid-handle changes invalidate the relevant handle.
Updating one detached resource emits a resource delta without invalidating an
otherwise stable conversation prefix. The cache-affinity key is only a future
scheduler hint; no worker-placement result is claimed.

\subsection{Agent-side compatibility before native engine work}

The pre-SGLang deployment gate asks whether PRA's logical record semantics can
survive an existing agent without patching its model runtime. The
\texttt{DeepSeekHarnessPRAAdapter} consumes durable tool-result, session-reference,
and attachment events. The \texttt{PiCodingAgentPRAAdapter} consumes Pi's public
tool-completion and completed-message events. Both deduplicate event identities,
retain typed provenance and task/session IDs, and emit the same tensor-free
\texttt{PRAWireRequest} used by the HTTP gateway.

Across \AgentPluginCases{} deterministic five-seed contract cases, the DeepSeek
and Pi bridges each pass \DeepSeekPluginPass\% and \PiPluginPass\% of identity,
typing, task-metadata, deduplication, and explicit-fallback checks. In every G10
case the ordinary engine receives labeled evidence text, zero logical resources,
and no native-K/V claim. These tests validate the integration boundary, not the
quality or latency of either upstream agent.

\subsection{Compilation ladder}

The eager path is the correctness baseline.  The first compiled primitive is a
paired \texttt{index\_select} over K and V with dynamic selected-token count.
Cold compile time and warm latency are separate rows, and parity is checked
against eager outputs.  Failure never falls back into an eager row.

Triton, custom CUDA, and fusion follow profiling rather than precede it.  The
candidate hot paths are interval gather, deduplication/remap, packing, and
position preparation.  A kernel is justified only if it improves the complete
request after launch, transfer, and synchronization costs.  This ordering
prevents a fast isolated kernel from becoming a misleading end-to-end result.

\subsection{Thin vLLM handoff}

PagedAttention showed how virtualized K/V blocks improve serving utilization
and batching~\cite{kwon2023vllm}.  Deep PRA integration would additionally make
logical PRA blocks, semantic residency, and retrieval-aware prefetch visible to
the scheduler.  That is outside this paper's boundary.

The implemented \texttt{VLLMThinBackend} instead emits a
\texttt{VLLMThinRequest}: prompt, stable selected URIs, materialized token count,
and ordinary K/V metadata.  Semantic scores do not cross the boundary.  An
ordinary engine can consume already selected memory without owning retrieval
policy.  When no executor is installed, the backend produces the handoff for
inspection and raises on generation rather than pretending to serve.

\section{Relation to Existing Systems}

PRA is complementary to several kinds of memory optimization.  H2O and SnapKV
select or retain influential cache entries inside an active sequence
\cite{zhang2023h2o,li2024snapkv}.  PRA begins from stable external identities and
query-time addressability, so its inactive detail K/V may live outside the
accelerator.  These methods can eventually share page layouts or eviction
mechanisms, but their selection semantics differ.

Prompt Cache and CacheBlend reuse precomputed prompt or retrieved-knowledge
state~\cite{gim2023promptcache,yao2024cacheblend}.  Their lesson is directly
relevant: recomputation and transfer can dominate.  PRA adds bounded semantic
selection before native K/V consumption.  RetrievalAttention similarly joins
vector retrieval with long-context attention, reinforcing the need to count
index and transfer work rather than only selected attention work.

GQA reduces the number of physical K/V heads~\cite{ainslie2023gqa}.  Our runtime
preserves this layout through warm storage and materialization.  It does not
expand K/V to query-head count as an implementation convenience.

\section{Canonical Evaluation Methodology}

\subsection{Questions and claim levels}

The initial gate asks four narrow questions.  First, does the portable selected-
K/V implementation preserve exact tensor parity?  Second, how large are eager
gather and interval-pack costs?  Third, how much does CPU residency add for the
same selected payload?  Fourth, can the local toolchain execute the compiled
path?  Quality and routing selection remain frozen.

Claims are labeled by evidence.  \emph{Measured} means executed on the recorded
host.  \emph{Inherited} means read from a checked-in earlier experiment with
its own protocol.  \emph{Contract only} means code exists but no external
engine ran.  \emph{Architectural} means only an interface comparison is made.

\subsection{Superseded evidence ledger}

Several early agent-cache results remain useful diagnostics but no longer
support the stronger claim originally associated with them.  We retain their
artifacts rather than deleting inconvenient evidence.  Table~\ref{tab:superseded-agent-evidence}
states the demotion rule applied throughout the report.

\paragraph{Strict resident-subset invariant.}
After the one-time source bootstrap, a strict native request may evaluate only
genuinely new history delta, the current request suffix, and generated tokens.
It must satisfy
\[
R_{\mathrm{orig}}=R_{\mathrm{synthetic}}=R_{\mathrm{partial}}=0,
\qquad C_{\mathrm{selectedKV}}=0,
\]
where the three $R$ terms count re-evaluated retained-original history,
newly encoded post-cache replacements, and repeated uncached old-history tails.
The copy term counts physical movement used to pack selected K/V.  Source
bootstrap, new-delta encoding, and canonical-suffix lifecycle traffic are
reported independently and never hidden inside these zeros.  A result is
\emph{hybrid native plus transformed context} rather than strict native PRA if
any post-cache replacement token is evaluated, even when
$R_{\mathrm{orig}}=C_{\mathrm{selectedKV}}=0$.

This quarantine also governs earlier quantitative runtime sections in this
report. Their artifacts are retained to document the development path, but a
number printed from a pre-gate artifact is not a current engine or product
claim. The machine-readable audit enumerates the affected bundle identities and
the frozen rerun order.

The first corrected-run prequalification uses one frozen QASPER example with
Qwen3-8B MLX 8-bit on the 48~GiB M4 Pro. Native Memory is captured as a view of
the already evaluated live prefix, not encoded independently. The alias path
and an owned value-identical reference execute the same segmented consumer and
have zero maximum logit difference; E0 prefix-cache and segmented E2 greedy
outputs also match in all four bounded regimes. Their gold-answer log
probabilities differ by 0.177887, which is retained as cross-consumer numerical
drift rather than misclassified as a selection error. Selected history is not
re-encoded and selection materialization copies zero K/V bytes. Physical copy
beyond selection remains unmeasured, and the run is explicitly
\emph{prequalification}: its source tree was dirty and host load was not timing-
controlled, so none of its latency values is admissible as a headline.

\subsection{Gateway session and prefix protocol}

The controlled gateway fixture executes five turns with changing selected
records under four conditions: legacy message-zero G10 insertion,
prefix-preserving G10, E0 message deltas, and an E1 logical
message/resource-delta contract. It records whether each prior serialized
message sequence remains an exact prefix, the reusable-message fraction,
invalidations, JSON message and resource bytes, delta bytes, and session reuse.
The workload is deterministic and uses a simulated adapter so that transport
semantics can be isolated from model quality and network variance. Byte counts
refer to the fixture's JSON envelopes, not tokenizer output or K/V allocation.
Physical cache hits remain unknown unless adapter telemetry supplies them.

\subsection{Negotiated agent-transport protocol}

A second deterministic fixture follows one task through open, tool-result,
waiting, resume, and complete turns. Its detached stream contains a long
document, an atomic tool schema, a skill record, versioned task state, and a
large tool result. The conversational spine grows normally. We freeze the same
selected record identities and compare complete TEXT rendering, PRA-FULL, and
PRA-DELTA JSON requests. Reported bytes include OpenAI fields and PRA metadata;
visible-token estimates count message text only. Selection parity, task-status
projection, and provider-native tool-schema preservation are hard assertions.
The fixture does not call a language model, so it measures protocol semantics
and retransmission rather than answer quality or TTFT.

\subsection{Portable primitive protocol}

The new benchmark uses PyTorch 2.12.1 with CUDA 12.6 on Windows 10, Python
3.10.11, and an NVIDIA GeForce GTX 950M with compute capability 5.0.  Each
synthetic tensor has four physical K/V heads, width 64, and 8,192 candidate
tokens in float32.  Selection admits 512 tokens (6.25\%).  Batch sizes are one
and four.  CPU rows use five warmups and 30 timed repetitions; CUDA rows use ten
warmups and 50 repetitions.  Every CUDA timing synchronizes before and after
the operation.

Indexed gather uses evenly distributed selected token IDs.  Interval packing
uses eight intervals under the same 512-token budget.  The hierarchy comparison
holds intervals and output bytes fixed while moving sources among HBM, pinned
CPU, and ordinary CPU.  Compiler errors are artifact rows.  The benchmark is a
mechanism profile, not TTFT or throughput for a language model.

The layout sweep holds 8,192 total source tokens across four references and two
layers.  It packs 32-token pages in layer-major, reference-major, chunk-major,
or block-major order, then restores an identical 512-token logical selection.
Store construction is a cold cost; reported layout gather latency follows the
same warmup and repetition protocol as the other device rows.

\subsection{Inherited profiles}

Two earlier artifacts answer adjacent questions.  The Paper 2 public-API demo
ran frozen Qwen3-0.6B over two QASPER and two HotpotQA examples.  It records
reference ingest, query encoding, routing, generation, K/V fractions, transfers,
and peak GPU allocation.  The Paper 4 lifecycle fixture ran eight deterministic
resources through authenticated cold, warm, and hot states.  Its answer proxy
is required-document availability, not language quality.  We do not pool these
measurements with the new microbenchmark or compute cross-host speedups.

\subsection{Execution-policy mechanism profile}

We instantiate the same random-weight, three-layer tiny Llama configuration for
five seeds and ingest two stable synthetic references. Every condition uses
three active PRA layers, deterministic decoding, and at most three generated
tokens. We compare request/shared at first and last routing layers,
request/per-layer, token/shared at first and last routing layers, and
token/per-layer. The profile records routing and selection epochs, temporal and
layer Jaccard, selected-identity agreement, and generation wall time. It tests
execution semantics and relative routing work, not language quality or
production latency. EOS can terminate before three tokens, so token-level
operation counts are averaged over the actual forwards.

\subsection{RAG engine-mode protocol}

Retrieval and selector quality belong to Papers~3.2 and~3.3.  Here MultiHop-RAG
is only a controlled engine workload: a fixed 50-question, seed-11 cohort and a
tamper-evident receipt hold selected document intervals and token budgets fixed
between realizations~\cite{tang2024multihoprag}.  The engine comparison is
Selected Context (visible text re-prefilled per request) versus Native Memory
(the same selected text encoded as detached MLX K/V), each measured cold and
warm.  We report TTFT, total latency, throughput, ingestion time, visible and
native tokens, retained K/V bytes, peak memory, and output parity.  Selector and
answer-quality artifacts remain available for audit but are not repeated here.

\section{Results}

\subsection{Four-claim evidence map}

Table~\ref{tab:four-claim-evidence} is the report's claim ledger.  Later
mechanism and product results are evidence for one of these rows rather than
additional independent claims.

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\scriptsize
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{p{.21\linewidth}Yp{.25\linewidth}}
\toprule
Claim & Strongest current evidence & Present boundary \\
\midrule
Correct live-history semantics & Same-resident-state controls qualify llama.cpp,
vLLM-Metal, and the bounded vLLM CUDA path.  Selected history is not re-encoded,
and 100\% is a semantic no-op. & Fresh-prefill/cache-reconstruction rows are
diagnostics, not evidence of resident reuse. \\
Engine portability contract & vLLM-Metal passes 24/24 lifecycle checks; bounded
vLLM CUDA adopts scheduler-owned pages with zero K/V copy, H2D transfer, or
history re-encoding.  HF, MLX, and SGLang now also pass their declared sparse
consumer/lifecycle scopes. & HF's fused Triton path remains limited to its
declared batch-one Qwen/CUDA geometry; the Metal kernel is limited to head
dimension at most 256 and direct MLX batch one, while SGLang evaluates batched
rows independently.  vLLM CUDA still excludes partial pages, hybrid groups,
and multiprocess execution. \\
Agent retention semantics & Paper~8.5 supplies backend-neutral plans and
matched-budget controls.  Prompt-pinned E2+F1C preserves 3/3 paired
episode-six solves, uses 33 versus 34 calls, and saves 41.71\% on the paired
denominator. & Three identities do not establish greater-than-90\% population
preservation.  Older E2/E3 and R4/P1 rows remain diagnostics rather than
defaults.  Paper~4.5 independently measures whether logical omission becomes
resident K/V omission. \\
Coding-agent utility frontier & Fresh MLX and SGLang-MLX/Qwen2.5-Coder-14B
controls reproduce the plain engine's six actions and exact patch at PRA-100.
Task-aware PRA-90 preserves both official solves and call counts, with zero
selected-history re-encoding or selected-K/V copy.  A separate hybrid
llama.cpp/Qwen3-Coder-30B transfer preserves its eight-call solve while omitting
40.23\% weighted steady-state original K/V.  A post-fix MLX/Qwen3-4B BF16 gate
also matches all seven plain actions at PRA-100; tail-90 preserves the official
solve and patch in five calls at 93.81\% mean retention. & The stricter frozen matched-tail
policy loses the Task-1 solve identically on MLX and SGLang-MLX at 89.57\%
retention, demonstrating policy/model dependence.  The successful task-aware
MLX/SGLang result is one short task and realizes only 2.19\% physical-input saving; other K/V
movement and consumer scratch remain engine dependent.  The llama.cpp plan
contains post-cache receipts and is not a strict resident-subset result.
The Qwen3-4B tail row has only 6.19\% within-trajectory omission; its 37.20\%
paired reduction is dominated by call contraction, while qualification-
reference copying and a 626.5~MB peak request temporary remain visible.  Earlier
agent rows remain superseded diagnostics.  Fresh HF/vLLM rows, broader tasks,
and repeated cross-model economics are pending. \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\caption{Four-claim evidence map.  Passing a mechanism gate does not silently
promote task utility, portability, zero-copy behavior, or runtime benefit.}
\label{tab:four-claim-evidence}
\end{table}

Four derived quantities remain deliberately separate:
\begin{align}
G_{\mathrm{context}}&=C_{\mathrm{full}}/C_{\mathrm{selected}}, &
G_{\mathrm{session}}&=T_{\mathrm{selected\ needed}}/T_{\mathrm{new}},\\
G_{\mathrm{native}}&=C_{\mathrm{selected}}/C_{\mathrm{native}}, &
Q_{\mathrm{useful}}&=(\mathrm{requests/s})P(\mathrm{quality\ pass}).
\end{align}
Here $C$ must use one stated accounting boundary and $T$ one stated materialized
unit; the ratios are not interchangeable. In the scheduler-invisible CUDA
vLLM fixture, a historical pre-gate diagnostic, scheduler-accounted tokens give
$G_{\mathrm{context}}=276/36=7.67$ and
$G_{\mathrm{native}}=36/20=1.80$; E2 carries no scheduler-visible source token,
but the 20 query tokens remain. In the separate 50-turn agent trace, resource-
body transmissions give $G_{\mathrm{session}}=275/21=13.10$. Because every
CUDA row passes its quality gate, useful throughput equals 56.25 requests/s for
E0, 45.46 for HOT E2, and 42.44 for WARM E2. Context and Native Gain therefore
do not imply a throughput win.

\subsection{RAG results: engine realization only}

\paragraph{Historical pre-gate diagnostic.}
Every Native-Memory exactness, latency, throughput, and memory value in this
subsection predates \EngineContract{}. The raw
values are retained for audit and hypothesis formation only; they do not
qualify current MLX behavior or workload economics.

Papers~3.2 and~3.3 report retrieval, selection, evidence coverage, and answer
quality.  Their current policy keeps generic document selection unqualified.
Paper~4.5 therefore asks only what the runtime does after a selection is frozen.
On the 50-question 2K cohort, Selected Context and Native Memory match every
output and quality metric; detached MLX K/V introduces no additional quality
change in this comparison.

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\scriptsize
\caption{MLX-LM Qwen3-4B 4-bit realization metrics for 50 frozen 2K RAG
selections on a 48~GiB M4 Pro. Times are milliseconds; cold includes native
encoding, while warm Native Memory reuses retained immutable K/V.}
\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{%
\input{../shared/results/paper4_5_runtime_productization/rag/powered_qwen3_4b_mlx_4bit_n20_2k_50/paper_runtime_table.tex}}
\label{tab:powered-rag-runtime}
\end{table}

Native Memory reduces mean visible prompt width from 2,105 to 79 tokens while
carrying 2,026 selected native tokens. Cold native execution pays 96~ms more
end-to-end than Selected Context (3.121 versus 3.024~s), a bounded 3.2\%
regression. Once the same selection is retained, mean TTFT falls from 2.640~s
to 0.166~s and total latency from 3.031~s to 0.638~s; throughput rises from
10.56 to 50.21 tokens/s. Warm native active detail is 299~MB, while measured
peak unified memory is 0.63~GB lower than the repeatedly prefilled Selected
Context path. The native-memory and economic gates pass only for this repeated-
selection regime; they do not override the selector qualification owned by
Papers~3.2 and~3.3.

At the 4K boundary, contiguous selector-frozen realization remains exact. Cold Native
Memory costs 6.313~s versus 6.109~s for Selected Context, a 3.3\% regression.
After retaining the exact selection, Native Memory costs 0.799~s versus
6.121~s, an 86.9\% reduction, while moving 4,084 selected tokens out of the
visible prompt. The cost is approximately 602~MB of active all-layer K/V.
This is a repeated-selection benefit, not a comparison with a selected-text
prefix-cache hit.

The MLX persistent-corpus mode changes the reuse unit from a complete
selection to independently encoded 256-token chunks. Across 50 changing
questions it reuses 22.0\% of chunk lookups, materializes 80,117 unique native
tokens rather than about 101,294 selected tokens, and reduces cumulative
request wall time from 151.19 to 131.78~s (12.8\%). Mean TTFT falls from 2.633
to 2.163~s. Retained detail reaches 6.38~GB, however, and generated behavior is
not exact: official score changes from 0.440 to 0.380 while token F1 changes
from 0.071 to 0.086. This is not a cache-corruption result. Each chunk is
encoded independently and \texttt{combine\_native\_memories} preserves its
source-local post-RoPE positions; concatenated Selected Context assigns one
global sequence of positions. The experiment consequently measures a real
chunk-resident representation policy, not transport parity. We mark the
selection, native-parity, and economic decomposition gates
\texttt{NOT\_APPLICABLE\_PERSISTENT\_CORPUS}; position-aware composition remains
required.

On the same M4 host, pinned MLX-LM Qwen3-8B 4-bit repeats the engine result:
both selector-frozen text/native pairs are output-exact. Generic cold Native
Memory costs 5.636~s versus 5.535~s, a
1.8\% total-latency regression. Retaining the same native selection reduces
warm total latency from 5.541 to 0.990~s (82.1\%) and TTFT from 4.908 to
0.276~s. Active detail is approximately 285~MB. The 8B run therefore repeats
the native-memory and repeated-selection result across model scale.

\subsection{Stable prefix and session-delta contract}

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.96\linewidth]{gateway_prefix_reuse.pdf}
\caption{Five-turn deterministic gateway profile. The left panel reports
logical reuse of the exact prior serialized message prefix after turn one; the
right reports message bytes sent. Resource bytes are reported separately in
Table~\ref{tab:gateway-session}.}
\label{fig:gateway-prefix}
\end{figure}

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\caption{Controlled gateway session profile. Stable turns use the four turns
after initial setup. Reuse is logical serialized-message reuse, not an
engine-reported cache hit.}
\label{tab:gateway-session}
\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{\input{../shared/results/paper4_5_runtime/generated_gateway_session_table.tex}}
\end{table}

The legacy message-zero G10 control changes the leading evidence block on every
eligible turn, preserving zero of four prior prefixes and recording four
invalidations. Moving evidence to the current turn preserves all four prior
prefixes, with a mean logical reuse fraction of 72.8\%. Full-history
prefix-preserving G10 sends 3,355 message bytes because it still retransmits
history. The session-delta E0 contract retains the same logical prefix while
reducing message transport to 921 bytes; four turns reuse the prepared engine
session.

The E1 contract sends 281 message bytes and 2,297 resource-envelope bytes,
using resource additions, updates, and removals rather than repeating resource
contents indiscriminately. Its combined delta bytes are not lower than the
tiny text fixture because typed resource metadata and operation envelopes are
explicit. This is a semantic and accounting result, not evidence that PRA
transport is always smaller. All four conditions report physical prefix-cache
hits as unknown. No vLLM/SGLang affinity, TTFT, throughput, or scheduler result
is inferred from logical prefix stability.

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\caption{Five-seed repeated-session accounting. Turn one selects A/B/C; turn
two selects B/D. Prefix-cache tokens, native reuse, and TTFT were not measured,
so they remain explicit rather than being inferred from logical visibility.}
\label{tab:session-realization}
\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{\input{../shared/results/paper4_5_runtime/session_realization/generated_session_realization_table.tex}}
\end{table}

Table~\ref{tab:session-realization} isolates the new ledger from retrieval and
engine effects. Without logical reuse, Selected Context renders the selected B
again and materializes 28 tokens across the two turns. Session-aware realization
preserves B's 16-token active occurrence and materializes only the 12 tokens of
D, reducing total visible tokens from 88 to 72 while preserving the controlled
quality check. Full Context also exposes 72 tokens in this fixture but counts all
72 as newly materialized. These are deterministic token-accounting results, not
TTFT or attention-compute measurements; Native Memory remains
\textsc{Not measured} in this cohort.

\subsection{Typed transport avoids repeated record bodies}

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.82\linewidth]{agent_transport_protocol/agent_transport_wire_bytes.pdf}
\caption{Per-turn JSON bytes for the five-turn task/tool workflow. PRA-FULL
preserves explicit metadata but repeats all resource bodies; PRA-DELTA sends a
body only for ADD or UPDATE and keeps unchanged resources logical.}
\label{fig:agent-transport-bytes}
\end{figure}

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\caption{Negotiated transport contract over one deterministic long-running
task. Byte totals are wire serialization, not model tokens or latency.}
\begin{tabular}{lrr}
\toprule
Mode & Total bytes & Resource bodies sent\\
\midrule
TEXT & \AgentTransportTextBytes{} & 24 rendered copies\\
PRA-FULL & \AgentTransportFullBytes{} & \AgentTransportFullBodies{}\\
PRA-DELTA & \AgentTransportDeltaBytes{} & \AgentTransportDeltaBodies{}\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

PRA-FULL is larger than TEXT in this fixture because it carries explicit
identity, views, provenance, task state, and authorization metadata in addition
to complete bodies. PRA-DELTA reduces total wire bytes by
\AgentTransportDeltaTextReduction{} relative to TEXT and
\AgentTransportDeltaFullReduction{} relative to PRA-FULL, while reducing body
transmissions from \AgentTransportFullBodies{} to
\AgentTransportDeltaBodies{}. All five turns preserve selected identity, task
status, and the provider-native tool schema. These are transport invariants,
not evidence that logical PRA improves model quality. A matched model benchmark
must hold selection, model, positions, profile, and generation fixed.

We therefore repeated the comparison with a real MLX-LM Qwen3-0.6B model on
ten held-out QASPER selections. Each condition used the same chat template,
selected records, task state, provider-native tool schema, greedy decoder, and
24-token budget. The gold answer remained in an out-of-band evaluator rather
than request metadata. TEXT, PRA-FULL, and PRA-DELTA produced byte-identical
model messages and identical outputs in \AgentModelParity{} examples; their
mean gold-answer log probability was also identical at $-40.35625$. Mean TTFT
was 507, 506, and 481 ms, respectively, but ten independent first turns do not
support a latency-ordering claim. Their mean wire sizes were
\AgentModelTextWire{}, \AgentModelFullWire{}, and \AgentModelDeltaWire{} bytes;
first-turn typed metadata has a cost before delta reuse begins.

The long-session fixture makes that reuse visible. At 50 turns, including one
forced reconnect and full resynchronization, TEXT sends
\AgentScalingTextBytes{} bytes, PRA-FULL sends \AgentScalingFullBytes{}, and
PRA-DELTA sends \AgentScalingDeltaBytes{}. Delta transport is therefore
\AgentScalingDeltaTextReduction{} smaller than repeated TEXT and
\AgentScalingDeltaFullReduction{} smaller than PRA-FULL. It sends 21 resource
bodies rather than 275 while preserving selection, task state, and tool schema.

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=.78\linewidth]{agent_transport_scaling/agent_transport_scaling.pdf}
\caption{Cumulative wire bytes over 20- and 50-turn workflows. The reconnect
forces one resource resynchronization; unchanged resources remain body-free on
the other delta turns.}
\label{fig:agent-transport-scaling}
\end{figure}

\subsection{Selection sharing removes repeated routing work}

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.96\linewidth]{execution_policy_tradeoff.pdf}
\caption{Five-seed tiny-HF mechanism profile. Routing operations validate the
execution contract; wall time is host- and model-specific and is not a serving
speed claim.}
\label{fig:execution-policy}
\end{figure}

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\caption{Implemented policy points in the five-seed mechanism profile. Times
are means over random-weight tiny models and do not measure answer quality.}
\begin{tabular}{lrr}
\toprule
Condition & Routing operations & Generation ms\\
\midrule
Request/shared, last & 1.0 & \ExecutionRequestSharedMs{}\\
Request/per-layer & 3.0 & \ExecutionRequestPerLayerMs{}\\
Token/shared, first & \ExecutionTokenSharedRoutes{} & \ExecutionTokenSharedMs{}\\
Token/per-layer & \ExecutionTokenPerLayerRoutes{} & \ExecutionTokenPerLayerMs{}\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

Request/per-layer performs exactly three routing decisions, but amortizes them
over generation. Token/shared and token/per-layer observe the same average
number of autoregressive forwards; sharing reduces routing work by
\ExecutionRoutingReduction{}$\times$, from \ExecutionTokenPerLayerRoutes{} to
\ExecutionTokenSharedRoutes{} operations. Tiny-model wall times are effectively
tied for the two token modes, which means Python and model overhead dominate at
this scale. The routing-layer sweep also changed selected identities, confirming
that routing layer is a semantic parameter rather than a scheduling-only knob.
The stable selections within these short completions yield temporal Jaccard one;
longer trained-model studies are needed before making a churn or quality claim.

Device & Primitive & Batch 1 & Batch 4\\

CPU & indexed gather & \RuntimeCpuIndexBatchOneMs{} & \RuntimeCpuIndexBatchFourMs{}\\

\begin{figure}[H]

\caption{Portable primitive latency.  This older GPU does not make small
batch-one gathers faster than the CPU, but CUDA scales better at batch four.}
\end{figure}

The indexed CUDA path is \RuntimeCudaIndexBatchOneMs{} ms at batch one.  The
eight-interval pack is \RuntimeCudaPackBatchOneMs{} ms because it launches and
concatenates multiple slices.  At batch four, CUDA indexed throughput increases
to roughly \RuntimeCudaIndexBatchFourThroughput{} million selected tokens/s,
whereas the CPU reaches about \RuntimeCpuIndexBatchFourThroughput{} million
million.  These values identify packing and fragmentation as optimization
targets; they do not include routing or attention.

\subsection{Residency dominates the selected payload}

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\caption{Median CUDA interval materialization latency for an identical selected
payload.  Output size is 1 MiB at batch one and 4 MiB at batch four.}
\label{tab:hierarchy}
\begin{tabular}{lrr}
\toprule
Source residency & Batch 1 (ms) & Batch 4 (ms)\\
\midrule
HBM resident & \RuntimeCudaHBMMs{} & \RuntimeCudaHBMBatchFourMs{}\\
Pinned CPU $\rightarrow$ CUDA & \RuntimeCudaPinnedMs{} & \RuntimeCudaPinnedBatchFourMs{}\\
Ordinary CPU $\rightarrow$ CUDA & \RuntimeCudaOrdinaryMs{} & \RuntimeCudaOrdinaryBatchFourMs{}\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=.82\linewidth]{memory_hierarchy_latency.pdf}
\caption{The same logical selection has different physical cost depending on
residency.  Pinned transfer helps, but does not erase the hierarchy boundary.}
\end{figure}

At batch one, pinned transfer is \RuntimePinnedRatio{} times the HBM-resident
cost, and ordinary CPU transfer is \RuntimeOrdinaryRatio{} times that cost.
This is the central result of the initial systems gate.  A configuration that
moves inactive K/V off accelerator reduces residency, but request latency then
depends on locality, cache reuse, and transfer overlap.  Any future headline
must report both memory and latency.

\subsection{Physical layout is secondary to fragmented remapping}

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=.88\linewidth]{layout_gather_latency.pdf}
\caption{Warm materialization from four physically distinct contiguous stores.
Every layout restores the same logical per-layer K/V before attention.}
\end{figure}

Layer-major, reference-major, chunk-major, and block-major stores place the same
four-reference, two-layer native K/V in different contiguous orders.  A compact
placement index maps URI, layer, and logical page offsets back to physical
ranges.  All outputs match the unpacked reference exactly.  On CUDA, the
slowest and fastest medians differ by only \RuntimeLayoutSpreadBatchOne{} at
batch one and \RuntimeLayoutSpreadBatchFour{} at batch four.  The nominal
fastest layouts are \RuntimeLayoutFastestBatchOne{} and
\RuntimeLayoutFastestBatchFour{}, respectively, but those small differences do
not justify a universal preference on this synthetic cohort.

More importantly, all layout-remap paths take roughly 1.4--1.7 ms on CUDA,
several times the single-source interval pack.  They reconstruct selected
logical ranges across Python placement records and page fragments.  The next
optimization target is therefore indexed vectorized remapping and fewer
launches, not a claim that one physical ordering has won.

\subsection{Compilation is a negative capability gate; fallback is explicit}

The \texttt{torch.compile} API is present, but neither local compiled path is
valid.  CPU Inductor cannot find the Microsoft C/C++ compiler.  CUDA compilation
rejects compute capability 5.0 because the Triton backend requires capability
7.0 or newer.  No warm compiled latency is reported and no eager result is
relabeled as compiled.  Triton and custom CUDA therefore remain untested on
this host.  The request path now detects that condition before attention and
uses a bounded two-pass segmented softmax over the canonical intervals rather
than failing after source prefill or packing them into a dense K/V tensor.
On a real four-turn mini-swe-agent history with Qwen2.5-0.5B FP16, that fallback
selects 2,467 of 2,713 source tokens at their original positions, re-encodes
zero selected-history tokens, copies zero K/V bytes, and is matched-consumer
logit exact (maximum delta 0.0).  It reports 24 streaming layer calls and
3,096,576 logical attention-temporary bytes.  This is a correctness and
capability-routing smoke, not fused-kernel or latency evidence; the artifact is
\path{shared/results/paper4_5_runtime_productization/coding_agents/engine_gates/hf_qwen25_05b_streaming_fallback_smoke_20260919.json}.

\subsection{Earlier profiles locate larger costs}

The new primitive measurements explain the cost of moving an already selected
payload.  Earlier end-to-end artifacts provide the complementary scale: how
much time was spent before and after materialization when the public model API
and authenticated resource lifecycle were exercised.

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=.78\linewidth]{inherited_end_to_end_profile.pdf}
\caption{Inherited Paper 2 Qwen3-0.6B profile over four examples.  The log scale
shows that routing time is small relative to native reference encoding and
generation on that protocol.}
\end{figure}

The inherited profile reports \InheritedRoutingMs{} ms mean routing time,
\InheritedIngestSeconds{} s mean reference ingest, and
\InheritedGenerateSeconds{} s mean routed generation wall time.  Mean evidence
recall is 0.667 and materialized detail K/V is 0.066 of the candidate total.
This supports optimizing cache reuse and encoding amortization before replacing
the router with a custom kernel.  The cohort contains only four examples, so it
does not establish a general workload distribution.

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=.68\linewidth]{cold_warm_hot_profile.pdf}
\caption{Inherited Paper 4 lifecycle proxy over eight examples.  Cold includes
fetch and native encoding; warm reuses native encoding; hot reuses selected K/V.}
\end{figure}

The lifecycle TTFT proxy falls from \LifecycleColdMs{} ms cold to
\LifecycleWarmMs{} ms warm and \LifecycleHotMs{} ms hot.  Required-document
recall is 0.875.  The result validates state transitions and shows the size of
the reuse opportunity, but it is not an LM answer-quality or production-serving
measurement.

\subsection{Semantic storage lifecycle}

A 120-turn mixed trace compares plain LRU, size-aware LRU, and weighted
task/type-aware retention at the same 80 KB WARM quota. Weighted retention
raises the utility-weighted hit rate from 0.700 to 0.796, preserves every
long-running task and shared-document access, and reduces SOURCE
reconstructions from 214 to 184. Its estimated context-resolution cost falls
from 1,170 to 936 ms. These are policy-trace results; the extra Python scoring
work is not presented as engine serving speed.

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=.76\linewidth]{storage_policy_trace/storage_policy_trace.pdf}
\caption{Equal-quota retention on a mixed task, document, and tool-result
trace. Weighted policy protects active task state and shared documents while
allowing reconstructable transient records to churn.}
\label{fig:storage-policy-trace}
\end{figure}

The runtime now makes the cache hierarchy a shared semantic contract rather
than an engine-specific accident. Each typed record has an authoritative
SOURCE representation and may have derived native detail in COLD, WARM, or HOT
form. HOT means attention-ready and request-pinnable; WARM means lossless native
detail suitable for fast promotion; COLD means capacity-oriented persistence
with lossless compression and optional, separately named K/V quantization.
The physical HOT object remains an engine concern---vLLM pages, an independent
SGLang object, or MLX arrays---but record type, task status, dependencies,
reconstructability, grace periods, and quotas are decided once by
\texttt{PRAStorageManager}.

The deterministic policy retains plain LRU, size-aware LRU, reuse-count, and
reload-cost controls, and adds weighted LRU. Its score combines recency,
frequency, reconstruction cost, typed-record prior, task state, authorized
sharing, dependency count, byte cost, and reconstructability. Open, blocked,
or waiting tasks enforce minimum WARM lifetimes. Completion schedules delayed
compaction rather than deletion; an open dependent task cancels aggressive
compaction. Session close releases transient native detail without treating a
native-cache quota as permission to delete SOURCE. Newly encoded detail first
observes a grace period, avoiding speculative persistence of short-lived tool
and terminal records.

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\small
\caption{Shared lifecycle controls over HF, vLLM, SGLang, and MLX labels with
five seeds each (20 runs). Rates are policy outcomes under a fixed synthetic
pressure trace, not end-task model quality.}
\input{../shared/results/paper4_5_runtime/storage_lifecycle/generated_storage_lifecycle_table.tex}
\label{tab:semantic-storage-lifecycle}
\end{table}

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=.82\linewidth]{../shared/results/paper4_5_runtime/storage_lifecycle/storage_lifecycle_tradeoffs.pdf}
\caption{Engine-neutral retention controls and generic symmetric-int8 byte
transport. The int8 result is a codec diagnostic; model-specific quality and
active-attention bytes remain separate gates.}
\label{fig:semantic-storage-lifecycle}
\end{figure}

Lossless HOT$\rightarrow$WARM$\rightarrow$COLD$\rightarrow$HOT recovery and
SOURCE reconstruction are exact in 20/20 runs. Under a 24-byte pressure
fixture, plain LRU loses the shared document in every run whereas the weighted
policy retains it in 20/20; active-task and downstream-dependency protections
also hold in 20/20. A persistence grace period reduces writes for the transient
closure trace from 20 to zero. Generic symmetric int8 occupies 0.25 of FP32
bytes with 0.0103 RMSE, 0.28 ms median quantization, and 0.07 ms median
dequantization on this host. This does not supersede the MLX model-specific
int8 quality result, nor show that WARM and COLD improve generation latency.
It establishes policy parity, exact lossless recovery, and a measurable reason
to defer transient writes; natural lifecycle distributions remain open, while
the following small probe begins engine-native promotion measurement.

\paragraph{Historical pre-gate engine evidence.}
The engine measurements in the remainder of this subsection predate the
corrected live-history, original-position, copy-accounting, and lifecycle
contract and do not qualify current exactness or economics.

The next probe replaces the in-memory HOT bridge with live attention objects:
reserved vLLM-Metal pages, SGLang MLX arrays backed by the built-in HiCache
file API, and MLX arrays. Each of three natural QASPER selections is generated
from HOT, demoted to lossless WARM, promoted and generated again, moved through
int8 COLD, and finally recovered by a newly constructed lifecycle manager.
MLX-format WARM storage is layer segmented: each K and V array has an atomic
manifest entry and checksum, and a consumer profile can mmap only requested
layers. HOT promotion retains the lower durable copy, so a process failure
during an active request does not erase the recoverable representation.

\centering
\small
\caption{Three-example live-engine storage mechanism probe. Ratios include
request-time promotion and generation but exclude background demotion and COLD
conversion. Prefix is the mean common HOT/int8 output prefix in tokens.}
\resizebox{\linewidth}{!}{%
\input{../shared/results/live_storage_lifecycle/generated_live_storage_lifecycle_table.tex}}

\begin{figure}[H]

WARM output is exact in 9/9 engine--example pairs, and restart promotion
succeeds for every engine. Median WARM/HOT request ratios are 1.97 for MLX,
1.90 for SGLang, and 1.96 for vLLM; the corresponding COLD/HOT ratios are
1.84, 1.89, and 2.00. Median background WARM transitions cost 348, 158, and
362 ms, while COLD conversion costs 940, 701, and 1,016 ms. These are small
mechanism cohorts, not tail-latency estimates. Int8 COLD is 0/9 sequence-exact,
with first-token parity in 6/9 and short mean common prefixes. The signed F1
changes are noisy on three low-accuracy examples and do not rescue sequence
equivalence. Named product profiles therefore keep lossless COLD as the
default; int8 remains explicit, model-calibrated, and disabled unless its own
quality gate closes.

We then scale the same live protocol to 20 examples per cohort: QASPER,
HotpotQA, and 2WikiMultiHopQA at Qwen3-0.6B, plus QASPER at Qwen3-1.7B, for
each engine. Five smaller QASPER cohorts add Llama-3.2-1B to all three engines
and Gemma-3-1B to vLLM-Metal and MLX-LM. Lossless WARM reproduces HOT generation
in 265/265 pairs and all 17 managers recover after restart. Diagnostic int8
COLD is exact in only 61/265 pairs. The pinned SGLang-MLX build rejects Gemma's
mixed global/sliding-window topology before PRA attachment; this is a backend
compatibility boundary, not a PRA parity failure. Table~\ref{tab:live-storage-scaling}
keeps model and dataset identities separate; its percentiles describe finite
cohorts rather than an online arrival distribution.

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\scriptsize
\caption{Expanded live lifecycle matrix. Latencies include request-time
promotion and generation; background demotion/conversion remains separate.}
