Consequently, Paper~4.5 promotes no reduced-history production default, but it
does admit M2/P1 to native-engine qualification as a named research treatment.
The three exported fixtures contain 7, 6, and 9 exact requests and are bound to
their prefix, trajectory, selected-message, resource, and policy digests.  The
same file must be consumed unchanged by every engine; Paper~4.5 cannot rerun or
approximate the selector.  A paired replay bundle separately freezes every
full logical request, generation setting, and recorded assistant response.  A
common mapper converts the selected message identities into original-position
historical K/V intervals while leaving the current causal tail on the ordinary
wire.  The first tokenizer gate applies that mapper to all 22 requests with
Qwen3-4B: every geometry is valid, and mean realized retention is 88.21\%,
46.89\%, and 49.67\% for Tasks~3, 4, and 5, respectively.  These values confirm
logical opportunity in engine tokens, not native-K/V correctness or savings.
Every execution row must preserve the selected-message digest and
report official resolution, calls, materialized tokens, selected-history
re-encoding, K/V-copy bytes, and consumer temporary bytes separately.  A
progress-spine plan must additionally preserve the natural user instruction
and terminal turn of every retained epoch; otherwise deleting the envelope can
create an invalid system-to-assistant chat transition.  Detailed task rows,
stop diagnostics, hashes, and curves remain in the Paper~8.5 appendix and
machine-readable bundle rather than being duplicated here.

The request-level rows in the following mechanism audit predate the final
autonomous receipt-position and trial-start contract.  They remain useful for
isolating page rounding, copy paths, and lifecycle behavior, but their savings
are stale cross-engine diagnostics and are not pooled into the current product
or agent-quality claims.  Every engine must rerun the frozen autonomous
\textsc{Full}, PRA-100, and E2+F1C sequence before promotion.

The first historical cross-engine same-plan row fixes Task~4 request~5 and consumes it
unchanged through HF/CUDA, MLX, SGLang-MLX, and vLLM-Metal.  HF, MLX, and
SGLang each select 8,617/18,110 historical K/V tokens and append the same
117-token wire suffix, omitting 52.42\% of resident history K/V and saving
51.77\% of total visible tokens.  All three are same-subset token and logit
exact, re-encode zero selected-history tokens, and perform no selected-K/V or
interval-pack copy.  vLLM selects 8,624/18,096 complete-page source tokens and
appends 131 suffix tokens: page rounding adds 21 tokens, reducing historical
K/V saving to 52.34\% and total visible saving to 51.62\%.  It remains token
exact and attaches existing pages without copying.  Thus engine granularity
changes this request's realized saving by only 0.16 percentage point; it does
not change the frozen logical decision.  The HF row also clears ownership,
cancellation, stale-generation, offload/restore, and termination checks on an
RTX CUDA host.  These are request-level mechanism results, not autonomous
task-accuracy evidence.

This M2/P1 row is an \emph{earlier-policy mechanism control}, not the current
Paper~8.5 agent-policy result.  In particular, it contains no model-visible
compact closure receipts and its approximately 52\% saving must not be carried
forward as E2+F1C evidence.  The first receipt-aware E2+F1C executions fix
request~9 of the successful Scikit trajectory.  HF/CUDA, direct MLX, and
SGLang-MLX all
select 17,978 resident original-position K/V tokens from a 29,067-token source,
encode 664 compact-receipt tokens at their original logical positions, and
append the 305-token active suffix.  Charging those receipts gives 35.87\%
history saving and 35.49\% total-visible-token saving (38.15\% resident-only
K/V omission), with zero selected-K/V copy, exact same-subset logits and next
token, and passing lifecycle gates.  MLX and SGLang limit the first-layer
disjoint-attention peak to 172,499 bytes, 0.234\% of the 73,637,888-byte
selected-layer K/V extent.  The SGLang row further verifies genuine
offload: the source owner is removed without returning its cache to the
engine's reusable pool, and restore recovers the exact selected K/V.  These
remain request-level mechanism results, not autonomous agent solves.  The
receipt-aware vLLM/CUDA execution now causally encodes all six old-position
receipts, page-rounds 17,978 logical selected-history tokens to 18,016 resident
tokens, and moves five active-tail tokens into the terminal source page.  It
measures 35.75\% historical saving after receipts and 35.38\% total-visible
saving, only 0.11 percentage point below the interval-addressed engines.  Two
final requests both emit token IDs [151667, 198], matching HF and SGLang; the
first token also matches MLX, whose frozen run generated one continuation token.
Original selected history is neither re-encoded nor copied, and all sources
and aliases are released.  Receipt construction is presently a correctness
path, not a runtime result: 76.15 MB D2H capture, 239.93 MB host packing,
240.27 MB H2D reload, and 12.85 MB receipt-page copy-on-write total 569.20 MB
of explicitly charged materialization traffic.  The older M2/P1 rows are not
proxies for these E2+F1C results.

The corrected llama.cpp/Metal path now consumes the same frozen request with a
Qwen3-14B-Q4\_K\_M model.  It retains the same 17,978 original-position K/V
tokens, encodes the same 664 receipt tokens, and appends the same 305-token
suffix, yielding 35.87\% history saving after receipts, 35.49\%
total-visible-token saving, and 38.15\% resident-original-K/V omission.  Two
executions separated by request-slot erasure both emit token IDs
[151667, 198], with zero selected-history re-encoding, zero physical K/V copy,
preserved source positions, and empty source/request slots after teardown.
This qualification exposed and corrected two engine-level hazards that a
small contiguous smoke missed: attaching the final resident tail before an
earlier receipt causes a backward-position failure, while requiring every new
batch to begin at the immediately adjacent position rejects legitimate sparse
gaps.  The corrected path interleaves range attachment and receipt evaluation
causally and permits forward position jumps while continuing to reject
overlap, reversal, and non-contiguous positions within a batch.  The host was
heavily interrupted during source priming, so these timings are deliberately
excluded from runtime claims.  As with the other engines, this is a
request-level mechanism result rather than an autonomous agent solve.

A model-matched replication replaces the Qwen3 tokenizer proxy with the
Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B-Instruct tokenizer and runs the same request on the
Qwen3-Coder-30B Q4\_K\_M checkpoint used by the Paper~8.5 autonomous study.
The selected original-position K/V remains 17,978/29,067 tokens, but the six
role-aware closure receipts occupy 96 rather than 664 tokens under the correct
chat template; the active suffix remains 305 tokens.  The resulting history
saving after receipts is 37.82\%, total-visible-token saving is 37.43\%, and
resident-original-K/V omission is 38.15\%.  Two executions emit the identical
token IDs [3617, 46], re-encode zero selected-history tokens, copy zero
selected K/V bytes, preserve source positions, and leave both slots empty
after teardown.  The engine is pinned to revision
\texttt{e6e5d63dbc09b5ea6153822e39057f9b7de40eb0}.  This removes a tokenizer
confound from the engine handoff, but remains a frozen request-level mechanism
result; its non-isolated 181.6-second source capture and approximately
4-second sparse generations are provenance, not performance claims.

The first real MLX frozen-plan check now qualifies one Task~4 request at both
full and reduced retention on Qwen3-0.6B-4bit.  The reduced row retains
8,617/18,110 historical K/V tokens across 23 disjoint original-position
segments (47.92\% total visible retention), re-encodes zero selected-history
tokens, packs or copies zero selected K/V bytes, and matches the identical-
subset dense oracle with zero maximum logit delta and the same next token.
Its first-layer consumer peak increases by 148,061 bytes, only 0.42\% of the
35,295,232-byte selected-layer K/V extent.  The matched full-retention row is
also exact and clears the same lifecycle gate.  These are request-level engine
results, not an autonomous Task~4 solve or a three-task policy replication.
A second reduced request from Task~5 also qualifies: it selects
11,988/23,077 historical K/V tokens across 34 segments (52.35\% retention),
has zero oracle logit delta and zero re-encoding or copy, and limits the
first-layer peak delta to 151,526 bytes (0.31\% of selected-layer K/V).  This
extends same-subset correctness to a second task identity.  A Task~3 request
also qualifies at 88.82\% retention, with 14,154/15,954 historical K/V tokens,
zero oracle logit delta, and a 153,916-byte first-layer peak delta (0.27\% of
selected-layer K/V).  The strict reduced-history smoke therefore covers one
request from each of the three frozen policy tasks, but Task~4 is still the
only matched full-retention control.

This smoke also exposes a material kernel-dispatch constraint.  With 16-token
wire-prefill chunks, two earlier rows remain next-token exact but differ from
MLX's dense prefill kernel by maximum logit deltas of 1.000 and 1.125, above
the predeclared 0.005 gate.  Fast-exponential and explicit dtype-rounding
changes do not repair the mismatch.  Token-at-a-time wire prefill makes the
dense and sparse paths use a compatible vector reduction and yields zero
delta.  We therefore retain step one as a correctness mode and make no speed
claim; a matching fused multi-token consumer remains required for economics.

SGLang-MLX clears the same reduced Task~4 request as a request-level lifecycle
row at 47.92\% retention: same-consumer logit delta, selected-history
re-encoding, and interval-pack copy are all zero, and the 157,811-byte
first-layer peak delta is 0.45\% of selected-layer K/V.  Its corrected 100\%
control also passes against an independent ordinary resident-prefix cache:
both arms append the same 117-token tail, emit the same two tokens, and have
zero maximum logit delta, while PRA-100 re-encodes and packs zero selected-
history bytes and clears every lifecycle check.  An earlier fresh-whole-prompt
comparator produced the same tokens but a 0.9365 logit delta.  That row is
retained as superseded evidence because it changed cache-consumption mode and
therefore compared different prefill-kernel dispatches, not PRA-100 against
the no-PRA prefix-cache baseline.  These are request-level results; they do
not yet establish autonomous task parity or runtime benefit.

vLLM-Metal 0.29.0 now clears all six frozen Task~4 requests on the 48~GB M4
Pro.  Every PRA-100 request matches an independent ordinary prefix-cache
control with a complete source-prefix hit.  Every M2/P1 request matches its
identical-subset reference.  Weighted total visible retention is 47.04\%, or
52.96\% logical saving; per-request retention ranges from 45.38\% to 48.48\%.
Complete-page alignment adds 126 tokens in total, exactly 21 per request.
Both six-request cells re-encode zero selected-history tokens, use zero
selection-pack bytes, and attach existing pages without a physical K/V copy.
The lifecycle rows also add zero active bytes when two requests borrow the
source concurrently.  The separately reported offload/restore exercise does
copy K/V across the residency boundary and is not counted as request reuse.
This is a complete frozen request sequence, but not yet an autonomous Task~4
solve; it establishes engine realization of the policy rather than policy
accuracy.

The current campaign freezes one end-to-end task per qualified engine before
any Easy-14 expansion.  Its first post-fix row is llama.cpp with
Qwen3-Coder-30B Q4 on \texttt{scikit-learn\_\_scikit-learn-14496}, episode six
of the locked Paper~8.5 independent-task session.  Every run starts from empty
wrapper and engine-session state at temperature zero.  Two ordinary
\textsc{Full} controls resolve in eight calls and have identical request,
assistant-content, and shell-action hashes.  PRA-100 also resolves in eight
calls and matches both controls exactly.  Its first request evaluates the
canonical 26,370-token source once; seven later requests use resident K/V with
zero selected-history re-encoding, physical K/V copy, total K/V copy, or H2D
transfer.  This is an agent-level semantic no-op, not merely final-patch parity.

The frozen prompt-pinned E2+F1C policy also resolves in eight calls.  It
materializes 128,380 versus 213,524 message-content tokens, a 39.88\%
reduction.  Across the seven post-bootstrap requests, weighted resident-K/V
omission is 40.23\%: 113,643 selected K/V tokens versus an estimated 190,141
under full retention.  The 0.35-point difference from logical saving is due to
chat-template and mandatory-current-state accounting.  Retained-original-history
re-encoding and selected-K/V-copy bytes both remain zero, but the bundle does
not separately aggregate the model evaluation used for its compact closure
receipts.  The arm is therefore hybrid native plus transformed context and does
not pass the strict resident-subset invariant.  The first
two actions match \textsc{Full}; request three begins a different successful
path, and same-index actions reconverge on requests five through eight.
Completion tokens rise from 748 to 825.  The reduced arm therefore preserves
the official solve and call count without claiming an identical trajectory.

The all-call mean K/V omission is 35.22\% after charging the initial full
bootstrap.  Consumer temporary bytes remain unmeasured, not zero.  The
auditable bundle is
\path{shared/results/paper4_5_runtime_productization/agent_memory_plans/paper8_5_e2_f1c_scikit14496_v1/autonomous_llamacpp_qwen3coder30b_postfix_v1}.

\paragraph{Selection-only transfer exposed a cross-engine bootstrap error.}
A subsequent strict export from Paper~8.5 contains no compact receipts or
synthetic replacement text.  Auditing its first autonomous request found that
all five campaign adapters had treated source capture as a model-visible
\textsc{Full} request: llama.cpp, HF, MLX, SGLang, and vLLM silently deferred
the requested selection until request two.  Such a run does not test the
frozen policy on the first action and can permanently alter an autonomous
trajectory.  The corrected contract separates source population from
consumption.  The complete canonical transcript is evaluated once into an
owned cache, but request one immediately consumes its declared resident
subset.  llama.cpp, HF, MLX, and SGLang perform a non-generating capture.
vLLM's public generation API has no prefill-only operation in the pinned
version, so its capture samples and discards one hidden token; that token is
neither returned nor committed to agent history and is reported separately as
bootstrap overhead.

The corrected llama.cpp control uses Qwen3-Coder-30B Q4 on
\texttt{django\_\_django-15741} with the same locked five-episode prefix.
Plain full-history execution resolves in ten calls.  Native PRA-100 resolves
in eight calls with zero selected-history re-encoding and zero selected-K/V
copy.  The first three shell commands and first two complete assistant
responses match, after which the successful trajectories differ; PRA-100
therefore passes official utility and physical-accounting gates but not exact
action-trajectory parity.  This difference must be investigated as a
prefix-cache versus fresh-prefill numerical/dispatch effect before claiming a
deterministic semantic no-op for this identity.

The strict original-position policy omits 36.53\% of cumulative logical
history and still re-encodes and copies zero selected K/V, but it fails the
official task after twenty calls.  The same policy had succeeded when Paper~8.5
presented selected records as compact ordinary text.  Thus compact-text policy
qualification does not automatically transfer to sparse original-position
K/V consumption: positional presentation is part of the treatment.  This is
an admissible negative result and blocks promotion of this selection-only
policy as a Paper~4.5 default.

\midrule
