The evidence, trajectories, manifests, and per-request ledgers are under
\path{shared/results/paper4_5_runtime_productization/agent_memory_plans/paper8_5_selection_only_e2_django15741_v1}.  Diagnostic runs that generated
request one from full history, or attempted an empty selected suffix, are
quarantined and excluded from the table.

The corrected direct-MLX request-9 transfer remains below the autonomous
admission line.  MLX 0.32.2 / MLX-LM 0.31.3 and
\texttt{mlx-community/Qwen3-14B-4bit} revision
\texttt{a4d9b2df59d2c150bef02fcbe0d91046\allowbreak b7ca33a4} capture the canonical source
without a model-visible bootstrap completion and apply the declared subset on
request one.  The fused interval-addressed consumer then produces a maximum
logit difference of 0.5 against its independently packed, identical-subset
MLX reference, exceeding the predeclared 0.01 gate.  The request is rejected
before generation; it is not counted as either task success or failure.  A
16~GB-host attempt is separately quarantined because canonical capture drove
active swap to 23.5~GB.  The reported numerical negative is from the 48~GB
host, where memory pressure remained healthy.  Holding tokens, positions,
model, and reference fixed while changing only the fused consumer to the eager
multi-segment implementation produces the same 0.5 delta; candidate and
dense-reference first-token IDs both equal 3617.  The long-context discrepancy
is therefore not specific to the fused Metal kernel.  Tolerance relaxation is
not a valid substitute for a new, predeclared decision-equivalence protocol.

The bounded eager control sharpens the diagnosis without clearing the frozen
request.  With 154 source tokens, fused and eager disjoint consumers each have
0.0 logit delta against a physically packed reference consumed by the same
implementation.  Against MLX's native dense consumer, however, the maximum
deltas are 1.375 and 1.1611328125.  Both strict runs preserve all four
generated tokens; a separately labeled fused 16-token diagnostic also
preserves 16/16.  Its relaxed diagnostic tolerance is deliberately excluded
from qualification.  The result separates exact selection/interval addressing
from unresolved cross-consumer numerical equivalence.  The existing numerical
protocol remains a strict negative; any deterministic decision-equivalence
protocol must be declared separately and must compare every generated token,
not reinterpret this failed gate after the fact.

The first corrected direct-MLX hybrid transfer uses the same task and frozen
five-episode prefix with Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B-Instruct 4-bit.  PRA-100 resolves
in nine calls.  E2+F1C resolves in twelve calls; all eleven post-bootstrap
sparse requests pass the positioned same-subset gate with maximum logit delta
0.0.  Retained-original-history re-encoding and selected-K/V copy are both zero.  The
sparse calls expose 302,031 full source K/V tokens and consume 180,052 selected
resident tokens plus 1,056 encoded closure-receipt tokens, giving 40.04\%
steady-state omission.  Charging the 26,367-token source bootstrap gives
36.82\% all-call omission.

Those 1,056 closure-receipt tokens are post-cache transformed context.  They
are correctly charged in workload accounting, but their evaluation disqualifies
this arm from strict resident-subset execution despite zero retained-original
re-encoding and zero selected-K/V copy.

The extra calls materially change the economic result.  Against the nine-call
PRA-100 control, E2+F1C saves 19.33\% of materialized message-content input,
19.19\% after completion tokens, and 15.23\% of selected-K/V plus receipt
workload.  The first three shell commands match.  At command four the reduced
arm performs an additional source read and later spends two calls recovering
from an incorrectly escaped \texttt{sed} substitution.  Because the active
task statement and every active-task causal turn remain selected, this is a
prior-session behavioral-priming effect, not current-task evidence loss or a
sparse-engine correctness failure.  The auditable bundle is
\path{shared/results/paper4_5_runtime_productization/agent_memory_plans/paper8_5_e2_f1c_scikit14496_v1/autonomous_mlx_qwen3coder30b_postfix_v1}.

A second cold execution reproduces all twelve request inputs, assistant
contents, shell commands, token totals, and zero-delta same-subset gates
exactly.  The three-call penalty is therefore repeatable on this identity.

A targeted diagnostic then enables one generic prior-mutation turn.  It still
resolves, but expands to 14 calls and materializes 238,527 tokens.  This saves
37.29\% against the treatment's own longer full-history counterfactual but only
0.17\% against PRA-100 message input; selected-K/V work is 3.12\% greater than
PRA-100 and 22.26\% greater than E2+F1C.  All 13 sparse requests remain exact
at logit delta 0.0 with zero selected-history re-encoding or copy.  The added
group is an unrelated Django finalization whose receipt reports only a
\texttt{patch.txt} write and whose displayed source diff is malformed.  This
rejects command-level generic mutation floors: a future floor must require an
authoritative source/workspace mutation receipt and a resource/DAG path to the
live frontier.

A frozen request-4 consumption control sharpens that conclusion.  With the
same selected-message digest materialized as packed ordinary text, two
repeated E2+F1C requests produce the same malformed, repetitive
\texttt{grep} pipeline, whereas two \textsc{Full} controls produce the same valid
action.  Yet every whole-epoch add-back (3/3), every individual causal group
from the preceding Scikit-learn issue (5/5), and even 82- and 118-token groups
from unrelated SymPy and Django issues restore a valid action.  This is a
non-specific prompt-conditioning effect, not evidence for a missing DAG edge
or mutation fact.  The native original-position E2+F1C request remains valid
and the task resolves.  Thus selection identity alone does not imply
behavioral equivalence across packed text and sparse original-position K/V;
each runtime must execute the frozen policy through its actual consumer.  The
diagnostic is released under the MLX bundle's
\path{first_divergence_oracle} subdirectory and is excluded from utility and
speed claims.

A post-fix autonomous SGLang-MLX row now separates mechanism correctness from
fresh-prefill trajectory identity.  On Scikit-learn-14496 with
Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B-Instruct 4-bit, ordinary fresh-prefill \textsc{Full} and
native live-K/V PRA-100 both resolve 1/1, use seven calls, retain the same
operation class on every aligned call, and submit a byte-identical patch.  The
PRA arm bootstraps 26,370 source tokens once, then records six native-reuse
calls with zero selected-history re-encoding, selected-K/V copy, host-to-device
bytes, or suffix-graft bytes.  Replacing the former second-full-cache and
suffix-graft path reduces its measured steady-state consumer temporary peak
from roughly 3.2--3.6\,GB per call (and a call-7 Metal OOM) to at most
41,559,048 bytes; the distinct bootstrap peak is 5,439,660,158 bytes.

The two arms nevertheless first diverge at action three.  A stopped
three-request diagnostic verifies exact equality between the live source token
IDs and fresh text retokenization through that divergence.  This excludes
selection, omitted records, and retokenization as causes and localizes the
difference to numerical sensitivity between fresh full prefill and incremental
prefix-cache continuation.  Consequently this row qualifies official success,
call count, final patch, and zero-copy/no-re-encoding mechanics, but not the
strict fresh-prefill exact-action-trajectory gate.

A second, selection-neutral live-prefix control resolves that ambiguity.  It
uses the same incremental consumer as PRA-100 while retaining every record.
All seven request-input digests, assistant-content digests, and command digests
match PRA-100 exactly; it also resolves 1/1 in seven calls and submits the same
patch.  Thus the matched-consumption gate is positive even though the
fresh-prefill gate remains negative.  Reduced-policy execution is permitted;
its outcome must be attributed to selection only against this matched control,
not against the numerically different fresh-prefill trajectory.  The auditable
bundle is
\path{shared/results/paper4_5_runtime_productization/coding_agents/engine_agent_gates/sglang_scikit14496_qwen3coder30b_postfix_v1}.

The admitted E2+F1C arm then executes the frozen reduced policy through the
same native consumer.  It resolves 1/1 and submits the same byte-identical
patch, while all 14 post-bootstrap requests report zero retained-original-
history re-encoding, zero selected-K/V copy, and zero host-to-device transfer.
Each request nevertheless evaluates 96 compact closure tokens at old logical
positions: 1,344 post-cache transformed-context tokens in total.  This makes the
arm a hybrid diagnostic, not strict resident-subset PRA.  Across
its realized 15-call trajectory it saves 35.07\% of selected message-content
tokens, 39.21\% after compact closure materialization, and 37.21\% of exposed
source K/V tokens.  This is an accuracy-preserving point but fails the
efficiency gate: the matched full-history control needs seven calls, commands
first diverge at request four, and the reduced arm's 39.11\% within-trajectory
input-plus-completion saving becomes a 33.10\% paired workload increase after
charging the extra calls.  The earlier call-eight Metal OOM does not recur over
all 15 requests after preventing evaluator-forced realization of source-backed
K/V slices and clearing request-local MLX graph/cache state at teardown; this
combined intervention does not identify either change as the sole cause.
However, steady-state consumer peak remains roughly 3.0--3.4\,GiB, so neither
latency nor low-temporary-memory qualification follows.  Oracle add-back at
request four, rather than Easy-14 expansion, is the next policy experiment.

Trace structure already rules out one tempting explanation.  At request four
E2+F1C retains the active task statement and all three completed active-task
turns, plus the two most recent completed episodes in full.  Its 35 retired
groups belong only to three older completed episodes whose principal files are
disjoint from \path{/testbed/sklearn/cluster/optics_.py}.  The extra calls
therefore do not demonstrate loss of the active decision/progress spine.
Add-back must distinguish useful cross-task workflow exemplars from numerical
or consumer-path sensitivity; exact recovery after restoring unrelated old
state would not make that state semantically mandatory.

No corresponding post-fix autonomous HF row exists yet; older HF request and
agent rows cannot be pooled with these results.

Earlier task-02--04 utility rows predate this cross-engine freeze and remain
diagnostic evidence for retention-policy design; they do not authorize an
Easy-14 matrix.  In particular, the task-03/04 counterfactuals below still
localize important omitted records, but the active scheduler will not add task
instances until every target engine completes the same plain, PRA-100, then
PRA-90 sequence on an independently admissible frozen task.

The first MLX autonomous admission attempt illustrates why model/task and
template admission precede PRA attribution.  Qwen3-14B 4-bit on task 01 reaches
the fixed 50-call limit and submits an empty patch (0/1 official solves), so no
PRA-100 or PRA-90 arm is run and the row is classified as an inadmissible
model/task pair, not a PRA failure.  Its frozen no-thinking template is checked
separately: the first-turn bytes and token IDs exactly match the stock
no-thinking template, while completed assistant records retain the same empty
thinking wrapper so the next rendered prompt contains the complete earlier
prompt as a byte and token prefix.  The stock template fails that two-turn
prefix test.  Qwen3.5-27B is also excluded from this gate, rather than counted
as a failed solve, because three of every four layers use recurrent
GatedDeltaNet state instead of a token-addressable K/V history; the qualified
interval-selection contract does not apply to that state.

The first vLLM CUDA autonomous admission similarly stops before PRA.  On the
8~GB RTX 5060, Qwen2.5-Coder-7B AWQ requires 2~GB of CPU weight offload,
eager execution, and a 16K context to leave 2.66~GiB for K/V.  After two
separately recorded agent/parser configuration controls, the matched
text-command configuration executes 19 valid agent actions in 470.6~s but
makes no source mutation.  The run is terminated at its bounded no-progress
cutoff without submission or official grading.  Retention, K/V-copy,
re-encoding, and consumer-temporary fields therefore remain null: no PRA
request was issued.  A stronger 14B AWQ admission on the same task does clear
server startup at 16K, but only by CPU-offloading 6.39B parameters.  Decode then
falls to 0.4--0.5 tokens/s: two actions require 602.4~s, make no mutation, and a
third generation is cancelled at the bounded throughput cutoff.  This second
row is hardware-throughput inadmissible rather than a model-quality or PRA
failure.  The missing stateful OpenAI serving bridge is now implemented: it
maps an agent session onto the scheduler-owned page-alias primitive, evaluates
only the suffix, rolls canonical generations forward by sharing old pages plus
new complete suffix pages, and fails closed unless prepare, commit, hit, and
release receipts are observed.  Its contract/scheduler tests pass, and a real
Qwen2.5-Coder-1.5B/vLLM~0.28 CUDA attempt exposes two integration defects: a
stale remote worker ignored scheduler-alias metadata and attempted file-backed
K/V loading, while source-generation commit referenced a scheduler manager
bound only in the store branch.  Both paths are corrected and regression-
tested.  Two post-fix retries nevertheless lose the RTX host/WSL before
producing a canonical artifact.  A later isolated run exposes a third identity
defect: vLLM randomizes the scheduler request ID as
\texttt{external-random8}, while \texttt{LLM.generate} returns only the
external ID, so the driver could not find the already-emitted generation-commit
receipt.  The corrected resolver accepts an exact internal ID or a unique
randomized child and fails closed on ambiguity.

The resulting strictly PRA-100-only gate now completes all seven frozen
Task~02 turns on Qwen2.5-Coder-1.5B, vLLM~0.28, and the 8~GB RTX~5060.  Each
eight-token continuation is token-exact to a fresh dense full-history request
(7/7 pairs); resident source size grows from 3,104 to 4,208 complete-page
tokens and realized retention is 1.0 on every turn.  The six stateful updates
each observe exactly one alias hit, prepare, commit, and release.  Selected-
history re-encoding, selected-history K/V copy, total physical K/V copy, and
host-to-device transfer are all zero; the measured request-temporary peak is
206,446,080 bytes.  Closing the session leaves zero sources, active aliases,
or pending commits.  This promotes the bounded stateful mechanism, not an
autonomous SWE-bench outcome: no 90\% arm was launched, and task treatment
remains gated on a plain-success model/task pair.  Qualification remains
bounded to in-process V1, one homogeneous K/V group, and complete pages; at
most one partial page is re-evaluated and reported separately.

A fresh vLLM~0.28 admission separates that old 16K capacity point from the
single-task operating point.  The server now exposes CPU offload explicitly
and advertises its actually enabled automatic prefix cache.  With 2~GiB
offload, a 256-token action still takes several minutes because weight traffic
crosses PCIe during decode.  With no offload, a 4,096-token, one-session server
fits at 0.86 GPU-memory utilization and completes the 15-call Task~13 plain run
plus grading in 16.1~s.  It nevertheless grades 0/1 with an empty patch: the 7B
model repeats the same invalid \texttt{grep} command rather than adding the
required end-of-options marker.  A Task~04 follow-up reaches five valid calls
before an SSH transport reset and is classified as infrastructure-invalid.
Thus these runs establish the fast no-offload operating point, but do not by
themselves admit a vLLM PRA arm.

A subsequent frozen Task~05 admission supplies the missing pair.  On
\texttt{pytest-dev\_\_pytest-7982}, Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct-AWQ under
vLLM~0.28.0 V1 uses an 8,192-token context, no CPU offload, temperature zero,
and the same append-stable renderer in both arms.  Plain and PRA-100 each
resolve 1/1 in 15 calls and submit the same one-line patch.  Their ordered
assistant text plus parsed shell actions are byte-exact, with action digest
\texttt{2f5ab573\ldots a3ab}; the patch and chat-template digests also match and
the frozen configuration diff is empty.  Full normalized transcripts are not
required to match because two user observations contain nondeterministic
package/test progress and timing text; the formal gate compares the complete
ordered assistant action trajectory and final patch.

The PRA-100 lifecycle contains one initial store and 14 dense semantic no-ops.
All 14 updates observe alias prepare, commit, hit, and release; realized total
and engine-reported history retention are 1.0 on every request.  Selected-
history re-encoding, selected-history and total physical K/V copy, interval
copy, and host-to-device transfer are all zero.  New-history partial pages
account for 165 separately reported re-evaluated tokens (77 maximum on one
request), which are not selected-history re-encoding.  The sum of reported
request-temporary allocations is 517,376,512 bytes and the largest request is
148,832,768 bytes.

The PRA-100 run also converts two previously hidden integration assumptions into
regressions.  The native readiness probe now supplies the live-agent history
projection and accepts engine-native consumption evidence rather than a
llama.cpp-specific stage name.  During source rollover, a committed successor
may release the superseded registry pin while deferred request aliases remain,
but only when that successor owns the exact complete old prefix; without such
a successor, eviction remains fail-closed.

The first PRA-90 attempt then exposed a page-granularity retention defect rather
than a model-quality result.  The proxy met its logical/token floor but selected
3,040 resident page tokens where the engine required 3,111.  The corrected
planner makes every logically selected record mandatory, rounds upward by the
most recent omitted complete-page causal groups, and fails closed if the
authoritative engine-token floor is still unmet.  It may therefore realize more
than the requested 90\%; it may never silently realize less.

The corrected frozen PRA-90 run is a valid negative.  It grades 0/1 after 15
calls, first diverges from plain at action 6, and submits an empty patch after
later diffing a generated test path rather than the modified source file.  It
materializes 21,940 of 23,281 estimated model-visible tokens (94.24\% aggregate;
90.27--100\% per request), while the engine reports 90.28--100\% retained
history K/V.  Selected-history re-encoding, physical and total K/V copy, and
H2D are all zero.  Consumer temporaries total 524,545,536 bytes with a
148,922,880-byte peak.  Thus the failure is attributable to the frozen
retention policy after an exact PRA-100 control, not to rematerialization or
page-copy behavior.  Easy-14 remains locked, and the same in-process V1,
homogeneous-group, complete-page boundary still applies.
The plain/PRA-100 controls record PRA revision \texttt{1feca189}; the corrected
PRA-90 arm records \texttt{3a37ed22}.  The receipt therefore supports the
post-fix policy-quality result and byte counters, but not a cross-arm timing
claim.

We then transferred Paper~8.5's matched causal-tail control without importing
its text-only observation-boundary trimming: native K/V selection admits only
whole causal records under the exact token ceiling.  This control is negative
on both qualified engines tested.  On MLX Task~01 it diverges at action~3 and
grades 0/1 after nine calls, versus the six-call plain/PRA-100 trajectory.  It
retains 89.57\% of logical context (10.43\% estimated saving), re-encodes and
copies zero selected-history K/V bytes, but accumulates 15.43~GB of lifecycle
K/V copies and 10.40~GB of consumer temporaries (2.29~GB peak), then submits a
94,017-byte patch rather than the 1,165-byte reference.  On vLLM Task~05 it
also diverges at action~3, repeatedly recreates the same symlink experiment,
and reaches the fixed 8,192-token context bound after 28 calls; the rejected
request contains 8,234 input tokens.  Official grading records 0/1 with an
empty patch.  Aggregate logical retention is 88.05\% (11.95\% estimated
saving), while selected-history re-encoding, physical K/V copy, total K/V copy,
and H2D remain zero; consumer temporaries total 903.8~MB with a 148.9~MB peak.
The reducer now treats this post-trajectory context exhaustion like the frozen
step/time limits---an explicitly labelled bounded task failure---rather than an
infrastructure-invalid row.

These transfers strengthen the ownership boundary rather than select a new
runtime default.  The earlier task-aware MLX arm solves in six calls at 97.81\%
retention, whereas its causal-tail transfer fails at a materially lower
retention; vLLM's two 90\% policies both fail, but the causal tail doubles the
call count before exhaustion.  Because those realized budgets are unmatched,
the receipts do not prove structural superiority of either selector.  They do
show that simple recency can discard the agent's progress spine even when the
native engine consumes the selected K/V exactly.  Paper~4.5 therefore retains
the task-aware progress-spine policy for subsequent engine work; matched-budget
oracle and exclusion-policy development remains in Paper~8.5.

Paper~8.5 subsequently qualified the same matched causal-tail policy on a
different autonomous stack.  OpenHands with Qwen3-Coder-30B preserves 3/3
official resolutions, reduces actions from 114 to 79, saves 22.49\% within the
candidate trajectories, and saves 46.86\% against paired \textsc{Full} input
(source commit \texttt{ad1152b9}).  This does not invalidate the MLX and vLLM
negatives above: it demonstrates agent/model/task dependence rather than a
universal selector.  It does, however, promote the policy from an unqualified
control to a cross-agent research candidate.  The Paper~4.5 import receipt
freezes a stricter contract: only whole-record logical selection transfers to
resident K/V.  Paper~8.5's extra ordinary-text slicing of oversized
observations is excluded unless an engine implements original-position
record-internal K/V spans; re-encoding a compact replacement cannot be counted
as reuse.  The 46.86\% paired figure includes trajectory contraction and is
therefore not an engine-local K/V or latency claim.

The direct SGLang-MLX Qwen3-Coder-30B bridge clears its real two-turn admission
smoke.  The model returns the frozen
``OK'' then ``DONE'' responses, the second prompt extends the resident source,
and both turns report zero selected-history re-encoding, zero selected-history
K/V copy, and zero host-to-device transfer.  Each turn separately reports a
3,342,336-byte device-side canonical suffix graft; the measured request peaks
are 805,306,376 and 402,653,192 bytes, with 144 fused-attention calls per turn.

The stronger task admission exposed two ordinary-generation defects before any
PRA treatment.  First, the server used only the tokenizer EOS identity and
omitted renderer/end-of-turn stop identities; the frozen contract now uses the
union $\{151643,151644,151645\}$.  Second, the pinned SGLang-MLX backend accepted
but ignored the requested repetition penalty.  The correction applies the
llama/Ollama sign-aware transform before token choice: positive seen logits are
divided by the penalty and non-positive seen logits are multiplied by it, with
duplicate history IDs transformed once.  For greedy decoding with $U$ unique
penalized IDs, the exact winner must lie in the original top $U+1$: at least one
candidate in that set is unpenalized and dominates everything below it.  The
implementation therefore gathers only that bounded candidate set, restores the
full-vocabulary lowest-ID tie rule, and retains a full-scatter fallback for
sampling, edit rows, and log-probability requests.  Unit tests cover stop-ID
composition, sign and zero handling, duplicate IDs, decode-history refresh,
ties, and 100 randomized property cases against a full-vocabulary reference;
the focused SGLang bridge suite passes 20/20 tests.  A real-model probe then
terminates naturally after a 17-token single-command response.  On a deliberately
repetitive 256-token greedy benchmark, however, exact top-$U+1$ processing takes
5.722~s versus 3.975~s without a penalty (44.74 versus 64.40 tokens/s), a 43.95\%
elapsed-time overhead; it is a correctness repair, not a speed result.

With both fixes frozen, the matched Qwen3-Coder-30B Task~02 plain arm completes
and grades 0/1.  It accepts one read-only source-location action, modifies no
file, and then produces three consecutive format-error responses; all four
responses reach the 4,096-token cap.  This is a model/scaffold admission failure,
not a PRA regression.  The predeclared plain-before-PRA gate therefore withholds
both PRA-100 and PRA-90.

A second frozen admission uses the dense Qwen2.5-Coder-14B MLX 4-bit family.
Its first real bridge request exposed a cross-family implementation defect:
the fused attention path assumed the Qwen3 \texttt{q\_norm}/\texttt{k\_norm}
modules, which Qwen2.5 does not define.  The corrected path applies these
normalizers only when the model exposes them and otherwise preserves Qwen2's
native unnormalized projections.  After that correction, a real two-turn
``OK''/``DONE'' bridge smoke passes with 61/61 second-turn history tokens and
zero selected-history re-encoding, selected-history K/V copy, or H2D transfer;
the separately reported canonical suffix graft is 6,684,672 bytes.  The same
frozen model/task pair nevertheless fails plain Task~02 admission: it submits
after nine valid actions and modifies one file, but its 443-byte patch inserts
an undefined \texttt{attr} check at function entry and the official grader
scores 0/1.  This is again a plain model/task failure, not a PRA result, so the
PRA-100 and PRA-90 arms remain withheld.

Task~01 provides the required plain-success pair for this SGLang engine.  Its
first admission attempt also identified a systems defect before producing a
scientific row: ordinary SGLang-MLX prefill submitted the complete 2K-token
agent prompt in one call and materialized prompt-wide vocabulary logits.  The
corrected executor evaluates non-final prompt chunks through the model trunk
without the logit head and requests logits only for the final chunk.  With the
same three stop identities as above, a frozen first-request replay completes in
19.54~s with 102 generated tokens and is byte-identical to the direct MLX
reference response.

The resulting full plain Task~01 run resolves 1/1 in six actions.  Its ordered
actions and submitted patch are exactly equal to the direct MLX control.
PRA-100 also resolves 1/1 in six actions with the same action digest and patch,
1.0 realized and engine-reported history-K/V retention on all six requests,
zero selected-history re-encoding, zero selected-history K/V copy, and zero
H2D transfer.  Canonical suffix graft is 92,798,976 bytes; consumer temporary
allocation is 4,156,645,776 bytes cumulatively with an 805,306,376-byte peak.
Wall time is 231.2~s, retained only as provenance because this single host run
is not an isolated timing experiment.

The first PRA-90 attempt is quarantined for a measurement-contract failure:
the proxy's whitespace estimator declared 1,530/1,649 visible tokens (92.78\%),
but the engine tokenizer found only 2,670/3,002 resident tokens (88.94\%), 32
below the exact 90\% floor.  The engine correctly rejected that request, while
the outer agent retried it through request 32.  The repaired path rounds up at
whole causal-group boundaries with the authoritative engine tokenizer and
never re-encodes the restored spans.  Its fresh run resolves 1/1 in six calls:
all commands and the submitted patch are byte-identical to plain, response text
is exact through call five, and call six changes only non-operative reasoning
wording.  Exact history retention is 100\% for calls one--four, 91.54\% for
call five, and 92.57\% for call six.  Across the run, selected-history
re-encoding, selected-K/V copy, and H2D transfer are all zero; canonical suffix
movement is 92,012,544 bytes, cumulative consumer temporary allocation is
4,156,531,664 bytes with an 805,306,376-byte peak, and the fused consumer makes
21,888 attention calls.  The single warm 70.1-s wall time is provenance, not a
speedup claim.  SGLang therefore clears plain, PRA-100, and PRA-90 on this
frozen autonomous task without authorizing expansion to another task.
The control manifests record engine revision \texttt{88a74f66}, whereas the
floor-corrected PRA-90 manifest records \texttt{e21788af}; this revision
discontinuity is another reason not to interpret the wall times economically.

The direct MLX no-gateway bridge is implemented under the same contract.
It owns a persistent canonical live cache per session, extends only an append-
stable suffix, treats PRA-100 as the full-context semantic no-op candidate, and
serves PRA-90 through disjoint intervals with original positions.  Every sparse
request must also clear a dense same-subset reference gate at maximum logit
delta 0.005.  Request borrowing, exact-once release, and session termination
are contract-tested, while interval packing, dense-reference packing,
canonical graft/reallocation, H2D traffic, and consumer temporary bytes are
reported separately.  The Qwen2.5-Coder-14B 4-bit / Task~01 pair clears plain,
PRA-100, and PRA-90 admission on the 48~GB M4 Pro under one frozen package
identity: MLX 0.32.1, MLX-LM 0.32.0, model/tokenizer revision
\texttt{29efdbab55a1}, and PRA revision \texttt{be87bb88}.  The clean plain and
PRA-100 runs both resolve 1/1 in six actions and have identical ordered action
and 1,165-byte patch digests.  PRA-100 reports 1.0 realized retention on all
six requests, zero selected-history re-encoding, zero selected-history K/V
copy, and zero H2D transfer.  This result required two engine-neutral
correctness repairs: lifecycle fixtures now split at completed record
boundaries, and a union of adjacent record intervals that covers the complete
source is treated as full retention rather than being sent through disjoint
attention.

The earlier direct-MLX PRA-90 numerical negative is superseded by an engine
correctness repair, not a relaxed threshold.  A forced 100\%, single-contiguous-
interval diagnostic still differed from native attention by 0.015625, ruling
out selection, interval order, sparse RoPE positions, and reference-subset
construction.  The remaining difference was reduction geometry: Qwen2.5's
native MLX path scales queries before the dot product and uses two-pass vector
attention with fast exponentiation, block reduction, and half-precision
partials.  The corrected interval-addressed Metal kernel reproduces that
native two-pass arithmetic without packing selected history.  In the frozen
autonomous run, the two sparse requests both have maximum same-subset logit
delta 0.0 under the unchanged predeclared 0.005 gate.

The admitted PRA-90 run resolves 1/1 in six calls and submits the identical
patch.  Its first response-text divergence is call six and changes only
non-operative reasoning wording; all six shell commands remain equal to plain.
The causal floor retains 100\% of engine K/V on calls one--four, 91.54\% on call
five, and 92.57\% on call six.  Because four short-history requests round up to
full retention, aggregate visible-materialization retention is 97.81\%
(8,725/8,920 estimated tokens), so this task avoids only 195 estimated prompt
tokens.  Across all six requests, selected-history re-encoding, selected-K/V
copy, and H2D transfer remain zero.

These runs qualify semantics, not production economics.  PRA-100 reports
5,556,928,512 total K/V-copy bytes, including 92,798,976 bytes of canonical
suffix graft, and 3,337,912,320 cumulative consumer-temporary bytes with a
651,476,992-byte peak.  PRA-90 reports 8,403,419,136 total K/V-copy bytes,
including 92,012,544 suffix-graft bytes and the packed dense qualification
reference on its two sparse calls; consumer temporaries total 4,461,199,360
bytes and peak at 1,248,509,952 bytes.  Observed model/task wall times are
170.10~s for plain, 19.65~s for PRA-100, and 22.03~s for PRA-90, but one
non-isolated task is provenance rather than a speedup estimate.  The result
therefore keeps logical retention, selected-history copy, canonical growth,
qualification-reference copy, consumer temporaries, and wall time as distinct
fields.

A fresh post-fix replication at source revision \texttt{02e53d01} uses MLX
0.32.2 and MLX-LM 0.31.3 on the same 48~GB host.  It again resolves plain,
PRA-100, and task-aware PRA-90 in six calls, reproduces the reference patch,
and records zero selected-history re-encoding and zero selected-K/V copy.
PRA-100 remains action-trajectory exact.  The task-aware arm first differs
only in the sixth response wording and retains the same 8,725/8,920 physical-
to-logical input count.  Current-build PRA-100 consumer temporaries total
3,917,021,184 bytes and peak at 955,351,040 bytes; its total K/V-copy count is
5,556,928,512 bytes.  Task-aware PRA-90 retains the prior 4,461,199,360-byte
temporary total, 1,248,509,952-byte peak, and 8,403,419,136-byte total K/V-copy
count.  The matched causal-tail control is also reproduced as a valid policy
negative: 0/1 after nine calls, 10.43\% own-trajectory input saving, zero
selected-history re-encoding/copy, and a destructive submitted patch.  The
immutable receipts are under
\path{coding_agents/engine_agent_gates/mlx_task01_qwen25coder14b_postfix_02e53d01/}.

HF now has the corresponding direct, no-gateway agent bridge in code.  It keeps
a persistent canonical \texttt{DynamicCache}, treats PRA-100 as the dense
live-prefix no-op, consumes PRA-90 through original-position intervals, and
grafts only newly generated assistant K/V back into the canonical owner.  The
telemetry separates zero selected-history re-encoding/copy from tail packing,
cache-reallocation copy, suffix-graft copy, total consumer temporary bytes, and
model calls.  Focused executor/lifecycle tests pass, including a real tiny-Qwen
PRA-100 then PRA-90 sequence.
The subsequent real-CUDA mechanism smoke on Qwen2.5-Coder-1.5B validates that
accounting boundary.  PRA-100 retains 153/153 source tokens with zero selected-
history re-encoding or copy, but ordinary \texttt{DynamicCache} growth and the
new suffix still move 24,514,560 bytes in total.  The valid record-rounded
PRA-90 row retains 137/151 tokens (90.73\%), again copies and re-encodes no
selected history, and reports 1,953,728 attention-temporary bytes and
9,633,792 total lifecycle-copy bytes.  An earlier manually underfilled
137/179 row is explicitly superseded; both HF and SGLang now fail closed below
$\lceil rN\rceil$ unless the request declares an arbitrary-subset mechanism
probe.  This fallback smoke is not autonomous task evidence.

On the same Qwen2.5-Coder-1.5B revision and frozen Task~02 trajectory, the
earlier incremental-cache versus cold full-history diagnostic is greedy-token
exact on all seven eight-token continuations, but its maximum bfloat16 logit
delta is 0.34375.  That comparison changes the prefill reduction shape and is
therefore a rematerialization diagnostic, not a PRA-100 numerical gate.  The
corrected CUDA gate forks the ordinary control and PRA-100 from the identical
resident \texttt{DynamicCache}; PRA-100 then passes 7/7 continuations with
zero maximum logit delta.  Every turn retains all source tokens (1,683 on turn
one through 4,382 on turn seven), returns the dense cache object by identity,
and reports zero selected-history re-encoding, K/V copy, interval packing, and
host-to-device bytes.  The two experimental cache clones total 1,198,317,568
bytes across the seven turns and are reported separately as measurement-only
state-fork overhead; they are absent from the production attachment path.
Thus exact same-state PRA-100 parity and zero-copy selection coexist without
misclassifying ordinary \texttt{DynamicCache} growth or oracle cloning as
selected-history attachment cost.

The first autonomous CUDA attempts exposed two further plain-path defects that
supersede the earlier attribution to BitsAndBytes alone.  HF forward calls were
not enclosed in \texttt{torch.inference\_mode()}, so autograd graphs survived
successive one-token \texttt{DynamicCache} concatenations: a 1.5B model grew
until PyTorch reported 19.64~GiB allocated and failed on the 8~GiB device.
After that repair, the same model remains within roughly 4--6~GiB, but it is
model/scaffold-inadmissible: on both Task~01 and the trivial one-character
Morse-map Task~13 it emits multiple actions on its first response and then
loops on the format-recovery example to the 50-call limit without a patch.

The stronger Qwen2.5-Coder-7B NF4 retry then exposes a separate prompt-logit
allocation: the model projects every one of roughly 2K prompt positions into
the full vocabulary even though the agent consumes only the last row.  The
common forward path now requests one retained logit through
\texttt{logits\_to\_keep=1} (or the model's equivalent argument) when
supported.  This optimization is independent of PRA and preserves the next-
token computation.  Bounded 256-token cache extension then removes the
remaining prompt-activation OOM while preserving absolute positions.  The
corrected plain path completes Task~13 for ten calls in 119.2~s and Task~04 for
fifteen calls in 104.6~s, but both grade 0/1 with empty patches.  Task~13
follows the PR description's \texttt{simpy} typo, attempts interactive editors
and package installation, and never reaches the mapping; Task~04 repeatedly
constructs an invalid reproduction script.  These are two plain
model/scaffold admission failures after the runtime repair, not PRA
regressions.  HF PRA-100 remains withheld until a plain-success model/task pair
is found.

A subsequent exact-revision retry moves only the model process to Windows and
keeps mini-swe-agent and the official grader in WSL, joined by a transparent
loopback relay.  This avoids the earlier WSL memory-pressure failure without
changing the model, tokenizer, native template, NF4/FP16 quantization,
temperature-zero sampling, or scaffold.  Task~05
(\texttt{pytest-dev\_\_pytest-7982}) is a valid 8,192-token plain admission
failure: it remains within the declared window (6,402 prompt tokens at maximum,
7,426 including the requested 1,024-token completion allowance), reaches the
30-call limit in 378.5~s, modifies no file, submits an empty patch, and grades
0/1.  The model repeatedly searches configuration files for
\texttt{follow\_symlinks=False}; this is model/task behavior, not a PRA result.

The one permitted follow-up, Task~01, also reaches 30 calls without a mutation
or patch and receives an observed 0/1 grade.  That row is nevertheless
quarantined rather than counted as frozen 8,192-token evidence: the pre-fix HF
endpoint advertised no context limit and silently accepted prompts through
14,797 tokens.  Call 13's prompt plus declared completion allowance is 8,190
tokens; call 14 is the first overflow, and 17 calls lie beyond the nominal
limit.  The correction adds an explicit \texttt{max\_model\_len=8192} engine
guard for both ordinary and native requests.  It checks the full rendered
logical position extent plus the declared maximum completion before allocating
a plain cache or extending canonical K/V, advertises the limit in health, and
requires the runner preflight to match it exactly.  Sparse selected width is
not substituted for logical position extent.  No HF PRA-100 request was run:
one valid plain pair failed and the second row violated its frozen cap.

This also changes the comparison with ordinary prefix caching. Prefix caching
can reuse only the common leading run and must either keep every middle turn or
evaluate a replacement suffix when selection changes. Live-record PRA keeps
the canonical full session once, adds request membership to selected existing
cells, and in the sparse smoke exposes only 26 of 531 source cells to the new
request. Thus a selection change no longer implies thousands of re-prefill
tokens. It may still change the number of K/V cells read by attention and the
amount of resident memory, so four quantities remain disjoint: logical token
retention, selected K/V attachment, newly evaluated tokens, and wall time. The
first three now have physical telemetry; no speedup is claimed until a matched
agent run shows that reduced K/V reads dominate membership and residency costs.

The same frozen agent history also shows why transcript rematerialization
cannot be assumed equivalent across engines.  The earlier reconstructed-cache
rows remain diagnostics rather than engine comparisons.  Product adapters must
preserve the live K/V state that generated the trajectory, or explicitly
qualify a strict rematerialization mode; they must not silently substitute one
for the other.

\subsubsection{Claim 3: agent retention semantics}

The first-divergence audit also rules out ordinary sampling temperature as the
proximate explanation: both task-03 and task-04 arms sent
\texttt{temperature=0}.  At the request producing the first changed executed
action, both nominal-90\% selectors omit exactly logical messages 4--5: the
assistant command that displayed the target source file and its full-source
observation.  Because the old whole-bundle packer treated 90\% as a ceiling,
the 1,151- and 1,152-token bundles exceed their remaining budgets by only 224
and 167 tokens but are dropped completely.  Realized retention collapses to
62.01\% for task 03 and 57.72\% for task 04.  The corrected floor policy keeps
the bundle and realizes 97.39\% and 96.15\%, respectively.  In task 03 the
request-6 logical prompt is byte-identical to the existing full-history prompt;
in task 04 response wording has already diverged at request 5 although the
executed command is unchanged.

A frozen restore-only counterfactual on the same 48~GiB M4 Pro now makes this
localization causal at the next-action level.  For each task it compares the
recorded sparse request-6 prompt, that prompt plus only messages 4--5, and its
matched full logical request, with three temperature-zero repeats.  On task 03,
the sparse condition chooses a whole-file read in 0/3 matches, whereas restoring
only the source-view action--observation bundle recovers the recorded 100\%
targeted \texttt{grep} command in 3/3.  On task 04, the sparse condition again
chooses a whole-file read; restoration changes it to a target-specific
\texttt{grep} in 3/3, but does not recover the separate 100\% trajectory's
immediate \texttt{sed} edit.  This is strict causal recovery for task 03 and
directional recovery for task 04: under task 04's frozen 90\%-trajectory
request-5 wording, source evidence is important but insufficient.  Commands
are deterministic in every cell, although task-03 reasoning wording changes
once without changing its command.  These K/V counters describe ordinary
contiguous llama.cpp prefix-cache reuse during repeated prompt controls, not
sparse PRA attachment.  A target-bearing 256-word child span exists in each
large observation, motivating both a \texttt{recent\_source\_turns} floor and
hierarchical within-record selection rather than either dropping or retaining
the entire source view.

\subsubsection{Claim 2: engine portability contract}

The cross-engine expansion follows the same qualification ladder already
required of llama.cpp.  Its first rung requires that ordinary and PRA
continuations fork from the same engine-resident K/V, 100\% selection is a
semantic no-op, selected history is never tokenized or prefilled again, source
positions and stable causal-record identities survive, and request membership
does not mutate source ownership.  HF and vLLM-Metal now clear request-owned
lifecycle controls; the bounded vLLM CUDA scheduler alias clears them in its
declared scope.  The newest MLX and SGLang disjoint-consumption candidates also
clear their declared numerical, allocation, and lifecycle gates through the
shared interval-addressed Metal consumer described below.
Earlier reconstructed-cache rows compare incremental execution with an
independently rebuilt cache and consequently remain rematerialization
diagnostics, not this qualification.

The corrected same-state probes pass for HF and MLX.
On the frozen task-02 agent transcript, Transformers 5.17/PyTorch 2.14 with
Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct FP16 on MPS and MLX-LM 0.31.3 with Qwen3-0.6B 4-bit each
match the ordinary continuation on all 10 tested turns with zero maximum logit
delta.  Both paths select the complete resident cache as a view, report zero
selected-history re-encoding, and copy no K/V.  A subsequent requested-90\%
sparse-position probe supplies stronger but deliberately separate evidence.
Across seven eligible turns per engine, HF and MLX both match an independent
reference consuming the identical selected K/V and original source positions:
7/7 exact token sequences and zero maximum logit delta, with no selected-text
re-encoding.  A corrected causal-floor rerun at the same repository revision
also passes 7/7 for each engine.  Its selected/source ratios range from
90.32\% to 95.66\% (mean 92.88\%), so the nominal 90\% setting is now a floor
rather than the 67.1--94.4\% ceiling behavior of the superseded planner.  The
packed reference paths clear semantic sparse-position correctness and
cross-engine logical-selection agreement; selection, consumption, and
lifecycle are evaluated separately below rather than inheriting qualification
from those results.

The corrected vLLM-Metal bridge now runs matched release packages: vLLM
0.29.0+cpu and vLLM-Metal 0.29.0, with native shared-library and Metal-library
hashes captured from the active environment and source revision
\texttt{73908058}.  Full retention matches 10/10 turns, and the seven eligible
requested-90\% turns match an identical selected-page reference.  A real
request-owned lifecycle gate on task-02 turn 4 retains 2,944/3,104 page tokens
(94.85\%) with holes and original position extent 3,104.  All 24 checks pass:
two requests alias the exact source block IDs, active memory does not increase,
request attachment records zero copy events, selected history is not
re-encoded, finish/cancel/error release is exact-once, stale generations are
rejected, and termination and tombstones are enforced.  Lossless offload and
restore move 712,002,099 bytes and preserve the source fingerprint and output;
that residency transfer is reported separately from zero-copy request
attachment.

The first vLLM 0.28 CUDA worker-side prototype was useful but copied
804,651,008 bytes across the seven-turn frozen probe.  Source audit localized
the problem: an external connector match returns only a token count, after
which \texttt{KVCacheManager} ordinarily allocates a fresh request table.  The
replacement integration therefore runs one boundary earlier, at
\texttt{Scheduler.\_get\_local\_prefix\_cache\_hit} before
\texttt{allocate\_slots}, and installs the source request's existing
\texttt{KVCacheBlock} objects in the authoritative scheduler table.  On an RTX
5060 with vLLM 0.28 and Qwen2.5-1.5B, the complete seven eligible task-02
turns (4--10) retain 90.48--96.03\% of complete pages.  Each turn launches two
simultaneous same-subset borrowers; all 14 outputs are exact, with seven source
pins, 14 alias installations, 14 releases, and zero K/V-copy, host-to-device,
or selected-history-re-encoding bytes or tokens.  A separate three-page
mechanism smoke exercises concurrent completion, real cancellation, injected
scheduler error, stale-generation rejection, borrowed and deferred eviction,
and exact-once release: five aliases are installed and five are released, with
final page reference counts zero.  This is a bounded zero-copy result, not a
general vLLM claim: it requires in-process V1, complete pages, and one
homogeneous K/V group.  Partial pages, hybrid groups, multiprocess
scheduler/worker execution, autonomous agent execution, and runtime economics
remain unqualified.

SGLang-MLX likewise keeps selected-history re-encoding at zero while preserving
Radix/source-owner isolation, borrower-safe removal, eviction and offload,
exact-once terminal release, stale-generation rejection, restoration, and
tombstones.  Its replacement consumer is a fused interval-addressed Metal
kernel.  Rather than passing six selected slices as independent buffers, the
kernel receives the canonical source K/V once plus a compact interval table,
keeps original source positions in the cache contract, and performs one online
softmax while reading only admitted ranges.  Across all seven eligible task-02
turns, realized retention spans 90.32--95.66\%; selection pack bytes and
allocator delta remain zero.  The turn-4 first-layer peak delta falls from
12,199,774 to 19,371 bytes, only 0.163\% of the 11,907,072-byte selected layer,
and the candidate is final-logit exact against a packed-value oracle preserving
the same segment boundaries and using the identical consumer.  All seven turns
are token and logit exact under the matched consumer, re-encode no selected
history, and avoid a selected-K/V-sized allocation.  All combined consumption
and lifecycle checks now pass.  This qualification remains bounded:
SGLang's multi-request path evaluates each selected row separately and rejects a
batch that mixes ordinary and disjoint cache protocols.

The shared lifecycle registry has been exercised over real task-02 selected
cache objects for HF, MLX, and SGLang.  Two borrowers share one canonical
object; eviction is rejected while borrowed; cancelling one borrower preserves
the other; idle objects offload; lossless restore reproduces their fingerprints;
active termination cleans up; and tombstones prevent stale recreation.  These
ownership results remain independent of consumer numerical and allocation
gates even though all three declared scopes now pass them.

HF wires ownership into its synchronous model request and passes concurrent
borrowing, exact-once finish/cancel/error release, stale-generation,
offload/restore, termination, and tombstone controls.  On Qwen2.5-1.5B FP16 on
the RTX host, all seven eligible task-02 turns preserve original positions,
re-encode no selected history, and use zero persistent interval-pack or K/V-copy
bytes.  The replacement Triton kernel performs one fused online softmax over
canonical source intervals and the request-local tail.  It executes 196 layer
calls across the seven one-token continuations.  Against a packed-value oracle
with the identical causal segments, positions, and consumer, every turn is token
exact and maximum logit delta is 0.0, clearing the predeclared 0.001 gate.  The
one-token gate has zero request-tail copy; cumulative logical attention scratch
is reported separately as 19,292,784 bytes.  This qualifies the declared
batch-one Qwen/CUDA geometry, not a general latency advantage.

MLX-LM uses the same Metal consumer directly on Qwen3-0.6B 4-bit.  Across all
seven eligible task-02 turns, realized retention spans 90.32--95.66\%, with
zero selected-history re-encoding, zero selection pack bytes, and zero allocator
delta.  The kernel addresses those intervals in the original canonical arrays;
the maximum observed first-layer peak delta is 24,043 bytes, while the maximum
per-turn ratio is 0.163\% of
the selected layer, versus a selected-K/V-sized transient in the superseded
consumer.  Every packed-value matched-consumer oracle is token and final-logit
exact (maximum 0.0 versus the 0.005 gate), and ownership, exact-once release,
stale-generation, offload/restore, termination, and tombstone checks all pass.
Lossless offload separately serializes 706,726,451 bytes.  The earlier 1.0
cross-consumer logit delta remains a useful warning: changing from MLX's dense
SDPA to a different reduction while also changing storage did not isolate
selection correctness.

This correction adds a fifth entry to the superseded-evidence ledger.  A
same-subset comparison must hold the consumer fixed.  The older HF, MLX, and
SGLang rows simultaneously changed storage realization and attention reduction;
their logit deltas therefore diagnose cross-kernel rounding, while their measured
K/V copies and transients remain valid implementation defects.  The replacement
oracles are intentionally allowed to pack values, but preserve the candidate's
logical segment boundaries and original positions and execute the same consumer.

The agent SDK now carries a typed, request-scoped retention contract under
\path{pra.metadata.retention_policy}.  It controls the minimum number of
recent complete turns, the minimum newest records retained inside dense turns,
separate source/progress/mutation/verification recency floors, the oversized-record
chunk target, the dense-turn threshold, causal action--observation pairing, and
causal-bundle round-up.  Short records remain intact; large tool observations
split at natural file, test, stack, diff-hunk, or line boundaries; child spans
retain stable parent-record and causal-group identities.  This makes target and
realized retention distinct protocol fields rather than undocumented proxy
behavior.  The new \texttt{recent\_progress\_turns} control conservatively pins
explicit diagnoses, hypotheses, root causes, and proposed fixes together with
their causal observations; generic verbose reasoning is not promoted.  A
frozen task-04 regression verifies that its exact request-5 progress state and
latest source evidence both survive the 90\% policy.

\subsubsection{Claim 4: locked next-stage matrix}

Paper~8.5's explicit-episode R4/M2/V2/P1 result remains ineligible as a generic
native-transfer default because it depends on typed issue scope.  Its 6/6
paired result and descriptive 41.11\% mean saving are a mechanism upper bound.
The boundary-free atomic-policy E2 replacement fails its Easy-14 gate, and the
exact-prefix E3 candidate reaches only 16.62\% paired saving with one extra
call.  The newer strict M2/P1 cohort is the first candidate admitted to the
native-transfer stage: Tasks~3--5 preserve 3/3 official resolutions, realize
40.53\% candidate-own logical saving, and use 22 versus 30 calls.  Paper~4.5
imports the 22 hash-bound request plans, not the selection implementation.  A
native run must prove zero selected-history re-encoding/copy while preserving
each exact frozen plan and reporting official resolution.  The three-task
result remains too small and unstable to define a production default.

The completed baseline-conditional Paper~8.5 campaign frozen at commit
\texttt{b5e1969f} contributes a stronger physical stress fixture without
changing that claim boundary.  Its eighth identity,
\texttt{django\_\_django-15277}, follows seven completed issues in one
ordinary-role session; exact-prefix \textsc{Full} and frozen M2/P1 both resolve
in six calls, while logical message-input accounting falls from 236,111 to
89,534 tokens.  Paper~4.5 imports all six hash-validated requests at commit
\texttt{e4477166}.  Under the exact Qwen3-Coder tokenizer, the frozen plans
retain 36.46--38.22\% of request history.  This is a pre-execution geometry
result, not yet a native-K/V saving claim: the engine row remains open until it
reports zero history re-encoding, copy and temporary bytes, original-position
handling, and lifecycle qualification on these exact plans.
This fixture is not the later canonical-order unconditional-reliability
campaign, whose eighth \textsc{Full} control is unresolved; campaign order and
trajectory digests differ, so their outcomes are not pooled.

The first direct HF execution of that fixture now closes the request-level
mechanism gate, but not the task or fused-performance gate.  On
Qwen2.5-0.5B FP16 and a 4~GB GTX~950M, request~1 contains 37,811 resident
source tokens plus a 1,951-token local suffix.  Frozen M2/P1 selects 12,545
source K/V entries, realizing 36.4569\% total-history retention.  Its 24
logical record intervals coalesce to seven physical source spans.  The bounded
streaming-segmented fallback preserves original positions, re-encodes zero
selected-history tokens, and reports zero physical K/V copy, interval packing,
transient K/V copy, and request-tail copy.  Same-subset and restored logits are
exact (maximum delta 0.0), and all ownership/lifecycle checks pass.  The
consumer executes 384 streaming layer calls and reports 2,029,289,472
cumulative logical attention-temporary bytes; that quantity is neither K/V
copy nor a peak-allocation claim.  Because compute capability~5.0 cannot run
the Triton kernel, this row qualifies the explicit reduced-history fallback
only.  The earlier matched frozen-\textsc{Full} request is now superseded as a
dispatch diagnostic.  It unnecessarily sent one complete contiguous prefix
through the streaming sparse consumer and therefore changed the attention
reduction while reporting a 0.0869140625 delta against native HF.  Correct
PRA-100 execution instead shallow-forks mutable cache descriptors, aliases the
canonical K/V tensors, and continues through native dense attention.  The
2,713-token Qwen2.5-0.5B control clears that corrected path at maximum delta
0.0 against two independent ordinary-prefix executions and after restore,
with zero selected-history re-encoding or K/V copy.  The full 37,811-token
fixture, remaining five reduced requests, autonomous task outcome, and runtime
economics remain separately gated.

A fused RTX replication exercises the valid reduced-history path but no longer
defines PRA-100 semantics.  On Qwen3-0.6B FP16 and an RTX 5060, M2/P1 selects
12,545/37,811 history tokens as 24 logical intervals and seven physical spans.
The Triton consumer matches its identical-consumer subset oracle exactly,
restores exactly, re-encodes zero history tokens, and packs or copies zero
selected K/V bytes while passing every sparse lifecycle check.  It separately
reports 3,595,621,120 cumulative logical attention-scratch bytes and
3,370,106,880 cumulative request-tail append-copy bytes; neither is presented
as zero-cost reuse.  The old matched \textsc{Full} arm's 0.15234375
Triton-versus-native delta remains a useful cross-consumer numerical result,
but forcing full retention through Triton is no longer an admissible runtime
dispatch.  It neither disqualifies the sparse M2/P1 mechanism nor substitutes
for a native-dense PRA-100 control.

The same frozen M2/P1 request also passes the direct fused-MLX path on
Qwen3-0.6B 4-bit and the 48~GB M4 Pro.  Twenty-four logical record intervals
coalesce to seven original-position physical spans addressing the same 12,545
of 37,811 source K/V entries with zero selected-history re-encoding, zero
selection-pack bytes, and no physical K/V copy.  Selection changes active
allocator memory by 0 bytes.  First-layer attention peaks only 290,867 bytes
above baseline, 0.5661\% of that layer's 51,384,320 selected-K/V
bytes, so it does not allocate a selected-K/V-sized transient.  Tokens and
logits match the packed-value identical-consumer oracle exactly (maximum delta
0.0), including after lossless offload/restore, and every ownership/lifecycle
check passes.  The 4,336,489,740-byte offload payload is reported separately
from selection and attention temporary memory.  This closes one request-level
fused mechanism point, not the matched frozen-\textsc{Full}, autonomous-task,
or latency gates.

The corrected SGLang-MLX bridge independently clears the request/radix
lifecycle gate on the same Metal model, host, and frozen M2/P1 plan.  It
preserves the 24 logical records while coalescing them to seven
original-position physical spans, re-encodes zero history tokens, and reports
zero selection packing and physical K/V copy.  Same-subset generation and
post-restore state are exact, all lifecycle checks pass, and selection changes
active memory by 0 bytes.  Its first-layer peak delta is 290,867 bytes
(0.5661\% of selected-layer K/V) with no selected-K/V-sized transient.  This
is a distinct SGLang request and radix-lifecycle qualification, but it shares
the Qwen3-0.6B model family and M4 Pro with direct MLX and therefore is not
cross-model replication.  The 799.20-second complete runner time includes
source capture, reference work, offload/restore, and lifecycle probes; it is
not kernel latency.  Its same-revision matched \textsc{Full} control retains
all 37,811 source tokens, coalesces 192 logical intervals to one physical span,
and also passes exactness, zero-copy/re-encoding, allocation, and lifecycle
gates.  First-layer peak deltas are 290,867 bytes for M2/P1 and 323,587 bytes
for \textsc{Full}.  Complete lifecycle-runner time is 799.20 versus 1,327.73
seconds, a descriptive 1.66$\times$ ratio or 39.81\% reduction.  This single
sequential pair is neither a replicated estimate nor kernel latency.

The bounded vLLM 0.28 CUDA page-alias path clears the same receipt-free request
on Qwen3-0.6B FP16 and an RTX 5060.  Its 16-token cache pages round 12,545
logical selected-history tokens to 12,656 resident tokens, a 111-token
overhead.  With the 1,938-token wire suffix, total visible work falls from
39,762 to 14,594 tokens (63.30\% saving), while resident-original-K/V omission
is 66.56\%.  Two continuations both emit token IDs [151667, 198]; two
authoritative aliases are installed and released, with zero selected-history
re-encoding, physical K/V copy, host-to-device transfer, receipt encoding,
host packing, or copy-on-write.  Commit \texttt{d4e1568a} fixes a harness defect
that previously required a compact-receipt source even when the frozen policy
contained no receipts: the corrected path now aliases selected canonical pages
directly.  A same-revision matched \textsc{Full} control at commit
\texttt{2f168e0e} aliases all 37,824 resident page tokens, emits the same
[151667, 198] continuation twice, and retains zero copy/re-encoding and clean
release.  Mean post-alias two-token generation takes 0.296 seconds for M2/P1
and 0.694 seconds for \textsc{Full}, a descriptive 2.35$\times$ ratio.  The two
borrowers share one initialized run per arm after source capture; this is not a
replicated end-to-end latency, autonomous-task, or runtime-economics result.

The first matched MLX \textsc{Full} diagnostic passes all correctness and
lifecycle gates at 100\% retention, zero selected-history re-encoding/copy,
and exact logits, but it also exposes a physical-segmentation defect.  The
consumer executed 192 adjacent logical record intervals as 192 physical
segments; M2/P1 likewise executed 24 logical intervals even though its source
views coalesce to seven physical spans.  The observed 1,697.71 versus 431.28
seconds therefore confounds retained-token work with avoidable record-boundary
launch and reduction overhead and is quarantined from speedup claims.  The
engine-neutral plan and both HF and MLX consumers now coalesce adjacent K/V
views while retaining record and causal identities in the logical plan.

The post-fix pair at commit \texttt{090a7d52} closes that request-level
control.  M2/P1 reports 24 logical intervals and seven physical spans; Full
reports 192 logical intervals and one physical span.  Both arms preserve exact
tokens and logits before and after restore, allocate zero bytes during
selection, re-encode zero history tokens, copy or pack zero selected K/V bytes,
and pass every lifecycle check.  Their first-layer attention peak deltas are
290,867 and 323,587 bytes, respectively, far below their selected-layer K/V
extents.  The complete lifecycle runner takes 500.53 seconds for M2/P1 and
1,111.16 seconds for Full, a descriptive 2.22$\times$ ratio and 54.95\%
elapsed reduction.  The single sequential pair includes source capture, a
packed-value oracle, offload/restore, and all lifecycle probes; it is neither a
replicated estimate nor a kernel-latency claim.

The earlier causal-valid matched token-tail remains a control plan.  In the
current two-task, 18-decision frozen screen, this control retains 97.16\% of
tokens and reproduces 14/18 exact commands, compared with 97.22\% and 11/18
for delayed consumed-search exclusion plus two-read supersession (H1+H3);
exact response content is tied at 7/18.  The ordinary-text control can trim the
boundary tool observation, whereas the first native implementation retains
whole causal records and admits newest complete turns under the same strict
token ceiling.  We report that materialization difference explicitly rather
than presenting the two treatments as identical.

At 100\%, exact trajectory parity remains an engine implementation gate.
Below 100\%, Paper~4.5 compares engines on the same frozen logical plan;
divergence after a passed 100\% gate is a selection/consumption-quality
observation, not by itself an engine defect or evidence that one selection
policy is superior.

``Same frozen logical plan'' is literal: every engine receives the exported
record ledger rather than rerunning a selector.  The history and plan digests,
selected IDs, full and selected logical token counts, tokenizer revision,
agent, model, task, and sampling settings must agree.  Engine block rounding
is reported only as physical realization overhead.  This prevents a change in
model capability, agent trajectory, or adapter tokenization from masquerading
as an engine-dependent saving.

After engine qualification, the locked task-level matrix contains no gateway
arm: No PRA, 100\% PRA, and 90\% PRA.  PRA is crossed with an orthogonal
adaptor condition (no adaptor versus an immutable model-adaptor bundle) while
holding the selected records, retention budget, and physical materialization
policy fixed.  Exact no-adaptor parity at 100\% is an implementation gate; a
learned adaptor is an explicit model intervention and is judged by its frozen
identity and task outcome rather than assumed to be response-identical.  The
two 75\% rows (without and with the adaptor) are admitted independently only
when all three predeclared tasks pass at 90\% in their matching engine,
selector, materializer, and adaptor cell.

The executable Easy-3 card freezes the first three historical baseline-success
instances and expands them task-major into 15 cells: one No-PRA control and
four PRA cells (100\% and 90\%, each without and with a frozen adaptor) per
task.  Temperature is zero, seed is zero, top-$p$ is one, the direct llama.cpp
engine runs on the 48~GiB M4 host, and mini-swe-agent runs separately.  Receipt
preparation yields nine configuration-qualified no-adaptor cells and six
blocked adaptor cells; no task execution is counted by this preparation step.
Here ``adaptor'' has the narrow scientific meaning of a learned causal-record
routing/selection model applied before the K/V subset is frozen.  Structural
model mapping, prompt guidance, memory-consumption LoRA, and frozen replay are
not adaptor arms.  Because no certified Qwen3-Coder-30B bundle or typed
injection path exists, the six learned-adaptor cells fail closed rather than
silently falling back to the no-adaptor policy.

The first 90\% retention-window diagnostic keeps two mutation and two
verification turns fixed while varying completed-turn recency. The H10 and H5
arms reproduce the same first seven commands; H5 first removes history on
request 8 and immediately takes a different, unproductive source view. H4
first selects on request 7, and H3 on request 6. H10 is the strongest selection
candidate: it reaches the target edit and realizes 10.0\% estimated context
saving, but then repeats source inspection instead of verifying or submitting.
H5 also enters a discovery cycle, while H4 and H3 were stopped once their
boundary was localized. These intentionally terminated diagnostics are not
solve-rate rows. They show that shortening the history window does not repair
the task and can lose progress earlier; the next controlled variable is
consumption policy---post-mutation diff inspection, focused verification, and
submission after a successful check---rather than more selector training.

Exact interaction-history audit localizes the reduced-context failure more
sharply.  The legacy 100\% live-prefix-cache arm is response-for-response identical to the
successful plain trajectory.  In every legacy active-tail arm, however, the
first changed command is action 4: the selector retains the five task segments
and current action--observation tail but drops the first two completed causal
turns.  The plain agent advances to locating \texttt{Value}; the treated agent
re-inspects \texttt{CharField} and later repeats similar source views.  Thus the
early loss is a progress-memory selection bug, not a chat-template failure.
Because the reduced arms rematerialized selected text, this audit does not test
the replacement selected-live-K/V mechanism.

We repaired that policy with a v4 \emph{progress spine}.  In addition to the
pinned task and active tail, it pins the two latest completed causal turns and
the latest source-content, mutation, and verification turns, queries retrieval
with both the task and active state, and restores all records to causal
presentation order.  Source-content means the latest causal bundle whose
command actually displays file contents (for example \texttt{cat} or
\texttt{sed -n}); a later path-only \texttt{find}/\texttt{grep} discovery does
not displace that evidence.  This new count is exposed and recorded as
\texttt{recent\_source\_turns}; applying value 1 to the frozen task-03 and
task-04 request-6 payloads retains messages 4--5 in both cases.  Autonomous and
one-bundle counterfactual replays remain required before attributing recovery
to this change.
All 31 baseline request prefixes again pass the physical Qwen chat template at
75\%, 50\%, and 25\%.  On the same admitted task, v4 at 75\% resolves 1/1:
the first four commands remain exact, action 5 merely takes a narrower source
view, a failed stale-line edit is recovered, the patch is created at action 31,
and the exact submission occurs at action 33.  Estimated logical-context saving
is 22.3\%.  By contrast, v4 at 50\% resolves 0/1 after 50 actions with no
submitted patch despite 45.9\% estimated saving.  Its selected records contain
the exact earlier \texttt{CharField} path, yet the model launches redundant
whole-tree searches and later corrupts a method boundary while editing.  This
is a consumption/edit-execution failure with the required state present, so
the 25\% arm is withheld by the predeclared quality gate.

The v4 runs also expose the rematerialization boundary.  At 75\%, 135,757 selected
resource-token exposures require 57,754 newly evaluated tokens while 78,003
tokens hit exact composite-prefix deltas; no physical K/V copy is observed.
These are not selected-live-K/V reuse metrics.
Nevertheless, the run takes 608.0 seconds under severe host memory pressure,
so it is not admitted as a latency improvement over the earlier plain run.
The runtime now writes a paired JSONL history for every call containing the
exact logical payload, transformed physical payload, selected-resource digest,
and model response.  This makes future plain, G00, direct-native, and G11
deviations attributable at the first differing interaction rather than only at
the final SWE-bench score.

That trace then exposed two G11 integration faults before they could be counted
as task failures.  First, the llama.cpp wrapper advertised resource-delta
support but rendered only the newly supplied ADD/UPDATE bodies; on request 3
the native prefix therefore collapsed from the complete 2,576-token trajectory
to the 565-token new turn and the response diverged.  The wrapper now retains a
tenant--session--model resource inventory, reconstructs every ordered active
record before chat rendering, and fails closed when an unchanged body is
missing.  Second, G11 treated the intentional movement of completed chat turns
from inline messages into detached records as a destructive history rewrite.
It closed the live engine session, preserving outputs but losing the 2,293,
2,576, and 2,684 cached tokens observed on requests 2--4.  The selected-history
projection is now explicit; G11 retains that session while the native adapter
still compares exact token IDs and backtracks safely on a genuine rewrite.

The corrected frozen G11 replay is exact against the direct composite-resource path on all 33
requests: logical-request digests, ordered selected records, response text,
prefix-hit state, cached-token counts, native-token counts, wire-token counts,
and the no-K/V-copy flag all match.  Aggregate engine accounting is consequently
identical: 135,757 selected-resource token exposures, 57,754 evaluated resource
tokens, 78,003 resource-prefix hits, 7,553 sequential-prefix hits, 143,310
native tokens, and 8,950 wire tokens.  It emits the same 689-byte patch and the
official grader resolves the task, for direct/G11 transport parity at 1/1 and
22.3\% estimated logical-context saving.  The grader initially attempted to
repull the x86-only evaluator image as ARM after agent cleanup and failed before
patch application; regrading the unchanged prediction under the declared
\texttt{linux/amd64} platform resolved it.  The runner now propagates that
platform contract to both agent and grader.  Because the G11 run took 1,189.7
seconds on the already swap-saturated host versus 608.0 seconds for direct, no
gateway latency ratio or native selected-K/V claim is admitted from this pair.

We therefore preregistered a stronger controlled SWE-bench campaign from an
independent local-model study~\cite{av2026localcoding}. The checked-in benchmark
card preserves its ordered seed-0 cohort of 50 SWE-bench Verified instances at
source revision \texttt{8f894c2}; a canonical LF-joined digest
\texttt{20acb5f7...1818131} prevents accidental resampling. The source used
mini-swe-agent 2.4.0 with its unmodified text/backticks scaffold, SWE-bench
4.1.0 Docker grading, vLLM 0.22.1, 16,384 model tokens, 40 steps, temperature
zero, BF16 weights, and FP8 K/V on one H100 80~GB. Its reported controls are
7/50 for Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B-Instruct and 19/50 for Gemma-4-31B-it. These are
external reproduction targets, not PRA results.

The executable contract keeps both reference cells and all treatments disabled
until a qualifying host is recorded. The currently reachable local CUDA device
has 4~GB, and the known 48--64~GB Apple hosts cannot reproduce
vLLM/H100/BF16. Moreover, the source does not publish immutable
model/tokenizer revisions, package hashes, or grader-image digests. The runner
records these as source-provenance limitations, separately from actual local
configuration deviations. Current Qwen and Gemma HF revisions are pinned for
the new run, as are installed package-manifest digests. The campaign can test
reproduction of every reported setting and score, but it cannot claim binary
identity with the historical artifacts. Any actual configuration deviation
still leaves the cell partial and locked. After an admitted Gemma result of at
least 20\%, the scheduler orders gateway pass-through, matched
truncation, and PRA at 50\%, 25\%, and 12.5\% physical-context budgets. The
first ten ordered IDs separately define a BF16/Q8/Q6/Q5/Q4 precision
diagnostic; its result cannot be reported as fixed-50 reproduction evidence.
The treatment path is nevertheless executable rather than a placeholder. A
small OpenAI-compatible proxy leaves B1 payloads unchanged. For matched
truncation and B2 it keeps system messages and the current user observation
mandatory, then allocates the same residual budget to prior agent-visible
history. Truncation keeps the recent suffix; B2 divides earlier messages into
bounded records, ranks them with typed, BM25, and deterministic embedding
channels under reciprocal-rank fusion, and sends the selected records through
the gateway's G10 text-realization path. A recency fallback fills only unused
budget. Request receipts separate logical, selected, physically visible, and
avoided whitespace-token estimates from engine-reported tokenizer counts and
timings. The normalizer also reads mini-swe-agent's agent-visible trajectory
files: it records exact cumulative prompt and completion tokens, maximum prompt
tokens, a conservative repeated-context estimate, model and tool calls,
trajectory duration, termination, and patch size. Stable task hashes join these
rows to request traces without benchmark labels. TTFT and prefill remain null
when the endpoint does not expose them. This implementation readiness is not
treatment evidence while the baseline gate is closed.

\subsection{Distributed observability and semantic context telemetry}

The product runtime now carries one W3C trace context across the agent,
gateway, PRA realization path, and inference adapter.  This is not a new
coupling between those services: each remains a distinct OpenTelemetry service
(``\texttt{pra-agent}'', ``\texttt{pra-gateway}'', and
``\texttt{pra-runtime}''), and an engine-native provider contributes child
spans only when its pinned integration explicitly enables them.  Engines with
Prometheus but no native tracing are scraped directly; engines exposing only
timing APIs receive wrapper spans and normalized metrics.  Thus common PRA
telemetry does not pretend that every engine exports the same physical facts.

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\fbox{\begin{minipage}{0.92\linewidth}
\centering
\textbf{Request path}\par\smallskip
Agent $\longrightarrow$ Gateway $\longrightarrow$ PRA routing / realization
$\longrightarrow$ Engine\par
\smallskip
$\downarrow$ \hfill $\downarrow$ \hfill $\downarrow$ \hfill $\downarrow$\par
OpenTelemetry spans $\longrightarrow$ OTel Collector
$\longrightarrow$ trace backend\par
Prometheus counters / histograms $\longrightarrow$ Prometheus
$\longrightarrow$ Grafana\par
\smallskip
Engine-native scheduler and cache metrics are scraped beside, rather than
renamed as, PRA semantic context metrics.
\end{minipage}}
\caption{The observability boundary.  W3C context joins request causality,
while Prometheus and Grafana correlate semantic context decisions with
engine-native serving behavior.}
\label{fig:observability-architecture}
\end{figure}

A representative trace is rooted at \texttt{pra.agent.turn}; its
\texttt{pra.gateway.request} child contains session resolution and protocol
translation, followed by \texttt{pra.context.route}, selection and realization,
then \texttt{pra.engine.request}.  The attributes separate source and selected
tokens, already-visible and newly materialized records, prefix reuse status,
native attached bytes, storage tier activity, and queue/prefill/decode timing.
This decomposition answers whether a high TTFT arose in routing, WARM
promotion, native attach, engine queueing, or ordinary model prefill.
